# Development plan

This plan is the single authoritative home for implementation status. Architecture and specifications describe accepted behavior; they do not prove implementation.

**The #39 freeze is about scoring, and no winner exists.** [#39](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/39) has no frozen v0 protocol, no independent blind custody and no accepted destination/task rubric. That freeze gates **scored** comparison and held-out claims only; isolated matched **unscored** public-development baseline probes do not require it. No local winner is established, so [#41](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/41)'s winner-dependent incremental claim stays **deferred for lack of that evidence**. That records an evidence gap; it is not a scored comparison and not an adoption choice.

**What is merged still authenticates nobody.** The sentinel has no adapter at a real send point; the Presidio runner is a bounded offline development harness whose real 51-wheel execution path has still never been independently re-run, so its 51/51 screen, latencies and per-case counts remain **author-attributed** measurements on one host; the governance module is an offline foundation. What is merged is **non-enforcing**: not a deployed broker, vault, gateway or managed-network enforcement, and no protected-egress, tenant-isolation, held-out or production claim follows from it.

**Resource limits are per process, and limits are not causes.** The research helpers' bounds are **per process** (and `RLIMIT_NPROC` per real UID), so no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound exists without privileges, and the sentinel's own budgets do not bound process memory at all. None of that is evidence of permanent buffer retention, and none of it establishes a cause for the earlier full-run failures whose failing group, assertion and output were never captured: those stay **unattributed and unreproduced**, with no cause inferred and none excluded.

The live issue was reframed on 2026-10-02: the deliverables are a **minimal evaluation runner plus a concrete reuse recommendation**, not a prescribed worker architecture, and a persistent service or product deployment profile is deliberately **not** built. The bounded child-process runner already delivered is retained, unchanged in intent, as the isolated experimental runner that produced the evidence the recommendation rests on; nothing intentional was deleted to fit the new scope. Stage 1 is the runner, its pinned manifest and its conformance fixtures; Stage 2 is [the reuse recommendation](research/issue-113-presidio-reuse-recommendation-2026-10-02.md). The feature was written against accepted main `e6f1a46` and then merged with actual `origin/main` `6e4bc65` in commit `e066759`; the two real conflicts (`.gitignore`, one `src/normalized-detection.ts` import line) were resolved by keeping both sides, and the actual combined tree passes build, typecheck, the fixture guard and `npm test` **755/755** with 0 skipped. Main's #10 `detectConfigured`/`CONFIGURED` wiring, the per-occurrence `valueSpan` identity key and the cooperative parser deadlines (`isParseHost`) are all present and unchanged; the only change to main's side of that file is this feature's reviewed `locate` to `placeSpan` refactor plus exporting `Placed`. An independent review of the pre-merge head `b7ea120` returned REQUEST_CHANGES with four blocking findings; all four are fixed on this head with behavioral regressions (see the review-fix paragraph below). A narrow, **NON-ENFORCING, synthetic-only, unscored** Presidio candidate source now exists as [`src/presidio-candidate-source.ts`](../src/presidio-candidate-source.ts) (pure protocol, span and mapping translation) with the bounded child-process transport in [`src/presidio-worker-process.ts`](../src/presidio-worker-process.ts), a generated stdlib-only fake worker at [`test/fixtures/presidio-fake-worker/fake_worker.py`](../test/fixtures/presidio-fake-worker/fake_worker.py), the #5 evaluation bridge [`evaluations/presidio-development-trial.mjs`](../evaluations/presidio-development-trial.mjs), the pinned real configuration in [`evaluations/presidio-worker/`](../evaluations/presidio-worker/README.md) and one strictly additive export, `placeExternalSpan`, in the #6 seam so this adapter reuses #6/#7 provenance rather than re-deriving it. Classification v1 is unchanged, no new `subtype` was added, decision 010 and the taxonomy/information-model drafts remain **proposed and unwired**, and #65/#66 are not adopted.

The adapter's own contract: the caller never supplies the analysed text, the adapter re-derives it from the #6 result and the named target (whole view, #6 compatibility fold, or a #7 field value from a parse it repeats), digests it, and requires the worker to echo protocol, request, input reference, tenant, project, representation and digest — any difference is `REPLY_BINDING_MISMATCH` with no candidate. The plan holds a **frozen copy** of that target, so mutating the caller's own object after preparation cannot move where the worker's offsets are credited. The reply's analyzer version and language are **required pins** in the trusted scope and checked against the reply; only the pinned value ever reaches a provenance record or a shared report. Candidate evidence ids are derived from the caller's scope refs and per-request `inputRef` only, never from the analysed text, so an id correlates neither across tenants nor across requests and confirms no guessed value offline. Offsets are Python end-exclusive **code points** converted to UTF-16 through a precomputed map, and text containing an unpaired surrogate is refused before the request exists, because `TextEncoder` would replace it with U+FFFD while `len()` still counts one code point. Fractional, non-integer, reversed, out-of-range, unmapped-entity and out-of-score-range results are named and dropped; an over-long result array is a bounded failure, never a truncation; a worker `FAILURE` is `WORKER_REPORTED_FAILURE`; a run with no NER-capable engine is `PARTIAL` with `NO_NER`/`NO_TEXT_CONTEXT`, so a no-finding reply is never a clean success; and because a `FIELD` placement repeats the #7 parse, at most 16 results may be placed against one field per analysis, past which the run reports `REPLY_RESULT_LIMIT` rather than letting an untrusted result count multiply the parent's work. Placement is always #6's own, so a folded spelling, an escaped/percent/entity-decoded #7 value or a decoded view claims a covering span or the encoded envelope and never an invented original offset, and repeated values keep one span per occurrence. Detector-native types pass through a versioned table (`presidio-entity-map/1`: `PERSON`, `EMAIL_ADDRESS`, `PHONE_NUMBER`, `IP_ADDRESS`, `URL`, `MAC_ADDRESS`); anything else, including `US_SSN`, stays explicit as `UNSUPPORTED_ENTITY_TYPE` and is never coerced onto the nearest accepted class. Every candidate carries the producer/version/input provenance #3 expects and **no** `sensitivity`, `confidence`, `reversible`, `scope` or `trust`: a Presidio score is detector evidence, so a candidate composed through `composeClassification` stays `UNRESOLVED` until trusted configuration supplies a sensitivity. Upstream filtering that this boundary cannot observe is named rather than reconstructed — `POST_FILTER_REPLY_ONLY`, `UPSTREAM_SCORE_THRESHOLD`, `UPSTREAM_DUPLICATE_SUPPRESSION`, `UPSTREAM_ALLOW_LIST`, `UPSTREAM_CONTEXT_ENHANCEMENT`, `NO_NER`, `NO_TEXT_CONTEXT`, `UNSUPPORTED_ENTITY_TYPES` — and the one supported seam that adds evidence back, `AnalysisExplanation`, contributes only what this repository can vouch for: the configured recognizer's **class name** when it is one of the six recognizers the pinned manifest enables, never a pattern name, and the `enhancedByContext` boolean (Presidio's own `EntityRecognizer.id` embeds a per-process counter). Presidio allow lists hold values, so the reply carries a **count** only and no field can hold one.

**Every string this boundary reports is a committed constant, a closed code or a trusted pin.** A reply's entity type, pattern name and recognizer id are untrusted content: the identifier grammar the reply schema applies is a *shape* check, and a planted value that happens to satisfy it is still a planted value, while a worker repeating one string in two fields is one worker agreeing with itself rather than corroboration. Type names are therefore reported by name only when they are members of the committed vocabulary `PRESIDIO_DECLARED_UNSUPPORTED_ENTITY_TYPES` (which this repository does **not** claim is the upstream catalogue's complete list), recognizer ids only when they are in `PRESIDIO_PINNED_RECOGNIZER_IDS`, and a pattern name never at all; everything else becomes a count, a boolean or a closed code (`unsupported[].entityType: null`, `unpinnedUnsupportedTypes`, `recognizerIdUnpinned`, `patternNameReported`, `declaredUnsupportedTypes`). Both vocabularies are pinned by version in `provenance.labelVocabularyVersion` (`presidio-label-vocabulary/1`), adding a row is a reviewable edit, and a dropped label is never promoted to a candidate. The regression that holds this plants a grammar-legal token in every untrusted string slot a reply offers, walks **every string a returned result can contain** and allows only a committed constant, a closed code, a trusted pin or the three shaped digests/ids — so any future reportable field must be justified to pass it. The shared trial record additionally omits `provenance.textDigest`, which is the trusted binding digest of the analysed text rather than a reportable field.

Worker bytes are untrusted protected traffic. The transport spawns one process per analysis, hands it a fixed minimal environment (nothing inherited, `PYTHONNOUSERSITE`, no bytecode writes), bounds startup and execution separately, caps stdout and stderr bytes and the reply line, **counts and discards stderr** so no Python traceback, path, value or exception text can reach a result, maps spawn failure and every timeout to a fixed code, and SIGKILLs and reaps on each failure path. The environment is a fixed minimum that the transport declares, but that is not the same as a guarantee: Node's own coverage instrumentation rewrites `child_process.spawn` and adds `NODE_V8_COVERAGE` to the child, which the deterministic suite found and now pins in the fake worker's own allowlist. The trial's authoritative environment boundary is the sandbox's `--clearenv` plus its explicit `--setenv` list, not the transport's `env` option. The adapter transforms no value, resolves no mapping, calls no remote recognizer and authorizes no effect; it is not wired into classification v1, policy, the #19 sentinel or any sink.

Local evidence on this branch (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, Python 3.14.4, Linux x86_64; **no push, PR, remote CI or approval; the `origin/main` merge above is a local commit**): locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0, and one `npm test` run on the **actual merged tree** passes **763/763** with no skips, of which 57 are new across four files (34 pure-adapter, (non-BMP prefix/inside-match offsets, 200 generated astral/combining/invisible conversions, fold cover spans, verbatim versus escaped #7 field placement, repeated values, decoded-view envelopes, seven binding mismatches, unpaired surrogates, framing and extra-field rejection, eight malformed-span shapes, score bounds, unmapped types, the accepted-v1 composition, producer/seam provenance, filtering provenance and limits, no-NER/worker-failure/partial replies, overflow and a 64-case hostile-reply sweep, byte input), plus 12 transport groups (real subprocess scan, environment minimum, startup and execution kill, stdout/stderr flood, crash before and after a reply, non-UTF-8 output, framing, a planted-value exception that leaks through no channel, a child that exits before reading its request, over-limit and at-limit result arrays, reply mutation and invalid commands), 7 #5-bridge groups (the D01/D02 records carry codes and counts only, a transport failure is an explicit per-field failure, an unmapped type stays explicit, a worker that claims the NER capability its pin denies is refused end to end, a planted entity-type label cannot reach the shared record, an unknown case is refused, and the documented command line runs the same bridge) and 4 stub-engine worker-boundary groups (the child-side result bound **derived from the observed OK -> FAILURE transition** rather than read out of the worker's source, both sides of that transition asserted, seven ill-typed manifests, three smuggled-request-line shapes, and the pinned manifest identity). Compiled coverage of the two new modules is 100 % lines / 95.86 % branches for `presidio-candidate-source.js` and 97.67 % / 91.25 % for `presidio-worker-process.js`; the two lines left in the transport are the outer `spawn` and `stdin.write` catch blocks, which are documented in the module header as unreachable behind the configuration validation rather than exercised. The transport and bridge groups require a real `python3` and report a **named skip** without one; a run reporting them skipped is not evidence.

**Real-stack gate, cleared before any execution.** Before installing, importing or executing anything, all 51 wheels of the resolved `presidio-analyzer==2.2.364` stack were downloaded wheel-only and screened with #46's independently reviewed host helper read from Git object `7136c9af48b484a0e43b544cbb668ac4eaf046bd` into `/tmp` (not copied into the repository): **51/51 `OK`**, every downloaded digest equal to the independently fetched PyPI record, **0 yanked**, **0 OSV advisories** for each package at its pinned version, wheel metadata read under the helper's bounds. 28 of 51 declare no PEP 639 `License-Expression` and carry only a legacy `License` field; that metadata gap is recorded in the manifest and is a gate for any future packaging or adoption, not for this disposable local trial. No wheel, sdist or model weight is committed to the repository. The chosen configuration is Presidio's own `NoOpNlpEngine` with six explicit self-contained recognizers, one language (`en`), no country set, no allow list, no context enhancer, and `tldextract` reconfigured to its bundled snapshot: **no NLP weights are downloaded or loaded and no runtime model download happens**, at the measured and stated cost that 2.2.364 has no `PERSON` recognizer outside its NLP-engine recognizers, so this configuration cannot find a person name. `tldextract.extract` had to be rebound as well as `TLD_EXTRACTOR`, because Presidio's `EmailRecognizer.validate_result` calls the module-level function, which is a closure over the default instance and fetched the suffix list on first use.

**Screened smoke/conformance result, re-run on the merged head, unscored and not a comparative win** (`presidio-development-trial.mjs` over public D01-DEV-001 and D02-DEV-001, each field analysed in a network-unshared `bwrap` sandbox with a disposable offline venv, and each field an independent process): both cases `PARTIAL` with reasons `['NO_NER_CAPABILITY']` and limitations exactly `NO_NER`, `NO_TEXT_CONTEXT`, `POST_FILTER_REPLY_ONLY`, `UPSTREAM_SCORE_THRESHOLD`, `UPSTREAM_DUPLICATE_SUPPRESSION`. This set is now reproducible from the committed manifest: the first review found that `NO_TEXT_CONTEXT` had followed the enhancer's `DISABLED` switch rather than the engine, so a no-NLP configuration understated its own coverage gap and the recorded set could not be regenerated; it now follows the engine, since without an NER-capable engine there are no tokens or lemmas for a context stage to read. The record also carries `droppedControls` (empty for both cases) and the pinned `producerVersion`/`language`. D01: 3 development controls planted, **1 matched** (the phone number), 2 missed (the person's name, and the `.invalid` email address, which `EmailRecognizer` invalidates because its TLD check requires a real public suffix), 5 events emitted, 4 of them false positives from the URL recognizer matching `persona.de`, `example.in` and similar fragments. D02: 2 controls planted, **1 matched** (the documentation-range IPv4), 1 missed (a bare host name, for which this configuration has no recognizer), 4 false positives. The US_SSN recognizer's "very weak" 0.05 patterns are dropped by Presidio's own per-entity threshold before the reply, which is exactly the evidence loss `UPSTREAM_SCORE_THRESHOLD` names. Every case reported the secret-escape and task claims as **untested** because nothing is sent, and the printed record contains only fixed codes, counts and evaluator-minted ordinals — no value, span text, fixture field or host path. These numbers are a smoke/conformance measurement of one pinned configuration over two public development cases; they are not a benchmark, not #40 evidence, not a #39 v0 lock and not #48 input.

**Independent review of the pre-merge head `b7ea120` returned REQUEST_CHANGES; all four blockers are fixed on this head with behavioral regressions.** B1 (HIGH): the plan stored the caller's live target object, so anything that mutated it between preparation and completion moved where the worker's offsets were credited while every binding check still passed; the plan now holds a frozen copy, pinned by a regression that reuses one mutable target across two fields and mutates `fieldIndex` between prepare and complete. B2 (MEDIUM): candidate evidence ids were a 64-bit prefix of SHA-256 of the analysed text, which both correlates across tenants and confirms guessed values offline; ids are now derived from the caller's scope refs and per-request `inputRef`, mixed together so a reused `inputRef` across tenants cannot collide either. B3 (MEDIUM): the recorded limitation set was not reproducible from the committed manifest, corrected as above and re-measured. B4 (MEDIUM): a worker-chosen `runtime.version`/`runtime.language` could reach the printed record; both are now required pins in the trusted scope, checked against the reply, and only the pinned value is carried, pinned with a grammar-legal planted token that the old identifier grammar would have accepted. The review's non-blocking items are also fixed: an over-cap `unsupported` report is now named `UNSUPPORTED_REPORT_LIMIT` instead of dropped silently and a worker that both declares and returns a type can no longer double-count it; the real worker emits a complete protocol frame on every failure path, so an unusable or absent manifest is now named `WORKER_REPORTED_FAILURE` with zero candidates instead of being refused as a malformed frame, and it no longer truncates results behind an unnamed cap because the adapter owns `REPLY_RESULT_LIMIT`; the three over-claiming test groups now prove what they claim (the kill test has a positive control that really writes the marker, the execution deadline is exercised by a worker that replies and then stalls, and the hostile-object test asserts the string boundary no trap is invoked through); timers are declared module-scoped under `node:timers` instead of merging a global `setTimeout` return type into the whole program; and a control that is absent or ambiguous in the projected field is reported as a dropped control instead of being filtered away with no record. The `placeExternalSpan` `FIELD` `pathRef` placeholder is **stable across requests** (`external-field-<view>-<index>`) and is deliberately not comparable with `detectNormalizedCandidates`'s request-bound digests; it never reaches a printed record.

**A second independent review of `f86386d` returned REQUEST_CHANGES; both blockers and the non-blocking items are fixed on this head.** F1 (MEDIUM): `runtime.nerAvailable` was still the worker's own claim and it alone decided COMPLETE vs PARTIAL and whether `NO_NER`/`NO_TEXT_CONTEXT` were printed, so a worker claiming a capability its pin did not have erased its own coverage limits and turned a degraded run into a clean one — the same defect class as B4, on the third `runtime` field. `expectedNerAvailable` is now a **required trusted pin**, read from `manifest.configuration.nlpEngine.nerAvailable`, checked against the reply (`NER_CAPABILITY_MISMATCH` with no candidate), and the pinned value alone drives `provenance.nerAvailable`, the `degraded` decision and every limitation; pinned by an adapter-level regression covering both directions and by an end-to-end bridge regression using the delivered fake worker. F2 (MEDIUM/LOW): the worker truncated at its own 4096-result cap and answered with an unnamed `PARTIAL`, contradicting two claims this diff had added; the cap is **gone**, so the transport's `WORKER_OUTPUT_LIMIT` and the adapter's `REPLY_RESULT_LIMIT` are the only bounds and both name themselves, and the two claims are now true. Also applied: a reply that overflows both bounds reports both `REPLY_RESULT_LIMIT` and `UNSUPPORTED_REPORT_LIMIT`; the worker `FAILURE` branch uses the pinned NER capability rather than hard-coding `true` for the limitation set; and the module header now states that the repeated #7 parse runs without #6's optional `host` argument, so the cooperative deadline still applies from its own default clock and only an injectable clock is out of reach.

**A third independent review of `c2bdae7` returned REQUEST_CHANGES; both blockers and the non-blocking items are fixed on this head.** F-A (MEDIUM): the previous fix for F2 deleted the worker's 4096-result cap, which is true of what the *parent* retains and false of what the child does before its first stdout byte — and that is exactly where the result array is filled and the whole reply is serialised. The child's memory and pre-output CPU were therefore bounded only by the host, and the README's "two named bounds" claim overstated it. The child-side `MAX_REPORTED_RESULTS = 4096` is **restored** and now refuses with a **named** `FAILURE` → `WORKER_REPORTED_FAILURE` rather than the earlier unnamed partial, so it never truncates; the transport's `WORKER_OUTPUT_LIMIT` and the adapter's `REPLY_RESULT_LIMIT` remain the other two, and the docs now say precisely what each bounds. The bound is **pinned** by a new stdlib-only boundary regression, [`test/presidio-worker-boundary.test.mjs`](../test/presidio-worker-boundary.test.mjs), which drives the real worker's unmodified `main()` against a synthetic injected engine: at the cap the worker reports every finding and the adapter names the parent overflow; past it the worker emits a sub-4 KiB frame with zero results and the adapter names `WORKER_REPORTED_FAILURE`. F-B (MEDIUM): the Stage 2 document's measured axis was contaminated by a Hylja comparison the same document records as unknown, and by downstream-utility certainty while the task claim is `untested`. The baseline comparison is now stated as a **source claim about `#37`/`#9`**, never as a measurement; "worse utility" is gone; option 4's exclusivity claim is corrected (option 3 configured with a model reaches the same place); every unbuilt option's row is re-labelled a projection rather than **M**; "#65's accepted vocabulary" is now "#65/#66's **proposed** vocabulary"; and the wheel-size row now distinguishes the 266 299-byte compressed artifact (the manifest's pinned `sizeBytes`) from 724 458 bytes uncompressed. The recommendation is now a deferral of runtime reuse that declares **no local winner**. Non-blocking: an invalid manifest that is valid JSON but not an object now answers a frame (`isinstance` guard plus a `filtering_block()` that cannot raise); a request line with trailing bytes after a complete JSON value is refused (`raw_decode` plus an exact-length check); an unreadable distribution version yields the fixed token `unknown` instead of an uncaught exception, which the adapter then refuses as `PRODUCER_VERSION_MISMATCH`; a worker-**declared** unsupported entity type with no corroborating result is reported as a bare count and its label never reaches a shared record (on that head a corroborated one kept its name; **that half of the rule is withdrawn** by the fifth-review paragraph below, because a corroborated label is still one worker agreeing with itself), and a repeated declared type is a malformed reply rather than last-wins; and the trace-on evidence is corrected to the measured fact that it really does emit 14 599 bytes of recognizer names and compiled patterns on stderr.

**A fourth independent review of `9549028` returned REQUEST_CHANGES; both blockers and the non-blocking items are fixed on this head, and the reviewer independently re-measured the restored child-side bound rather than reading it.** B1 (MEDIUM/LOW): the worker-`FAILURE` branch still returned `unsupported: parsed.unsupported`, so every worker-**declared** entity-type label survived on the one path that is an *ordinary* diagnostic - an unusable manifest or an import error - while only the successful path dropped it. That branch now returns no declared labels either and keeps only `declaredUnsupportedTypes`; the bridge regression covers `OK` and `FAILURE` replies and an adapter-level `FAILURE` frame, and asserts the planted token is absent from the printed record on each. B2 (LOW): the Stage 2 unknowns table still called the model "the only plausible source of Presidio's value", which is the exact claim R1 asked to remove and which §5 of the same document already contradicted; the row now names **two** unmeasured sources of value and points at the Swedish row, which is itself labelled as the other one. Non-blocking: the ungrammatical `pathRef` sentence is repaired to the accurate stable-across-requests wording; the boundary test's last group no longer asserts a regex over the worker's source text - it now **derives the bound from the observed `OK` -> `FAILURE` transition** and asserts both sides of it, which is behavioural evidence rather than a constant-matching guard; and the residual-limits paragraph now says precisely what has and has not been adversarially reviewed: the generated fake worker's boundaries and the real worker's framing, manifest, refusal and result-count paths were exercised against a synthetic injected engine, while the **real 51-wheel execution path** has never been independently re-run. The reviewer also corrected its own earlier stub count in this round; the bound conclusion is unchanged and is now measured at 4 096/4 097 findings rather than argued.

**Screened cost and disclosure measurements added for the Stage 2 recommendation** (same pinned stack, same network-unshared sandbox; **author** measurements on one host, not an SLA and not a comparative run): wall clock **1.01–1.07 s** for a one-shot analysis through the transport, of which **756 ms** is `spacy`/`presidio_analyzer` import and **123 ms** the first `analyze()` call, with **0.6 ms** engine construction and **1.68 ms** steady state per 44-character text; **122 220 KiB (119 MiB)** peak RSS for one worker process; a screened install of **51 wheels / 77.3 MiB**, 14 of them containing native code, 28 declaring no PEP 639 `License-Expression`. Trace-on and decision-process were both **enabled** in a pinned manifest over a 97-character text carrying a planted value: trace-on is **not** a no-op and emitted **14 599 bytes on stderr** carrying recognizer names and full compiled regex patterns (configuration detail, not the analysed values at this stage); the 898-byte reply carried no planted value, no `text_match`, no `pattern` and no `recognition_metadata`, and the transport's outcome contained none of it - **count-and-discard of stderr is the only thing keeping trace output out of a report**. The measured arm found 1/3 D01 and 1/2 D02 controls, could not look for a person name, discarded the synthetic `.invalid` email through its suffix check and produced four URL-fragment false positives per case. It is **not** compared to a Hylja baseline, because none was run on these inputs; the claim that `#37`/`#9` target the same synthetic values is a **source claim about Hylja**, and downstream task utility is **untested** (nothing is sent). The recommendation is therefore **reference-only, qualified defer** of runtime reuse - a deferral, not a finding that Presidio is worse, and not a local winner - with two unmeasured sources of value named (a screened model-backed profile, and the Swedish rule-based recognizers that were never enabled) and the model profile explicitly not built here.

**Residual limits a reviewer must weigh.** The binding is congruence, not authentication: a caller that fabricates the #6 result and the trusted scope can fabricate the request, and one process per analysis bounds cross-request reply replay but not a caller that lies about its own input. The host screen is supply-chain hygiene, not signature verification or an authenticated publisher conclusion. Resource bounds are per process, so a forking worker is outside what is measured, and a child that cannot be killed returns `WORKER_KILL_NOT_CONFIRMED` rather than claiming a clean reap. The sandbox wrapper is disposable infrastructure documented in the worker README, not committed or reviewed code. `placeExternalSpan`'s `FIELD` placement carries a the `FIELD` `pathRef` placeholder is **stable across requests** (`external-field-<view>-<index>`) and is deliberately not comparable with `detectNormalizedCandidates`'s request-bound digests; it never reaches a printed record. A self-review of the first committed head hardened four things that the groups above now pin: the transport's stdin error is handled instead of becoming an uncaught exception when a child exits before reading its request, the unconfirmed-reap case is an explicit `killedNotReaped` marker beside the original reason rather than an unreachable code, the `FIELD` placement repeat is charged against a cap, and `--case` now restricts the trial to the named cases instead of adding to the defaults (a CLI test caught it analysing an unrequested case). The generated fake worker's boundaries have been reviewed adversarially, and the real worker's framing, manifest, refusal and result-count boundaries have been exercised against a synthetic injected engine; the **real 51-wheel execution path itself** (third-party import, model-free recognizer behaviour, and the live adapter-to-Presidio span interaction) has never been independently re-run, and the manifest has not been through an independent adversarial review, remote CI has not run, and no release, trust, adoption, enforcement or scored claim follows from any of it.

**Fifth independent review: a reproduced disclosure of an untrusted label, fixed on this head (`presidio-report-sanitization`, based on `0536fa6`).** Root reproduced a real data channel on the approved head, not a hypothetical one: through the delivered `completePresidioAnalysis`, a reply that put one grammar-legal planted token in an unmapped result's `entityType` **and** in its matching `unsupported` declaration printed that exact plaintext in the result — `FAILURE`, zero candidates, and `JSON.stringify(result)` still containing the token — because a corroborated label was treated as reportable, and because for an unknown entity the type is examined before its span and score, so even a malformed result produced the same "evidence". A second reproduced case did the same through `producer.seam` on a *supported* `EMAIL_ADDRESS` with a valid span: both `patternName` and `recognizerId` set to the planted token reached the record. Root's point is the load-bearing one: two assertions from the **same** untrusted worker are not corroboration, a grammar is not a finite safe identity vocabulary, and a fail-closed decision does not make a plaintext report safe. The earlier approval of `0536fa6` covered the declared-only path and is **not** evidence for these cases. Both are closed by the same rule — **every string this boundary reports is a committed constant, a closed code or a trusted pin** — applied to *all* reportable reply paths rather than one branch: `PRESIDIO_DECLARED_UNSUPPORTED_ENTITY_TYPES` names only refused types this repository vouches for (explicitly **not** claimed to be the upstream catalogue's complete list), `PRESIDIO_PINNED_RECOGNIZER_IDS` is exactly the pinned manifest's enabled recognizer set, a pattern name is never carried at all, and every other refused label or identifier becomes `entityType: null` + `unpinnedUnsupportedTypes`, `recognizerIdUnpinned` or `patternNameReported` — an honest, countable evidence loss that never promotes a label to a candidate. `provenance.labelVocabularyVersion` (`presidio-label-vocabulary/1`) says which vocabulary produced a record's names. Known canonical observations stay legible (`US_SSN`, `LOCATION` and the rest of the pinned list still print by name; a pinned recognizer class name is still carried; supported spans and candidate utility are unchanged), and the bridge now reports `unpinnedUnsupportedResults`/`unpinnedUnsupportedLabels` instead of a name. **RED evidence, honestly attributed:** root ran its probe once against `0536fa6` and reproduced the disclosure (three `true`s); I reproduced the same three cases on this checkout before the fix (same output), then closed them (`false`/`false`/assertion-fails-on-purported-true). The new adapter regressions were shown to catch the old behaviour by re-introducing it in the **compiled output only** and rebuilding from source afterwards: the four new/updated adapter groups and the bridge group failed on the pre-fix semantics and pass on the fix. **Commands actually run on this head** (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, Python 3.14.4, Linux x86_64; no push, PR, remote CI, approval or human gate): `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0; focused `node --test test/presidio-candidate-source.test.mjs` **37/37**, `test/presidio-trial-bridge.test.mjs` **7/7** and `test/presidio-worker-boundary.test.mjs` + `test/presidio-worker-process.test.mjs` **16/16**, none skipped; and one full `flock /tmp/hylja-agentic-campaign/test.lock npm test` on the final tree **766/766 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** (prior head 763; the three new groups are the no-disclosure, seam and vocabulary cases, net of the one replaced group). Coverage was not re-measured and the 51-wheel run was not repeated. No third-party code, wheel or model was executed, installed or re-screened here; the Python workers and the host wrapper are byte-identical to the blobs reviewed earlier (`presidio_worker.py` `7fa5d097934dfd42be583af842276c8ade062c61`, `fake_worker.py` `aa5b480d7372969a04a2daf9aa7f30277c2325e8`, `presidio-worker-process.ts` `ba022e449a432ad47304301d903e7b7077e70b0f`, `manifest.json` `3f0a89bf3d991f4bcc4633342096341da1f743e2`), so the earlier adversarial review of them carries over unchanged, and the real 51-wheel run was neither repeated nor re-attributed to anyone else.

**Incremental #40 preparation, 2026-10-02 (corrected after an independent REQUEST_CHANGES on `b3af062a`, superseded run preserved): one matched, UNSCORED public-development comparison of three arms over the same 15 planted controls, on a fresh owned, name-and-digest-verified runtime.** [`evaluations/matched-development-comparison.mjs`](../evaluations/matched-development-comparison.mjs) compares the current native baseline (#6 `detectNormalizedCandidates`, **configured by omission only** - no tenant name dictionary, no #10 handle, no asserted #7 format, because building a dictionary from the fixture's own planted names would be tuning a detector against the cases being measured), the already-pinned `presidio-analyzer==2.2.364` configuration through the #113 arm, and their **measurement-only union**; the full record is [the dated research evidence](research/issue-40-matched-development-comparison-2026-10-02.md), which is where every number and limit lives. It reuses the existing owners: the Presidio arm is `runPresidioDevelopmentArm`, extracted from `presidio-development-trial.mjs` with that trial's printed record **byte-identical before and after**, and the scoring owner is still #5 - the run **throws rather than print** if its per-control breakdown ever disagreed with #5's aggregate. `src/` is unchanged and no detector, classifier, policy, threshold, recognizer set or dependency moved. Controls: every **v1-admissible occurrence the independent #39 oracle draft plants for these two cases** (7 of them, each bound to that draft's own span or dropped as `CONTROL_SPAN_DISAGREES_WITH_DECLARED_ORACLE_SPAN`), the 5 `#113` trial controls, and 3 engineering/SECRET controls; every scored label is accepted v1, none is planted from candidate output, none is dropped silently, and the draft's own subtypes (`CUSTOMER_NAME`, `HOSTNAME`, …) are cross-references only.

**What the review corrected, and what it superseded (the `b3af062a` run is not conformant).** Its pre-execution account was wrong in both directions: all **51 pinned digests are present**, and the defect is that the `zipp 3.23.0` record's digest/size/members are those of **`setuptools 84.0.0`** (whose own metadata says so; `zipp 3.23.0` exists here only vendored inside it), so the #46 screen is a complete *digest* set containing one record that **names a package its bytes are not**. That distribution installs `distutils-precedence.pth`, whose `import` line `site` execs at interpreter startup, and the prior venv ran it on **every** worker start - absence of an analyzer import was never evidence. The venv-content figure was also wrong: 9 586 installed files, 5 027 not from a selected wheel, 52 distributions, 1 `.pth`, 4 146 byte-compiled entries. The manifest keeps its 51 records and gains a dated `corrections[]` entry plus an `executionEnvironment` declaring the executed subset; the corrected run executes a **fresh owned 50-artifact runtime** built by `python3 -m venv --without-pip` plus standard-library wheel extraction (no installer code ran), with **`pip` and `setuptools` absent**, and [`evaluations/presidio-worker/verify_selected_runtime.py`](../evaluations/presidio-worker/verify_selected_runtime.py) verifies before execution that every selected artifact's *own* name matches its record, every runtime file is a byte-identical member of a selected wheel (4 609/4 609), the distribution set matches, and there are **0 startup hooks, 0 byte-compiled entries and 0 non-regular entries**. The prior `/tmp/hylja113` was read only, never executed again and never mutated. **Two further independent review rounds (`f0d8e1f` and `8883971`) each found defects in this new verification boundary and none in the measurement, and all are now fixed.** On `f0d8e1f`: the helper's own `status` used to be **copied** into the record while the checks were dropped, so a reply declaring `VERIFIED` beside a failing `RUNTIME_STARTUP_HOOKS` became a *verified execution environment* - **the status is now derived by the bridge from the checks it accepted, a disagreement is refused in either direction** (`VERIFICATION_STATUS_DISAGREES_WITH_ACCEPTED_CHECKS`), an incomplete check set is refused (`VERIFICATION_CHECK_SET_INCOMPLETE`), `ok` and `code` may no longer disagree, and `executionEnvironment` **carries the accepted checks** so a reader sees which passed and that `PINNED_RECORDS_ALL_RESOLVED` is deliberately not ok; the helper's refusals now **report their own fixed cause** instead of five distinct causes collapsing into one shape rejection; a **symlinked runtime entry is refused** rather than skipped because Python imports through one; name identity and screen coverage became **one check whose `ok` comes from two counters**; and the README names the limit it actually emits. On `8883971`: `RUNTIME_FILE_PROVENANCE` could report `ok: false` with `code: null`, because the check was the conjunction of six conditions while the code ladder named only two, so an **unreadable file** - a module Python can import whose provenance cannot be established - was a real runtime defect the record misreported as a protocol shape rejection; **every cause now has its own fixed code** by a documented priority, with every count in the detail whichever is chosen: `NON_REGULAR_RUNTIME_ENTRY`, `RUNTIME_ENTRY_UNREADABLE`, `RUNTIME_FILE_TOO_LARGE_TO_COMPARE`, `RUNTIME_SCAN_TRUNCATED`, `RUNTIME_FILE_NOT_IN_SELECTED_WHEELS`, `RUNTIME_SELECTED_FILE_MISSING`. In the same seam a pin record naming a **self-unidentifiable artifact** is reported as `ARTIFACT_OWNS_NO_SINGLE_TOP_LEVEL_DIST_INFO` instead of collapsing the reply, the previously dead `PIN_CHECKS` constant is **wired** into a completeness check on the helper's own check list rather than deleted, and the non-regular regression now covers a **symlinked directory and a FIFO** as well as a symlinked file. **No measured number changed in either round:** both reviews independently reproduced the tables below from the public fixtures, and the corrected run after each fix set is identical to the one before it apart from wall clock. The `falsePositives`/`Precision` columns were misleading: two of the "false positives" were exact detections of oracle-planted occurrences the eight controls omitted, so unmatched events are now **classified** (duplicate / planted-occurrence label difference / oracle-declared-negative contradicted / **unresolved**) and unresolved is never called an error. One section-4.2 sentence claimed the native arm "finds" the planted port when there is **no candidate at that span**; the vocabulary now says `NO_CANDIDATE_AT_SPAN` with `sameLabelElsewhere`, and the invented `persona.de` fragment example is withdrawn here and in the #113 document.

**Measured on the corrected runtime, `scored: false`, `enforcing: false`, sent-nothing:** native **8/15** controls (D01 3/7, D02 5/8), pinned Presidio **2/15** (1/7, 1/8), union **8/15** - so on these inputs combining added **0 additional matched controls and 8 additional unmatched events**, all unresolved, and folded 1 cross-arm identical event per case plus 1 intra-arm duplicate. Per #37 subtype: Presidio missed both EMAIL occurrences (its suffix check invalidates `.invalid`) and **could not look for a name at all**; the unconfigured native arm found EMAIL and PHONE by pattern and had **no NAME source configured**, so NAME recall for a *configured* native deployment is **not measured here**; `PERSON/NAME` is a miss in all three arms and no judge, sentinel or union credits it. The planted port is missing candidate generation in every arm, and the planted URL is not a Presidio hit (right label, different span). Exactly **one** over-generation event in the whole run is an established error against an independent declaration; the rest are a duplicate or unresolved. Upstream limits are the #113 set unchanged, with zero inexact placements, declared unsupported types and unpinned labels. **Nothing is sent in any arm, so #5 reports the escape and task claims as `untested` for all of them**: over-generation is not measured unnecessary cloaking and not task failure. The **Privacy Filter arm is `PENDING`/not installed, not screened and not run**, never a zero. Cost measured here: 5 one-shot worker processes per run, Presidio **2.21-2.39 s** for D01's one field and **8.74-10.24 s** for D02's four, against a native arm at **17-23 ms** per case - not an SLA, no peak-RSS figure re-measured, and the corrected runtime's higher per-process cost is **unexplained**. Two independent corrected runs produced **identical records apart from wall-clock milliseconds**. The real-stack failure path was re-characterized on the corrected runtime: a named `WORKER_CRASHED`, zero candidates, every control a miss, unchanged native arm and union, and the child's real diagnostic reaching no field of the record.

**Evidence at this head (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, Python 3.14.4, Linux x86_64; no push, PR, remote CI, approval or human gate):** `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0; focused `node --test test/matched-development-comparison.test.mjs` **23/23** and `test/presidio-trial-bridge.test.mjs` **7/7**, none skipped; one full `flock /tmp/hylja-agentic-campaign/test.lock npm test` on the final tree **878/878 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** and the same tree under `npm run test:coverage` **877/878 at 98.76 % line / 93.03 % branch / 96.89 % function** across all files, with the new bridge at **99.15 % line**. **The one coverage-run failure is a pre-existing, unattributed wall-clock budget in a different module, recorded here rather than re-run until green:** `test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs:148` group 269 `review: shared-prefix entries and adversarial payloads stay within a bounded-work budget` fails `elapsedMs < 5000` at **5 468.47 ms** on its `jo x ` input. This head's diff touches **no `src/` file** and nothing that group loads, the group **passes 72/72 when run alone with and without coverage instrumentation**, and it fails under `--experimental-test-coverage` in five of five whole-tree coverage runs while `npm test` passed **878/878** twice at this head. **No cause is attributed and none is excluded**, no budget was raised, no threshold or fixture was changed, and the intermittent nature matches what this plan already records about the egress-sentinel suite's 5-second budgets. It is not evidence about anything this head changed - the only uncovered lines being the documented defensive `catch` around the verifier's `spawnSync`, which reports an unusable interpreter through `error`/`status` instead, and one unreachable off-vocabulary branch (measured, not assumed). **RED checkpoint, honestly bounded:** this task changed no contract, so there is no pre-existing behaviour to reproduce red; what is reproduced red is **this task's own new code, eight times, each caught before any third-party result was accepted**. The screen verifier first refused all 51 records because its metadata reader addressed the dist-info *directory* instead of that directory's `METADATA` member; then the comparison rejected a legitimate `declared: null` because the validator required every detail value to be a count; then a malformed verifier reply was parsed **outside** the boundary and threw out of the comparison runner instead of being refused; then a helper answering a non-numeric runtime count was silently read as **zero** rather than refused; then the verification boundary **copied the helper's `status`** instead of deriving it, so a synthetic reply declaring `VERIFIED` with a failing `RUNTIME_STARTUP_HOOKS` check was recorded as a **verified** execution environment (the reviewer's adversarial matrix, reproduced here as three declared-status rows, the mirror case and an incomplete check set); then the runtime scan **skipped a symlinked entry silently**, so `RUNTIME_FILE_PROVENANCE` called a runtime clean while an importable out-of-tree module sat inside it; then a **runtime file the verifier cannot read** was reported as a protocol shape rejection because the provenance code ladder had no code for that cause; then a **self-unidentifiable pinned artifact** collapsed the whole reply as a shape rejection for the same reason. Each is pinned by the new groups, and the groups for the last six fail against the code as it stood before those fixes. **No red group is claimed against pre-existing behavior**: the manifest correction is additive, the trial extraction is proven behaviour-preserving by the byte-identical record, and no contract changed. **Remaining and unchanged:** #39's freeze, blind custody and scoring; #46 adoption due diligence; #48's reuse decision; #41's incremental claim, deferred for want of a qualified winner; downstream task utility; a configured native NAME/#10 arm; Swedish identifier families; any model-backed Presidio profile; a URL threshold sweep; a persistent worker; peak RSS; whether the real upstream configuration can suppress native SECRET (unexercised: the pinned allow list is empty); the independent adversarial review of the pinned manifest; and root's reopening of #113's first acceptance item, which this work corrects but does not close.

**Foundation issues [#1](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/1)–[#5](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/5) are closed as completed at their bounded, NON-ENFORCING scope.** Their independently reviewed implementations landed in [PR #51](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/51), [#52](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/52), [#53](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/53), [#56](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/56) and [#58](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/58); the public unscored #39 adapter landed separately in [PR #60](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/60). [Main CI run 36169013008](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36169013008) passed the fixture guard, locked build/typecheck/tests/coverage, dependency audit and ephemeral SBOM after those merges. Earlier local/stacked PR checkpoints below retain their historical timing, not the current merge/approval state. Product direction and the backlog remain foundation-only. The TypeScript source has a non-enforcing egress sentinel core, but still no identity authenticator, production adapter/broker, runtime-enforced Policy Engine, vault, transport interceptor or privacy boundary; #39 remains open without independent blind custody, approved destination/task rubric, a pre-tuning or final-v0 lock or a scored baseline.

Independently reviewed [PR #89](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/89), [#90](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/90) and [#91](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/91) merged bounded #6 normalized candidate composition, #7 YAML/TOML/XML parser subsets and #88 sentinel hardening. A disposable combined tree passed 390/390 tests and typecheck before merge; [main CI run 36395638877](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36395638877) passed the fixture guard, build, typecheck, tests, coverage, dependency audit and ephemeral SBOM at `77ac1c7`. **Integration note (local merge only; no push, PR, remote CI or approval here).** Current main `2f1ecab5653056d1e3491083c5b372d5c41ed6b6` (tree `31703f0`) was merged into this feature branch as one normal local merge commit over `cfef55b452c90d011659e6fdbc49f0c4a1effb6d`; `docs/plan.md` was the only file both sides changed and it auto-merged without conflict, so both sides' historical evidence is preserved verbatim. The sentinel contract is unchanged there: `src/egress-sentinel.ts` (blob `9c3ea065`) and `test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` (blob `94c7db39`) are byte-identical to the independently approved source head `cfef55b`, and the compiled `dist/egress-sentinel.js` is byte-identical (sha256 `bae27df4…`) to a disposable build of that exact approved head. That head's 60/60 focused groups, 405/405 full run and 99.57 % line / 95.24 % branch / 100 % function coverage are **its** evidence, not the combined count, and were not repeated here. Measured on the combined tree (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): a clean locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, focused `node --test test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` (60/60 groups), `npm test` (**604/604 tests**), `npm run check:release` (**17/17** synthetic release cases, `SYNTHETIC_RELEASE_GATE_CONSISTENT`) and `git diff --check` all pass. The sentinel's only merged-side changes to its direct inputs are `package.json` and a strictly additive `src/node-crypto.d.ts` (`update` widened to `string | Uint8Array`, new Ed25519 declarations for the release gate), which typecheck and build accept with the sentinel source untouched; main's new `src/adapter-conformance.ts` calls `checkEgress`, so the combined and release runs exercise the unchanged sentinel through main's release gate. Root-supplied evidence records main `2f1ecab`'s own prior integration as 590 tests with the same 17 synthetic release cases and remote CI run 36926486833 successful; that is main's evidence and is **not** the count of this merge. This note accepts nothing: proposed [decision 010](decisions/010-separate-information-dimensions-and-task-fidelity.md) and #65, #66 and #68 stay proposed, no runtime authority is added, and the modules still do not form a runtime-enforced privacy boundary, so no enforcement, held-out, approval, push, PR or remote-CI claim follows from a green local merge.

A local, not yet independently approved follow-on implements the #19/#88 residual encoded-view work for representations that declare their own reconstruction (labelled chunks, declared `+`/`/`/`>` joins, JSON arrays of labelled objects, decimal and Node `Buffer` byte arrays). Independent review of its published head `2f48013` requested changes: it found an encoded-looking field interleaved between labelled chunks that still released a planted original and canary, and it found that fail-closed assertions in the tests-only RED checkpoint had been re-scoped rather than satisfied. A second review of the corrected local head `5edaffc` (`REQUEST_CHANGES`, report only; it approves nothing and is not amended here) requested changes again on two points: the declared series was invisible whenever the sender's label names were all hex letters (`face1 … faceN`), so the same payload that `part1 … partN` blocks released a planted original and canary at 6- and 8-character chunk widths, and the budget paragraph mis-stated which RED assertions the replay actually leaves unsatisfied. Both are corrected here — a hex-labelled field is read when its own numbers declare one complete series, a dump offset column is still read as one and joins no reading, and the budget contract paragraph below states the replay exactly, in which only the decimal-array assertion is unsatisfied. Its evidence, new attack surfaces and measured residuals are recorded in the milestone section below. These modules still do not form a runtime-enforced privacy boundary.

A separate local branch adds the #114 overlap characterization of the accepted #3/#6/#7/#8 contracts plus a **proposed**, unwired union/rewrite draft and its contract; it is described in full in the milestone section below, it approves nothing, and #114, #13, #3, #7, #8, #37, #40 and #68 keep their existing status.

Public colleague invitations for separate policy and engineering-task review landed in [PR #69](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/69); they appoint nobody and establish no approval or custody. [#65 taxonomy research](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/65) and [#66 classification/privacy/sensitivity/trust/task-fidelity design](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/66) remain open pre-freeze inputs to #39. The relevant results must be reflected in an independently reviewed oracle schema and fidelity vocabulary **before** any #39 v0 freeze; public outreach or a passing preparation checker cannot substitute for that design gate.

A **proposed** [decision 010](decisions/010-separate-information-dimensions-and-task-fidelity.md) and [draft information-model contract](contracts/information-model-draft.md) offer a #66/#68 starting point for human review: separate semantic, privacy-attribute, sensitivity, trust, task-fidelity and treatment homes; conservative deterministic-floor/semantic-escalation resolution; a fidelity predicate vocabulary; typed non-reversible semantic placeholders; and a candidate #39 oracle annotation schema. Its executable [`draft-2` model](../src/information-model-draft.ts) uses `VALUE_SHAPE` for task fidelity, distinct from classifier `FORMAT` evidence, and rejects old `FORMAT` fidelity contracts. It is pure, property-tested and **not wired** into classification v1, policy or any adapter. It is AI-authored and AI-reviewed only, which does **not** satisfy the human #66 design/review gate or any #39 freeze prerequisite.

An AI-authored, **proposed** [transformation semantics draft for #68](contracts/transformation-semantics-draft.md) now exists as the design input for human review, beside the decision 010 draft rather than inside it. It keeps the existing `draft-2` vocabulary (`EXACT`, `IDENTITY_SYNTHETIC`, `OPAQUE_TOKEN`, `SEMANTIC_PLACEHOLDER`, `GENERALIZED`, `REMOVED`, `WITHHELD`) and adds release-form requirements: one released form per occurrence, no synthetic or opaque-token representation for credentials, every derived fact (presence, emptiness, validity, role, topology, local-effect outcome) approved for the actual destination in its own right, per-occurrence `EXACT` for low-risk task-critical values such as port `443` without letting usefulness select a treatment, entity-scoped identity with role retained as separately authorized content, shape-preserving subnet/topology generalization inside documentation ranges, placeholder grammar that stays inert in shells and tools while explicitly not being a security token, typed transformation failure instead of any original fallback, and a binding set covering the transformation contract version, policy/classification digests, task-fidelity contract version and digest, exact payload/source metadata, observed-sink metadata and the future independent exact serialized-byte check. It proposes paired privacy/utility development scenarios for the existing [#5 seam](contracts/synthetic-evaluation-seam.md) and no second framework. Evidence is deliberately split: what exists now is public, unscored development material (D01/D02/D05 fixture and oracle *proposals*, the unwired `draft-2` model, #7 `rewriteFieldValues` round-trips for JSON/DOTENV/INI/URL/connection strings and quoted YAML/TOML/XML, the #5 exact-byte capture); what is required later — a round-trip-safe unstructured/log rewrite that does not exist (#7 parses logs as `FIELDS_ONLY`), the frozen #39 v0 protocol, human-accepted #65/#66 dimensions and treatments, an implemented #13, a sentinel at a real send point, and [#43](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/43) comparisons — cannot be scored or claimed now, so #68 cannot be closed by local tests. Documentation only: no source, test, fixture or preparation-manifest bytes changed, no v1 classifier/policy contract, no accepted ADR and no existing proposal was modified; decision 010 and the #65/#66 drafts remain **proposed**, the placeholder grammar is unadopted, and this note is AI-prepared material for human review, not adoption, approval, enforcement, a sentinel guarantee or a benchmark result.

An independent AI review of the first local #68 head `e722eb164654ec484a0918949a94e93f5fd33377` (branch `agentic/fidelity-design`, base `2f1ecab5653056d1e3491083c5b372d5c41ed6b6`) returned REQUEST_CHANGES with three documentation defects, all reproduced here against the public API with disposable bounded synthetic probes before fixing: the draft's available-now evidence column listed #7 log round-trip rewriting, which does not exist (log parses are `FIELDS_ONLY`, so `rewriteFieldValues` refuses them with `SOURCE_NOT_COMPLETE`) and which §3.3 now states plainly; the draft claimed #19 does not know the placeholder grammar, whereas the shipped sentinel core already exempts a credential value that is a whole `[hylja:protected:KIND]` marker by shape alone, with no issuance tracking and no kind validation; and §14's unresolved-sensitivity question misdescribed the existing proposals, which already allow a typed placeholder when kind is independently established and destination policy permits, so the question now asks whether to keep or tighten that ceiling. That head was corrected at `fc0878444879e500de37e435c05c0e54593535da`; the following paragraph records what the next review of it found.

An independent AI review of the corrected head `fc0878444879e500de37e435c05c0e54593535da` (same branch and base `2f1ecab5653056d1e3491083c5b372d5c41ed6b6`) returned REQUEST_CHANGES again: it reproduced all three earlier fixes as fixed, then found that the corrected text named **one** live consumer of the placeholder grammar where there are **two**, and that my precision-note fix had introduced a routable prefix into a synthetic example. Both were reproduced with disposable bounded synthetic probes before and after the fix. The placeholder grammar is honoured upstream as well as downstream: `#8` `isReference` in [`src/secret-detectors.ts`](../src/secret-detectors.ts) skips a credential-shaped value whose whole text is a marker and emits **no candidate**, so it is never classified `SECRET`, so decision 009's ceiling and decision 010 item 1's credential floor never constrain it (probe: `password = "[hylja:protected:PASSWORD]"` → 0 candidates versus 1 `PASSWORD/context.key-assignment` for the equivalent real-shaped value; same asymmetry for URL userinfo and XML element; a lowercase kind is still flagged, an arbitrary uppercase kind is not); the #19 sentinel exemption is the later and weaker effect. Sections 7 and 11, scenario T13 and §13 gates 4–5 now name both layers in that order and scope issuance tracking, kind validation and policy-approved-only reference treatment to **#8/#13 and #19**, and §7 states the design consequence that a marker-shaped input value is an ordinary unclassified value. The routable-prefix regression is gone: the §6 example now uses `198.18.7.17`/`198.18.7.0/24` → `198.18.7.31`/`198.18.0.0/15`, strictly inside the enumerated documentation ranges (probe: the coarsened `/16` I had written was outside every enumerated range; `198.18.0.0/15` is inside). Two precision notes were also applied: §10 now says the parser's existing `SOURCE_NOT_COMPLETE`/`UNENCODABLE_REPLACEMENT` refusals are inherited as-is rather than renamed into the proposed `TRANSFORM_*` vocabulary, and §11 named the sentinel's header-token and userinfo rules as well as credential assignment, which the next paragraph corrects. Still documentation only: no source, test, fixture, evaluation, script or manifest byte changed, no accepted ADR, no edit to decision 010 or the #65/#66 drafts, and the #8/#19 behaviour above is described, not fixed or tested here — no repository test was added, because a test that only re-reads this document would prove nothing. No test suite result is claimed at this head: `src`, `test`, `evaluations`, `scripts` and the lockfile are byte-identical to `fc08784`, whose identical source tree passed `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (**590/590**, exit 0), so the broad suite was not repeated.

An independent AI review of head `60765d2f50a5bcef15c27c659b61200d1b2d321a` (same branch and base `2f1ecab5653056d1e3491083c5b372d5c41ed6b6`) returned **APPROVE**, reproducing all five findings from the two earlier rounds as fixed and confirming the synthetic-data cleanup, with one non-blocking factual error it recorded for correction: §11 over-listed the sentinel rules that exempt the marker shape, naming `pattern.credential-assignment`, `pattern.authorization-credential` and `pattern.url-password` as sharing one `REFERENCE` exemption, and the preceding paragraph repeated that here. The three do not share it. Re-derived from the shipped source and confirmed with a disposable bounded synthetic probe before fixing: `pattern.credential-assignment` exempts a whole marker because its `REFERENCE` set carries the marker alternative (and an unquoted one because that rule's not-a-value test starts with a bracket; `{"apiKey": "[hylja:protected:API_KEY]"}` → `ALLOW`, real-shaped control → `BLOCK [pattern.credential-assignment]`); `pattern.authorization-credential` lets a marker through only incidentally, because its token character class cannot match a bracketed marker (`Authorization: Bearer [hylja:protected:API_KEY]` → `ALLOW`, real-shaped control → `BLOCK [pattern.authorization-credential]`); and `pattern.url-password` exempts the separate `URL_PLACEHOLDER` set (`${VAR}`, `{{x}}`, `<x>`, `$VAR`, `%s`), which carries no marker alternative, so a marker in URL userinfo is still flagged `BLOCK` (`https://svc:[hylja:protected:API_KEY]@example.invalid/v1` → `BLOCK [pattern.url-password]`, while the same position with `${SYNTHETIC_VAR}` → `ALLOW`, so the rule is capable of exempting a reference there and simply excludes the marker). §11 now states exactly that and scopes "indistinguishable from an issued one" to the sites that exempt the shape; the over-statement ran in the fail-safe direction and changed no conclusion, gate or released behaviour. Prose only: no code, test, fixture, oracle, evaluation, script, manifest or dependency byte changed and no runtime exemption was broadened to fit the wording; the #8 detector's own `isReference` mask/reference behaviour is untouched and stays as documented. Checks run at this head: that bounded probe, `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` and `node evaluations/preparation-integrity.mjs` (exit 0, `PUBLIC_DRAFT_ONLY`), `git diff --check`, whitespace/line-ending and relative-link/anchor checks over the two edited files. No build, typecheck, coverage, dependency audit, SBOM or test suite was re-run, because `src`, `test`, `evaluations`, `scripts` and the lockfile are byte-identical to `60765d2`, whose identical source tree passed **590/590**, and no suite result is claimed here. Placeholder-grammar/spoofing review, issuance tracking, kind validation and any authentication of a marker remain **proposed** human gates (§13.4–§13.5) with #13 unimplemented and #19 unchanged; decision 010 and the #65/#66 drafts remain **proposed**, and this is still AI-prepared documentation for human review, not adoption, approval, enforcement, a sentinel guarantee or a benchmark result.
