Local #46 continuation on 2026-10-01 on branch `agentic/candidate-prescreen` (TypeScript source, tests and fixtures unchanged at `2f1ecab`; **documentation plus new research-only helper scripts under [`scripts/research/`](../scripts/research/README.md), not wired into `npm test`, CI, the core or any adapter, and to be reviewed as security-sensitive**): a [dated provenance and runtime follow-up](research/issue-46-presidio-provenance-runtime-prescreen-2026-10-01.md) now **verifies** the two previously unverified PEP 740 attestation bundles instead of only parsing them. Each bundle produced 31 separate reported checks with 31 PASS/0 FAIL/0 UNVERIFIED (62 total): DSSE v1 PAE signature recomputed and verified under the Fulcio leaf key, leaf→intermediate→root signatures recomputed against a trust anchor pinned by digest, the RFC 6962 inclusion proof recomputed here, the Rekor v2 signed entry timestamp and signed checkpoint verified against the log key in that same anchor, inclusion time inside the certificate validity window, the embedded precertificate SCT verified, the in-toto subject bound to the exact wheel bytes, and the certificate identity (SAN URI, OIDC issuer, source repository URI/digest/ref, build config URI, trigger, run invocation URI, visibility) compared against **independently fetched** GitHub records rather than against the certificate. The trust anchor was corroborated from two independent public sources with the TUF metadata's own signatures verified offline (root version 15, 5 of 6 keys against a threshold of 3; targets version 14 likewise), and treating Sigstore's roots as an anchor is recorded as a **research assumption, not a Hylja trust policy**. The same follow-up pins and screens the **minimum** anonymizer runtime (`presidio-anonymizer` 2.2.364 plus `cryptography` 48.0.1, `cffi` 2.1.1, `pycparser` 3.0 — digests compared against independent PyPI records, licenses read from the wheels themselves) and runs an unscored, synthetic-only `mask`/`hash` smoke inside an ephemeral unprivileged `bwrap` environment with no network, no host/repo/credential mount, read-only screened wheels, private tmpfs and rlimited CPU/memory/process/file/output/time; the offline install and import reported 40/40 installed members byte-identical to the screened wheel, and the smoke reported only booleans, counts and versions (all steps exit 0, zero candidate stdout/stderr bytes, reversible operators deliberately not exercised so no mapping state exists). Honest gaps carried forward: the published attestation is the **publish** predicate only, so nothing binds wheel contents to a source build and no reproducible-build or SLSA provenance evidence exists; `cryptography` 48.0.1 is affected by two HIGH and one MODERATE advisory with **no fixed version inside `presidio-anonymizer`'s declared `cryptography<49.0.0` range**, unreachable from the exercised code path but unresolved for adoption; the sdists, the analyzer and its eight mandatory dependencies, any NLP configuration, model weight or hosted-service extra remain unselected, undownloaded and unscreened; the wheels still package no LICENSE/NOTICE; and the full transitive/SBOM, maintenance, exit-path and legal review remain the later adoption gate. This is unscored development evidence and a public trial-gate step only — not a comparison, not a #39 pre-tuning lock or freeze, not blind custody, not #48 selection, not adoption approval and not any protected-egress boundary.

Correction pass on 2026-10-01 to that head, after an independent review returned REQUEST_CHANGES on the untrusted-output and resource-boundary layer of `scripts/research/` (the provenance verification and the smoke itself were confirmed reproducible and defect-free): the child result schema no longer accepts free-form strings at all — booleans, range-checked integers and this repository's fixed codes only, with rejected entries **counted** rather than named — and the child no longer supplies any version, entity-type or exception-class string, the driver deriving the runtime versions itself from installed metadata with a strict grammar and comparing them to the recorded pins; child execution moved to a new bounded path that counts and **discards** child stdout/stderr, kills the child's process group with `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` at a 64 KiB per-step ceiling (the `RLIMIT_FSIZE` in the sandbox bounds files, not pipes) and maps spawn failure, memory exhaustion, timeout and every other error to fixed codes so the driver emits one complete report for floods, timeouts and spawn failures and never a traceback (the outer wall-clock kill is not the driver and can end a run with exit 124 before any report is written — see [`scripts/research/README.md`](../scripts/research/README.md)); the sandbox process cap fell from 512 to 64 with the bounds now described precisely as **per process** (and `RLIMIT_NPROC` per real UID), no aggregate bound being available without privileges, recorded as a runtime-execution gate; and the host-side prescreen helper now digests bounded local bytes and **refuses an absent or mismatched index pin before opening any archive**, with member-count, member-size, metadata-size and metadata-compression-ratio bounds and fixed codes that echo no file, member, URL or body. Committed behavioural regressions were written first, and the corrected self-test is 55/55 at this head. **The red-phase counts previously recorded here (53 tests: 20 failures, 3 errors) are withdrawn:** no committed artifact reproduces them, because that intermediate red-phase test file was never committed and no disposed source tree is claimed to be committed or replayable here. The red phase is instead substantiated by an independent review of `cc0de14` (an external report, not committed to this repository) which ran this head's self-test against `7d6c2a4`'s copies of the seven helpers in a private temporary directory and got **55 tests, 19 failures and 8 errors**, with every `ChildResultSchemaTest`, `UntrustedOutputSentinelTest` and `PrescreenHelperTest` behavioural test failing. Those failures are exactly the original defects: the child result schema still accepted free-form strings, so the child could supply version, entity-type and exception-class text; the old child-execution path read the child's pipes with no per-step ceiling, so an output flood could exhaust the driver instead of yielding `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`; and the host prescreen read and parsed downloaded artifact bytes before refusing an absent or mismatched published pin. (In that comparison the bounded-execution cases pass only because the new `bounded_run.py` was supplied alongside; the timeout behaviour is established instead by the end-to-end sandbox probes described here.) Inert, candidate-free reproducers through the committed sandbox now show a hostile result file yields **zero** planted literals with 7 counted rejections, a 256 MiB+ output flood yields `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` with 0 bytes retained and a complete report, and a hanging child yields `STEP_TIMEOUT` with no traceback. Re-runs of the changed sources confirmed: the smoke reproduces every prior boolean/count (40/40 installed members, mask 92, hash 131, both pin matches); the hardened prescreen reproduces all 38 recorded artifacts exactly; and the corrected verifier reports **32 PASS per artifact, 64 total, 0 FAIL, 0 UNVERIFIED**, the only change being the added counted check that the attestation's transparency-log key is the pinned anchor's `rekor.sigstore.dev` key — which also corrects a misnamed anchor entry in the research document, along with the capture-mechanism, plant-lifetime and per-process-bounds wording. This safety work does **not** re-verify the signed bundles (inputs and verifier crypto unchanged), and every gate in the paragraph above — human #46 adoption review, #39 decisions, #48 selection, decision 010 and the taxonomy drafts — remains exactly as it was.

Documentation-precision pass on 2026-10-02 to `cc0de14` (documentation only: no runtime behaviour, threshold, resource limit, dependency, child-output-handling, candidate-screening-policy or test-assertion change — the edits are five places in the research document (four paragraphs and one observation-table row), `scripts/research/README.md`, and module docstrings/comments in four helpers; `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` still reports `Ran 55 tests ... OK`, `git diff --check` is clean, and the TypeScript tree is untouched). An independent review of that exact head returned **APPROVE** with **no blocking findings**, closed all five root findings (hostile child result, bounded child output and resource bounds, evidence precision, host prescreen hardening) and raised eight documentation-precision items, corrected there — one of them incompletely, as the next paragraph records: the dossier's stale 30-test self-test count (now the committed 55/55); the "only definitional change" claim, which omitted both that archive **entries** are now counted separately from **file members** and that package identity now comes from the artifact's own `METADATA: Name:` spelling while the tables and the index request use the normalized hyphenated key; the false claim that `HANDOFF_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` lowers a step deadline inside the sandbox (`--clearenv` plus an explicit `--setenv` list never passes it, so the driver's 120 s default stands, while host-side `HANDOFF_WALL_CLOCK_SECONDS` does apply); the "the driver always emits exactly one JSON report" claim, which the outer wall-clock kill can pre-empt with exit 124 and no stdout or stderr; the "no child byte is ever retained" wording (one transient 64 KiB chunk per read, no accumulation, and a counted ceiling that may overshoot by at most one chunk); the child-result schema docstring's silence on the trusted-side `RESULT_READ_ERROR:<PythonExceptionTypeName>` suffix the driver emits as `child_result_error_code`; the installed-tree pair's completion/match relationship; and the README's omitted host-prescreen self-test coverage plus its stale "both print one JSON object on stdout" line, the host prescreen writing a file instead. That review also re-probed the root findings from the committed tree with its own inert fixtures (its hostile-result probe through the committed sandbox returned zero planted literals with 9 counted rejections) and re-ran both evidence scripts, the hardened prescreen over all 38 recorded artifacts, and the self-test. Every gate above is unchanged: this is still not adoption approval, a comparative result, a #39 pre-tuning lock or freeze, blind custody, a benchmark score or any protected-egress evidence, and independent review of this documentation-only head remains required.

Follow-up on 2026-10-02 to that documentation-only head, after an independent review of it returned **REQUEST_CHANGES** with one **MEDIUM blocking finding** and no security, runtime, verdict or evidence-integrity defect (an external report, not committed here; it independently re-verified N1–N7 and the red-phase attribution against source, and confirmed the TypeScript tree untouched). The blocker was in the new README self-test coverage sentence: it enumerated host-prescreen pin refusals as **absent, malformed, mismatched-digest and mismatched-size**, but only three of those four are covered by a committed self-test — `EXPECTED_DIGEST_MALFORMED` is implemented and reachable (`prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py` refuses a syntactically invalid pin before any archive work) and has **no** committed case, so the sentence asserted regression coverage that does not exist. Reproduced here with no candidate code, no network and no sandbox: `grep -n MALFORMED scripts/research/selftest.py` returns nothing; `python3 -m unittest scripts.research.selftest.PrescreenHelperTest` runs 10 cases, none of them a malformed pin; and a bounded local call of `screen_local_artifact` on one inert synthetic wheel returns `EXPECTED_DIGEST_ABSENT` for an absent pin, `EXPECTED_DIGEST_MALFORMED` for `not-a-digest` and for a 63-character digest, `ARTIFACT_DIGEST_MISMATCH` for a well-formed but wrong digest, `ARTIFACT_SIZE_MISMATCH` for a wrong size, and `OK` for a matching pin. The fix is documentation-only, keeping the authorized prose scope and the 55/55 self-test count: **malformed** is removed from the enumerated coverage, and the branch is recorded in the README as an **open coverage gap** rather than claimed as covered — writing that inert case is a test-suite scope change, not taken in a documentation pass, and stays an explicit follow-up gate. (That head's wording also named only that one uncovered branch, which a later independent review found to understate the gap; the corrected set is in the next paragraph.) The same head also corrected three non-blocking precision points: the host prescreen's stdout line is no longer described as absolutely empty (the wrong-argument-count path emits a single `{"outcome": "USAGE"}` line before anything is screened); the dossier's `METADATA: Name:` sentence now records the file-name-derived fallback used when that field is absent or fails the helper's grammar, and separates the file-member count (the 40/40 figure) from the archive-entry count; and the two adjacent paragraphs' rejection counts are now explicitly distinguished (7 from the original author probe, 9 from the independent review's own inert fixture, a different hostile-result fixture, not a contradiction). Two reviewer observations were left as recorded, not silently rewritten: the pre-existing dossier phrase "archive-entry count … recorded in the tables here" predates this work (neither artifact table has an entry-count column; the enumerated seven fields were compared field-by-field against the recorded artifact JSON for the four screened runtime wheels, while for all 38 recorded artifacts the digest, size and `published_matches` were re-hashed) and was not altered here, and the tension between AGENTS.md's one-home rule and the dossier's 55/55 sentence stands, mitigated by the explicit link to this section.

Second follow-up on 2026-10-02, after an independent review of that head returned **REQUEST_CHANGES** with one **MEDIUM blocking finding** (again an external report, not committed here; no security, runtime, verdict or evidence-integrity defect, and everything fixed at the previous two heads independently re-verified). The blocker was the new README coverage-gap paragraph: it opened "**One** host-side refusal branch is implemented but has no committed self-test: `EXPECTED_DIGEST_MALFORMED`", asserting a uniqueness that is false of the code. Verified here by diffing the codes `screen_local_artifact` can return against the codes asserted inside `PrescreenHelperTest`: of its fourteen outcome codes, nine are asserted and **five are not** — `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`, `EXPECTED_DIGEST_MALFORMED`, `MEMBER_TOO_LARGE`, `METADATA_COMPRESSION_RATIO_EXCEEDED` and `METADATA_UNREADABLE`. (`MEMBER_TOO_LARGE` is asserted in `InstallerTest`, but for the **installer's** own member bound, not the prescreen's `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` check.) The `main()`-level fixed codes are unasserted too, because only the offline path is unit-tested: `FILENAME_UNPARSEABLE`, `PUBLISHED_RECORD_UNAVAILABLE`, `OSV_QUERY_FAILED`, `SKIPPED_UNVERIFIED_ARTIFACT` and the CLI's `USAGE`. Bounded inert reproducers here (no candidate code, no network, no sandbox, temp directories only) returned: a missing artifact path → `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`; `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` patched to 16 against a 4 KiB-member wheel → `MEMBER_TOO_LARGE` (the same constant-patching technique the committed oversize test already uses); 200 kB of `A` as `METADATA` in a deflated wheel → `METADATA_COMPRESSION_RATIO_EXCEEDED` (475-byte file on disk); an unparseable wheel name through `main` → `FILENAME_UNPARSEABLE`, reached before any index request; `METADATA_UNREADABLE` was **not** reproduced here, since it needs a genuinely corrupt member and my two bounded attempts at forging one were read successfully, so its reachability is reported as unreproduced rather than claimed; and the three index/OSV codes are unreachable without network, which this self-test deliberately does not use. The fix is again prose-only — the cardinal is corrected, the full set is named, and the two bounds this screen's safety case advertises (per-member size, metadata compression ratio) are marked implemented-but-uncovered — with **no** runtime or test change and the self-test still **55/55**. Practical exposure stays low: the archive is opened only after the digest and size agree with an independently fetched index record, so reaching most of these branches requires a hostile or broken *index* record. The uncovered branches, and the owner decision whether to add the inert cases or permanently document the gap, remain an explicit follow-up gate.

Third follow-up on 2026-10-02, on branch `agentic/candidate-prescreen-bounds` based on the published [PR #112](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/112) head `07dc8d1c93072f212e991a03fe606f61c52539d3` — the helper runtime that had already passed independent review at 55/55. **#112 is not merged and not adopted here**, this worktree's local `origin/main` ref `e6f1a46` contains no `scripts/research/` at all, and **nothing in this commit changes helper, runtime, threshold, pin, resource-limit, parser-policy or TypeScript behaviour**: it adds inert offline synthetic self-tests plus the three prose corrections below. **Eleven cases are committed to `scripts/research/selftest.py`** (55 → 66), all with obviously synthetic fixtures, no candidate code, no network, no sandbox, no secret and no real data. Six in `PrescreenHelperTest`: an unreadable artifact refused with `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`, its record carrying no path, no marker planted in the missing file name, no digest and no size (every non-outcome field `None`); a syntactically malformed expected digest refused with `EXPECTED_DIGEST_MALFORMED` for four shapes (non-hex text, a 63-character digest, an uppercase 64-character digest, a non-string) while an invocation counter proves **zero** archive opens, with a control in the same case showing the counter records one open for a matching pin, so the zero is a measurement rather than a stub that never fires; the maximum individual member bound enforced as `MEMBER_TOO_LARGE` by a small fixture against a patched `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` of 512 — restored in `finally`, asserted restored, and the same wheel accepted again at the production bound; the metadata compression-ratio bound actually refusing **deflated** metadata (256 KiB of one repeated byte, with the fixture itself asserted to be inside `MAX_METADATA_BYTES` and over `MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO`, plus a control where in-bound deflated metadata is accepted and read); a stored metadata member whose recorded CRC no longer matches its bytes refused as `METADATA_UNREADABLE` — real ZIP bytes and a real CRC mismatch, not a forged archive format, with no member or file name in the record; and one **recorded limit rather than a fix**: `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE` covers only the `os.path.getsize` failure, while a download entry that stats but cannot be opened as a regular file (a directory, or a file this process cannot read) escapes `screen_local_artifact`, and `main()`, as `OSError`/`PermissionError` carrying the pathname in the exception text instead of producing a fixed code and a record — a pre-existing defect, left unchanged here because this is a test-only commit, pinned by `test_recorded_limit_a_path_that_stats_but_cannot_be_opened_escapes` so that any future change to it has to update this note. That limitation was corrected at the next head; see the fourth follow-up below, which leaves this paragraph's account of `411fa52` intact. Five in a new `PrescreenMainOfflineTest`, driving `main()`'s remaining fixed codes through an inert `urllib` stand-in that keeps the stdlib's own `parse`/`Request`, counts attempts, and then raises or replays one already-recorded byte body — **never a socket**: `FILENAME_UNPARSEABLE` with zero requests attempted, `PUBLISHED_RECORD_UNAVAILABLE` with exactly one attempted request and no advisory field at all, `ARTIFACT_DIGEST_MISMATCH` from a recorded index record plus `advisories = SKIPPED_UNVERIFIED_ARTIFACT` with the advisory query never attempted, `OK` plus `advisories = OSV_QUERY_FAILED` from a recorded malformed advisory body, and the CLI's `USAGE` line checked through a subprocess (exit 2, exactly `{"outcome": "USAGE"}` on stdout, empty stderr).

The earlier README claim that the index and advisory codes "cannot be covered by this self-test at all, because it is deterministic and offline by design" is **withdrawn as false**: offline stubs reach those paths, and the previous suite simply used none. That claim, and the "not every implemented refusal is covered" paragraph that asserted a count of five uncovered codes by enumerating a partial list, are replaced by a statement of what the committed suite asserts plus an explicit non-exhaustive-coverage disclaimer, so this head claims **no** complete branch coverage. Two prose corrections follow the same rule: the dossier's pre-existing "archive-entry count … recorded in the tables here" now points at the recorded inspection JSON (with the fact that neither artifact table has an entry-count column), leaving its dated evidence, its `METADATA: Name:`/normalized-index-key attribution and its file-member-versus-entry-count distinction intact; and the dossier's closing 55/55 sentence became a dated pointer — the screen run used the earlier 30-test revision, and current count and pass state live only in this section — so the dossier stays a record of what that dated run observed. **Load-bearing evidence, disposable and outside the worktree:** the committed self-test was run against throwaway copies of `scripts/research/` with one refusal guard removed at a time, and each mutation failed exactly the corresponding new case — `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` guard → `test_maximum_individual_member_bound_is_enforced_and_restored`; metadata ratio guard → `test_metadata_compression_ratio_bound_refuses_deflated_metadata`; expected-digest syntax guard → `test_malformed_expected_digest_is_refused_before_any_archive_open` (four sub-case failures, one test); stat-failure guard → `test_unreadable_artifact_yields_a_fixed_code_and_echoes_nothing` (error); metadata read/CRC guard → `test_corrupt_metadata_member_is_reported_as_unreadable` (error). No production threshold was raised, no CPU or memory stress was applied, no candidate code ran, and every mutated copy was deleted; that harness is **not** committed and is not claimed to be replayable from this repository.

Actually run at this head, all offline: `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` → `Ran 66 tests ... OK`; `python3 -m unittest scripts.research.selftest.PrescreenHelperTest` (16 cases) and `...PrescreenMainOfflineTest` (5 cases) individually green; `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0 (the unchanged TypeScript fixture guard); the relative links and heading anchors of the three touched documents checked against the tree; and `git diff --check` clean. **Not** run at this head, because no input to them changed: the TypeScript `npm test` suite (590 pass / 0 fail at the base `2f1ecab`, whose `src/`, `test/`, `evaluations/`, toolchain and package files are byte-identical to this head's), the attestation verifier's crypto, the sandbox smoke, and the 38-artifact prescreen re-run — those results (40/40 installed members, 64 attestation checks, 32 PASS per artifact, the 55/55 self-test and the external review verdicts) belong to their own earlier heads and to external reports that are not committed here, and none of them is re-asserted by this commit. **Limits, stated rather than papered over:** the suite is not a coverage measurement; the live network behaviour of `fetch_published_record`/`query_osv` and their advisory-identifier extraction remain unexercised (only the inert stand-in runs), the CRC control is one synthetic corruption technique rather than a corruption sweep, the `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` case proves the check runs under a patched threshold rather than at the production value, and every sandbox limit above is still per process or per real UID with **no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound**. Independent exact-head review of this commit is required before it is relied on, and decision 010, the taxonomy and multidimensional drafts, the human #46 adoption gate, the #39 owner decisions, #48 selection and the v1 classifier/policy contracts are all exactly as they were.

Fourth follow-up on 2026-10-02 on the same branch `agentic/candidate-prescreen-bounds`, on top of the bounds head `411fa521cdaad3abfa365b9714b567a3f088fc0e`, after an independent review of that exact head returned **APPROVE** with no blocking finding and six remaining gates (an external report, not committed here; it re-ran all 66 cases, reproduced the previous head's mutation claims, and independently confirmed the helper and the whole TypeScript tree byte-identical between `07dc8d1` and `411fa52`). Root authorized the narrow correction that review recorded as its G-2 gate, so this is the **first commit on this branch that changes the helper**: `screen_local_artifact` wraps the single local digest read in `except OSError` and returns the existing typed `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE` record. A download entry that stats and then cannot be opened or read as a regular file -- a directory entry, a file this process may not read, or a read error after the stat -- is therefore a refusal instead of an `OSError`/`PermissionError` carrying the pathname, and `main()` keeps the rest of the batch and writes its report instead of losing it. Nothing else in the helper moves: the artifact size ceiling, the absent/malformed pin refusals, the order of the stat, pin, digest and size checks, the point at which an archive is first opened, every member-count, member-size, metadata-size and compression-ratio bound, the metadata read and CRC behaviour, the fixed-code set, the two network paths and the CLI are all unchanged, and this commit adds **no** dependency, threshold, privilege, aggregate sandbox limit, third-party code, network access or model execution. The refusal records nothing but its code -- no path, file name, exception, message, partial digest or size -- and it is decided before any archive is opened, any member parsed or any advisory query issued. Deliberately **outside** this correction: a failure to write the report file itself, and a download directory that cannot be listed, both remain trusted-administration paths, and the guard is `except OSError` only, so a non-`OSError` (a path containing a NUL byte, unreachable from `os.listdir`) would still raise rather than produce a code. No new PR is opened for it: the existing #112 carries the bounds work and this correction together.

The deliberately pinned limitation case was replaced rather than supplemented: `test_recorded_limit_a_path_that_stats_but_cannot_be_opened_escapes` asserted the escape and is gone, and **five committed cases** now expect the fixed behaviour. Three in `PrescreenHelperTest`: a directory entry refused with `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE` carrying no path, marker, digest or size and opening no archive; a successful stat followed by an injected open failure, with a deliberately wrong pin so the verdict is provably the read and not a digest mismatch; and a read that fails after the first chunk, with a *matching* pin and a real archive behind it, so no digest, size or other field may survive, no archive may open, and the same wheel is then screened to `OK` once nothing fails its read. Two in `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` drive `main()` over a mixed download directory under the offline index/advisory stubs: the entry that cannot be read is a typed refusal whose measured fields are absent rather than null, carrying `advisories = SKIPPED_UNVERIFIED_ARTIFACT`, while the verified entry beside it is unchanged (same observed digest, same `METADATA`-derived fields, `advisories = OSV_QUERY_FAILED`); exactly one archive is opened, exactly one advisory query is issued, both entries are still fetched from the index, and the report bytes contain no path, no file name, no exception class name and no planted marker. The read failures are injected at the helper's own `open`/`read` call by a test-only stand-in that fails named temporary paths and delegates every other path, including `main()`'s report file, because `os.chmod` cannot make a genuinely unreadable fixture for a suite that may run as root; the only mode changes anywhere in the committed suite are `chmod 0700` on temporary directories it created itself, and no file outside them is opened for writing or has its mode or ownership touched. **Red checkpoint, committed and replayable:** `0a23ed7` is tests-only with `prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py` byte-identical to `411fa52`, and at that commit `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` returns `Ran 70 tests` with `FAILED (errors=5)` -- exactly the five new cases and 65 passes; the green head is the commit that follows.

The same review's non-blocking documentation defect is corrected here as well: the third-follow-up block had been inserted above the second-follow-up paragraph it chronologically followed, which left the first follow-up's "the corrected set is in the next paragraph" pointing at the wrong paragraph. The block is moved below it, so the order is First, Second, Third and the pointer resolves. The move itself changed only the order -- every moved paragraph is byte-identical to its text at `411fa52` -- and the one addition inside the third follow-up is the dated cross-reference to this correction, which leaves that paragraph's account of that head intact.

**Load-bearing evidence, disposable and outside the worktree:** the committed self-test was run against throwaway copies of `scripts/research/`, each restored from a pristine copy between runs and deleted afterwards, with one guard changed at a time. A refusal that also recorded a partial digest and size (success-shaped partial) fails 4 of the new cases; a refusal that echoed the exception text into a record fails 5; a refusal deferred until after the archive has been opened and parsed fails 5 new cases and 3 pre-existing ordering cases (6 failures, 2 errors); an advisory query run for an unverified artifact fails the 2 new batch cases and the pre-existing skipped-advisory case; and a read refusal reported as a digest-mismatch verdict fails 5. The unmutated control passes all 25 prescreen cases. Both prescreen classes were additionally re-run with `socket.socket.connect`, `connect_ex`, `socket.create_connection` and `getaddrinfo` replaced by a raising stand-in -- 25 cases, 0 failures -- after separately showing that the same poison does fire on a real `urlopen` attempt, so no committed case reaches the network. No production threshold was raised, no CPU or memory stress was applied, no candidate code ran, and no mutated copy is claimed to be replayable from this repository.

**Counts and what was actually run at this head, all offline:** `selftest.py` is **70 tests** (66 → 70: five added, the pinned limitation removed), with `PrescreenHelperTest` at 18 cases and `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` at 7. `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` → `Ran 70 tests ... OK`; both prescreen classes run individually green at those counts; `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0 (the unchanged TypeScript fixture guard); the relative links and heading anchors of the touched documents checked against the tree; and `git diff --check` clean. **Not** run at this head, because no input to them changed: the TypeScript `npm test` suite (590 pass / 0 fail at the base `2f1ecab`, whose `src/`, `test/`, `evaluations/`, toolchain and package files are byte-identical to this head), the attestation verifier's crypto, the sandbox smoke, and the 38-artifact prescreen re-run -- those results belong to their own earlier heads and to external reports that are not committed here, and none of them is re-asserted by this commit. **Limits, stated rather than papered over:** the suite is still not a coverage measurement; the live network behaviour of `fetch_published_record`/`query_osv` and their advisory-identifier extraction remain unexercised; the read-failure cases inject at the helper's `open`/`read`, so they assert the helper's decision under a real `OSError`, not its behaviour on a specific filesystem, device or transport; a file removed between the stat and the open is handled by the same guard but is not separately exercised; `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` remains proven under a patched threshold rather than at the production value, the CRC control remains one corruption technique, and every sandbox limit above is still per process or per real UID with **no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound**. The README now states this fixed behaviour, names these cases and keeps the pre-existing gap as dated history; the dated dossier needed no change, because it describes one 2026-10-01 run and already points here for current scope. Independent exact-head review of this correction is required before it is relied on. **#112 is still not merged and not adopted**, and decision 010, the taxonomy and multidimensional drafts, the human #46 adoption gate, the #39 owner decisions, #48 selection and the v1 classifier/policy contracts are all exactly as they were.

Fifth follow-up on 2026-10-02 on the same branch `agentic/candidate-prescreen-bounds`, on top of the unreadable-artifact correction `7136c9af48b484a0e43b544cbb668ac4eaf046bd`, after an independent review of that exact head returned **APPROVE** with **no blocking finding** and four non-blocking findings, whose remaining gates were G-1 through G-7 (an external report, not committed here; it re-ran all 70 cases, re-probed the earlier heads, confirmed the whole TypeScript tree byte-identical between `2f1ecab` and `7136c9a`, and confirmed the helper byte-identical between `411fa52` and `0a23ed7`, the bounds head and its tests-only red checkpoint -- that is the helper range it actually compared: the helper is unchanged `07dc8d1` to `411fa52` and changes again at `7136c9a`, which is the read-error guard this paragraph's subject is built on, so no comparison of the helper across `07dc8d1` to `7136c9a` is claimed). Root authorized the narrow correction that review recorded as its **G-2** gate. **That gate was the concrete defect fixed here:** a FIFO, or any other non-regular entry named `*.whl` in a download directory, blocked in `open()` indefinitely -- opening a FIFO read-only waits for a writer that may never arrive, that wait is not an exception, so the committed `except OSError` guard never saw it; the reviewer's own bounded probe reproduced the block at both the bounds head and the corrected head, so it was pre-existing and untouched by `7136c9a`, and `main()` over such a directory would have lost the whole batch's report as well. `screen_local_artifact` now answers one narrow question before it reads anything -- **is this an ordinary regular file?** -- by opening the entry with `O_NONBLOCK`, asking the *opened descriptor* rather than the pathname (which a symlink or a replacement would misreport) whether it is a regular file, reading no byte, keeping no descriptor and polling nothing; anything else gets the existing typed `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`, which covers a FIFO, a directory, a socket file, a device, an entry this process may not open, and a symlink judged by its target. The refusal is decided before any archive is opened, any member parsed or any advisory query issued, and records nothing but its code. The review's other new gate, **G-1** (its LOW finding that the README row's blanket "Every local read failure is a fixed code" is falsified by two reachable paths), is corrected in the same pass: that sentence is now scoped to downloaded-artifact stat, digest read and archive read **inside the screen**, and names listing the download directory and writing the report file as the separate trusted-administration paths the same document already listed. Nothing else in the helper moves: the artifact size ceiling, the absent and malformed pin refusals and their precedence, the order stat -> pin -> non-regular check -> digest -> digest compare -> size compare -> archive, every member-count, member-size, metadata-size and metadata-compression-ratio bound, the metadata read and CRC behaviour, the fixed-code set, the two network paths, the CLI and the report format are unchanged, and this head adds **no** dependency, threshold, privilege, aggregate sandbox limit, third-party code, network access or model execution. **Stated rather than claimed:** this is a type check, not a preemption of regular filesystem I/O. It opens the entry and closes it again, and the digest is still read by reopening the path, with the archive then opened by path a third time -- so an entry replaced in between is caught only by the digest comparison, which is the pre-existing digest-then-reopen limit (that review's G-3), unchanged and still recorded. The check narrows which entries may start a read; it does not bind the bytes that are read, and it says nothing about a trusted download directory being trustworthy. This screen remains a supply-chain hygiene screen and is not provenance verification.

**Red checkpoint, committed and replayable:** `ddc7f4f` is tests-only with `prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py` byte-identical to `7136c9a`, and from a disposable `git archive` copy of it under an external `timeout --signal=KILL`, `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` returns `Ran 74 tests` with `FAILED (failures=5)` in 21.4 s -- the four new cases (the non-regular control case reports two subtest failures) and **all 70 previously committed cases passing**. The failures are exactly the defect: a FIFO that was waited on (`AssertionError: the screen waited on a non-regular entry instead of refusing it`), `main()` over a download directory that really holds one, and the character device, which the old code accepted as a digest comparison (`'ARTIFACT_DIGEST_MISMATCH' != 'ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE'`). The green head is the commit that follows.

**Four committed cases, all inert, offline and obviously synthetic (70 -> 74):** a real FIFO with no writer, refused under a wall-clock deadline with `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE`, no archive opened and no path, file name, exception or marker in the record; a real FIFO whose inert payload is already buffered, refused **without consuming those bytes** -- what is still in the pipe afterwards is the measurement, not an assumption; one case over the non-regular controls (a directory, a local pathname socket file, a character device and a FIFO), each refused eight times so that a single unclosed descriptor per refusal would show up as a descriptor count that grew, counted from `/proc/self/fd` and skipped rather than guessed where that does not exist; and `main()` over a download directory that really holds a FIFO named like a wheel, under the same deadline, where the entry is a typed refusal with no measured field and `advisories = SKIPPED_UNVERIFIED_ARTIFACT`, the verified wheel beside it keeps its digest, size, member count, `METADATA`-derived name and `OSV_QUERY_FAILED`, and the batch costs one archive open, one advisory query and two index fetches. Three are in `PrescreenHelperTest` and one in `PrescreenMainOfflineTest`. Each such call is bounded by one wall-clock deadline -- POSIX `SIGALRM` through `setitimer`, no thread, no child process, no polling loop, no orphan, with the timer cancelled and the previous handler restored in every case -- because an unreadable artifact is refused by *returning*, so "it returns instead of waiting" is a claim a test has to be able to falsify. Every fixture is created inside the suite's own temporary directory, is removed with it, holds at most a few obviously synthetic bytes, needs no privilege and touches no real user file; the device control is the platform's null device, opened read-only with no byte read from it, because `mknod` needs a privilege this suite deliberately does not take.

**Load-bearing evidence, disposable and outside the worktree:** the committed suite was run against throwaway copies of `scripts/research/`, each restored from a pristine copy between runs and deleted afterwards, with one thing changed at a time. Disabling the check fails **5** -- both FIFO cases, the character-device and FIFO controls, and the batch. Removing the descriptor `close` fails the **3** control subtests that allocate one (the socket entry never allocates any, which is exactly why it still passes). Making the check read a single byte fails **1**, the case that asserts the payload was not consumed. Giving the refusal a success-shaped `observed_sha256`/`observed_size` fails **9**, including the two pre-existing directory refusals. Moving the check to after the digest read fails the same **5**; the claim recorded at this head that this relocation *also* errors the **3** pre-existing read-failure cases is **withdrawn**, because a move leaves the `except OSError` around `digest_and_size` in place and a genuine read error still becomes a fixed code. An independent review of this exact head replayed the relocation as a single change and got `FAILED (failures=5)` with **0 errors**. Turning a read error into a re-raise takes a second change -- deleting that guard as well -- which is neither made nor claimed here, and the discarded tree behind the withdrawn number is not committed, so nothing is asserted about why it differed. The same review reproduced the other four mutations of this paragraph exactly. The unmutated control passes all 29 prescreen cases, and both classes were re-run with `socket.socket.connect`, `connect_ex`, `socket.create_connection` and `getaddrinfo` replaced by a raising stand-in -- 29 cases, 0 failures, poison fired 0 times -- after separately showing that the same poison does fire on a live `urlopen` attempt. No production threshold was raised, no CPU or memory stress was applied, no candidate code ran, no orphan process was left, and no mutated copy is claimed to be replayable from this repository.

**Counts and what was actually run at this head, all offline:** `selftest.py` is **74 tests** (70 -> 74: four added, none removed or replaced), with `PrescreenHelperTest` at 21 cases and `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` at 8. `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` -> `Ran 74 tests ... OK` in 1.35 s; both prescreen classes run individually green at those counts (`Ran 29 tests ... OK`); `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0 (the unchanged TypeScript fixture guard); the relative links and heading anchors of the two touched documents checked against the tree; and `git diff --check` clean. **Not** run at this head, because no input to them changed: the TypeScript `npm test` suite (590 pass / 0 fail at the base `2f1ecab`, whose `src/`, `test/`, `evaluations/`, toolchain and package files are byte-identical to this head), the attestation verifier's crypto, the sandbox smoke, and the 38-artifact prescreen re-run -- those results belong to their own earlier heads and to external reports that are not committed here, and none of them is re-asserted by this head. The 70, 66 and 55 counts, the earlier mutation and red-checkpoint results and the external verdicts quoted above stay attributed to the heads and reports they came from; they are not this head's result. **Limits, stated rather than papered over:** the suite is still not a coverage measurement; the live network behaviour of `fetch_published_record`/`query_osv` and their advisory-identifier extraction remain unexercised; the window between the non-regular check, the digest read and the archive open is **not** closed, so this head does not claim to preempt regular-file I/O and a swapped entry remains a trusted-filesystem limitation rather than a detected substitution; `mknod`-shaped device control is out of reach for a suite that takes no privilege, so the character-device control is the platform's null device; the deadline needs POSIX `setitimer` and the descriptor count needs Linux `/proc/self/fd`, and each is skipped rather than approximated elsewhere; a file removed between the stat and the open is handled by the same code but is still not separately exercised; `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` remains proven under a patched threshold rather than at the production value, the CRC control remains one corruption technique, and every sandbox limit above is still per process or per real UID with **no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound**. The README states this fixed behaviour, names the cases and the fixture limits and keeps the earlier gaps as dated history; the dated dossier needed no change, because it describes one 2026-10-01 run and already points here for current scope.
