Sixth follow-up on 2026-10-02 on the same branch `agentic/candidate-prescreen-bounds`, documentation only, on top of the non-regular-entry correction `a2aa9d1621bb1321571df3352460b50b9ce34c09`, after an independent review of that exact head returned **APPROVE** with **no blocking finding** and three LOW, non-blocking documentation-accuracy findings, D-1 to D-3, recorded as gates G-1 to G-3 (an external report, not committed here; it re-ran all 74 cases, replayed the red checkpoint, probed base and head, reproduced five disposable mutations and checked scope). **Nothing in this commit changes behaviour:** `prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py`, `selftest.py`, every threshold, the fixed-code set, the dependency set, the TypeScript tree, the dated dossier and the decision records are untouched, so `selftest.py` is still the same 74-case file with the same case names as at `a2aa9d1`. Each correction withdraws or narrows a claim rather than adding one.

- **D-1, the fifth follow-up's evidence attribution.** That paragraph credited the cited review with confirming "the helper and the whole TypeScript tree byte-identical between `07dc8d1` and `7136c9a`". The helper is **not** byte-identical across that range -- `7136c9a` is the very commit that added the `try/except OSError` around the digest read, and `git diff --stat` reports 16 insertions and 2 deletions -- and the cited report's own comparisons were the TypeScript tree `2f1ecab` ↔ `7136c9a` and the helper `411fa52` ↔ `0a23ed7`; the `07dc8d1` ↔ `411fa52` helper comparison belongs to the earlier bounds review. The sentence now names only the comparisons that report recorded, and no review is credited with a range it did not check. The underlying facts were never in doubt and are re-stated here as ranges verified at this documentation head rather than as an attributed review result: the helper is byte-identical `07dc8d1` ↔ `411fa52`, byte-identical `7136c9a` ↔ `ddc7f4f`, and the TypeScript tree (`src/`, `test/`, `evaluations/`, toolchain and package files) is byte-identical `2f1ecab` ↔ `7136c9a` and `2f1ecab` ↔ `a2aa9d1`.
- **D-2, the same paragraph's mutation record.** Withdrawn in the correction above and not reproduced at this documentation head: the review replayed the relocation of the non-regular check past the digest read as a **single** change and got `FAILED (failures=5)` with **0 errors**, so the pre-existing read-failure cases still pass and the claimed **3** errors do not. Re-raising a genuine read error requires deleting the `except OSError` guard around `digest_and_size` **as well**, a second change; it was not made, is not claimed, and no reason for the withdrawn number is invented -- the discarded tree behind it is uncommitted and its exact state is not asserted. The same review reproduced the other four mutations of that paragraph exactly (5, 3, 1 and 9 failures) and its unmutated control green, so only this one sub-claim is affected.
- **D-3, `scripts/research/README.md`'s refusal inventory.** The sentence "**Nine** committed cases assert the unreadable-artifact refusal: **five** in `PrescreenHelperTest` ... and **two** in `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` ... plus the stat-failure case above" was internally inconsistent (its own enumeration lists six helper cases, not five), omitted the `main()` batch whose unreadable entry is an injected read error from the main-class list, and repeated a status count that has one home. It is replaced by a behavioural inventory with **no** total: in `PrescreenHelperTest` a failed `getsize` stat, a directory entry, a stat followed by a failing open, a read that fails after the first chunk (the same wheel is then screened normally once nothing fails it), a FIFO with no writer, a FIFO whose buffered bytes must survive the refusal, and the non-regular controls with their descriptor counts; in `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` three `main()` runs over a mixed download directory whose unreadable entry is a directory, an injected read error and a real FIFO respectively, each leaving the verified sibling entry unchanged. Those are the 7 and 3 committed cases that assert `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE` in their respective classes, checked here against the named test definitions rather than against the prose. For the same reason the same paragraph's earlier sentence about the `main()` batch -- which said the batch ran "once with that entry a directory and once with it a real FIFO" -- now also names the injected-read-failure batch and attaches the deadline to the FIFO case, which is the only one of the three that runs under one; otherwise the two sentences would have contradicted each other and the new inventory. **No test was changed to fit the prose**, the non-exhaustive-coverage disclaimer is untouched, no branch is claimed covered, and the nearby sentence that says four cases carry the non-regular check now says they are the four that correction added, so it cannot be read as a second total.

**What was actually run at this documentation head, all offline:** the byte-identity ranges named above (`git diff --stat`, each empty except the helper range `07dc8d1` ↔ `7136c9a` at 16 insertions / 2 deletions); the case-name and class-membership extraction behind the D-3 inventory, which puts 7 `ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE` committed cases in `PrescreenHelperTest` and 3 committed cases in `PrescreenMainOfflineTest`; `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0 (the unchanged TypeScript fixture guard); the relative links and heading anchors of the two touched documents resolved against the tree; and `git diff --check` clean with a clean commit. **Deliberately not re-run, and not re-asserted:** the self-test and its two prescreen classes, because no test, helper or fixture byte changed in this commit and their results belong to `a2aa9d1` and to the reports above; likewise the TypeScript `npm test` suite (590 pass / 0 fail at the base `2f1ecab`, whose tree is byte-identical to `7136c9a`), the attestation verifier's crypto, the sandbox smoke and the 38-artifact prescreen re-run, which remain owed by their own heads. The 74, 29, 21 and 8 figures and the red checkpoint's `failures=5` stay the results of the heads that measured them. **No limit is relaxed:** the window between the non-regular check, the digest read and the archive open is still **not** closed and a swapped entry is still a trusted-filesystem limitation rather than a detected substitution; `except OSError` is still the only exception class the digest guard catches, so an embedded-NUL `ValueError` would still raise; listing the download directory and writing the report file are still trusted-administration paths; the suite is still not a coverage measurement; the live `fetch_published_record`/`query_osv` paths and their advisory-identifier extraction are still unexercised; `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` is still proven under a patched threshold rather than at the production value; the CRC control is still one corruption technique; the new fixtures are still Linux/POSIX-specific by design, with the descriptor count skipped rather than approximated where `/proc/self/fd` is absent and `mknod`-shaped device control still out of reach without privilege; and every sandbox limit above is still per process or per real UID with **no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound**. Independent exact-head review of this documentation-only commit is required before it is relied on. **#112 is still not merged and not adopted**, and decision 010, the taxonomy and multidimensional drafts, the human #46 adoption gate, the #39 owner decisions, #48 selection and the v1 classifier/policy contracts are all exactly as they were.

**Issue #123, the host-side wheel screen identity defect, fixed on 2026-10-02 as its own scoped change on base `e2b5714eca6df3b1cb0847b0fa9889bb0d71c625`** (research-only: `scripts/research/prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py`, its committed self-tests, the narrow prose correction of [`scripts/research/README.md`](../scripts/research/README.md) described at the end of this paragraph, and this paragraph; the TypeScript tree, the runtime verifier, the execution manifest, the trial scripts and every published research document are untouched). **The defect:** `screen_local_artifact` selected its metadata member by taking the **last** archive member whose name ended in `.dist-info/METADATA`, so a wheel carrying a *vendored* distribution's metadata — vendoring puts another distribution's `.dist-info` below the outer package directory — reported whatever came last. All 51 recorded manifest digests are present and correct; the one wrong **name** is the setuptools 84.0.0 wheel recorded as zipp 3.23.0, because setuptools' vendored `zipp` metadata is nested and came last. Nothing else about that record is affected, and no digest, size or advisory observation changes. Two further properties of the same code are corrected here because they are the same defect seen from the other side: the declared `Name`/`Version` were taken as the **first** match of their field with no check for a repeated field, and a malformed or absent one silently fell back in `main()` to the spelling parsed from the file name, so a wheel with no usable identity of its own still produced a named record. **The fix, in order, all inside the existing bounds:** the candidate metadata member is now selected by shape — a member whose name is exactly one directory level deep and ends in `.dist-info/METADATA` — never by position, so a nested vendored copy is not a candidate; zero candidates is the existing `METADATA_ABSENT`, more than one (including one member name written twice, a real ZIP shape) is the new `DIST_INFO_METADATA_AMBIGUOUS`, and a single candidate whose `{name}-{version}` directory stem does not parse is `DISTRIBUTION_IDENTITY_INVALID`; the declared identity must then be exactly one in-grammar `Name` and one in-grammar `Version`, an absent, repeated or out-of-grammar field being `DISTRIBUTION_IDENTITY_INVALID` rather than a value to be picked; that declared identity must agree with the archive's own root `.dist-info` stem (`DISTRIBUTION_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` otherwise) **and** with the identity the index publishes for the same version, which `screen_local_artifact` now takes as two required arguments beside the digest and size (`DISTRIBUTION_IDENTITY_UNVERIFIED` otherwise). Normalization is deliberate, minimal and stdlib-only — every run of `-`, `_` and `.` folds to one `-` and the name is lowercased (PEP 503) — and is applied only to compare two grammar-checked names, never to rewrite one into a record; versions are compared as recorded strings, with no PEP 440 parsing. **The advisory service is asked about the published identity, not the artifact's:** `main()` queries with the index's own name and version, and only for an `OK` outcome, so every identity refusal costs zero queries and reports `advisories: SKIPPED_UNVERIFIED_ARTIFACT`. The file name now drives only the index request (normalized once) and contributes no identity to any record. `fetch_published_record` additionally refuses a record that publishes no in-grammar `info.name`/`info.version`, names no entry for that exact file, or reports a byte count that is not an integer (`PUBLISHED_RECORD_UNAVAILABLE`), and reports a non-string digest as the absent pin the screen already has a fixed code for; that is the one adjacent change, and it removes a path where a non-numeric `size` reached `int()` and raised out of `main()`, losing the whole batch's report. **Unchanged:** the artifact size ceiling, the absent/malformed pin refusals and their precedence, the order stat -> pin -> non-regular-file check -> digest -> digest compare -> size compare -> archive, the digest/size-before-archive property, the member-count, per-member, metadata-size and compression-ratio bounds, the metadata read and CRC behaviour, `has_native_code`, the regular-file/FIFO refusal, the CLI, the report format, the HTTP bounds and the two network paths. This is still a supply-chain hygiene screen and still **not** provenance verification, an authenticated publisher conclusion or a bound on a swapped download entry.

**Evidence for #123, with counts and honest limits (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, Python 3.14.4, Linux x86_64; no push, PR, remote CI, approval or human gate, and no third-party package executed, installed or re-screened here).** Red checkpoint committed and replayable: `840a361` is tests-only with `prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py` byte-identical to `e2b5714`, and at that commit `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` returns **`Ran 96 tests` / `FAILED (failures=33, errors=9)`** — all 74 pre-existing cases pass, and the 33 failures and 9 errors are the 22 new cases only. The failures are the defect itself: a vendored record written last reported `SYNTHETIC-PLANTED-NOT-A-REAL-VALUE-9f3a` instead of `synthetic-sample` through the helper and drove the advisory query with it through `main()`, and four member orders gave two different identities — the vendored one wherever the vendored member was written last — and a vendored-only, two-root, mismatched or malformed-identity wheel returned `OK`. The 9 errors are the two cases that bind a *specific* published identity and therefore call the new required signature, which does not exist at the base. The green head is the commit that follows. `selftest.py` is now **96 tests** (74 -> 96: 22 added, none removed; one of them was renamed between the red checkpoint and this head, `test_a_published_record_with_an_unusable_digest_or_size_is_unavailable` at `840a361` to `test_a_published_record_that_cannot_be_screened_is_refused` here, because its sub-cases changed from three to five — so a reviewer replaying the red checkpoint by case **name** finds that one name missing and the same case under the new name), with `PrescreenHelperTest` at **36** and `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` at **15**; `python3 scripts/research/selftest.py` -> `Ran 96 tests ... OK`, and both classes run individually green (`Ran 51 tests ... OK`). **Fifteen** new helper-level cases cover the vendored record written last, four member orders, two root metadata members, one root member written twice, vendored-only metadata, root name and root version disagreement, repeated and out-of-grammar identity fields, a missing identity field, a `.dist-info` stem with no version, disagreement with the published record (other name, other version, and a published name or version outside the grammar, each with a control that the same wheel is `OK` against the identity the record really publishes), one distribution spelled three ways, metadata field order with a description line that looks like a header, and a member that merely ends in the metadata suffix. **Seven** new `main()` cases drive orchestration end to end through the inert offline stubs with an index-URL and advisory-identity spy: the correct identity observed by the query, a query never made for an identity the index does not publish, a record without a published identity, five record shapes that cannot be screened, five refused outer identities that cost zero queries, no file-name fallback, and the normalized index request URL. **Load-bearing evidence, disposable and outside the worktree, with the recipe for every figure so each is replayable:** the committed suite was run against throwaway copies of `scripts/research/`, **one contiguous string replacement in `prescreen_pypi_artifacts.py` per run**, each copy restored from a pristine tree between runs and deleted afterwards, each run being `python3 -m unittest scripts.research.selftest.PrescreenHelperTest scripts.research.selftest.PrescreenMainOfflineTest` (51 cases). Recipe and result: replacing the `roots`-selection block (`roots = []` ... `metadata_info, stem = roots[0]`) with one that also accepts any `.dist-info/METADATA` at **any depth** — inside the `for info in infos:` loop, `if stem is None and info.filename.endswith(DIST_INFO_METADATA_SUFFIX): stem = info.filename[:-len("/METADATA")]` with `.dist-info` stripped — and selects `roots[-1]` instead of `roots[0]`, i.e. the base's last-wins selection with the stem-agreement and published-identity checks left in place -> `FAILED (failures=10)`, 7 distinct cases (the vendored-identity cases, the member-order case, the vendored-only case and both ambiguity cases); `if len(roots) > 1:` ... -> `roots = roots[:1]` -> `failures=3`, 3 cases (both ambiguity cases and the `main()` batch); `or stem_identity[1] != version):` -> `or False):` -> `failures=1`, 1 case (the version disagreement); the two published-identity comparison lines -> `or False\n or False):` -> `failures=5`, 3 cases (the published-disagreement case with its four sub-cases, the index-does-not-publish case and the three-spellings case); appending the base's two `record[...] = record[...] or _grammar(file_name/file_version, ...)` lines after the `screen_local_artifact` call in `main()` -> `failures=9`, 4 cases (the no-fallback case, the refused-identity batch, the index-does-not-publish case and the cannot-be-screened case); `if len(names) != 1 or len(versions) != 1:` -> `if not names or not versions:` -> `failures=1`, 1 case (the repeated identity field); `if member_name.count("/") != 1 or not member_name.endswith(...):` -> dropping the depth condition -> `failures=9`, 5 cases (the vendored-identity cases, the member-order case, the vendored-only case and the refused-identity batch); the published-name comparison -> `name != expected_name` -> `failures=2`, 1 case (the three-spellings case); `if (normalize_distribution_name(stem_identity[0]) != normalize_distribution_name(name)` -> `if (stem_identity[0] != name` -> `failures=28`, 16 cases. `failures=N` is the runner's own count and counts sub-tests; the distinct-case count is given beside it because several of these cases carry sub-tests. **Two earlier figures from this paragraph are withdrawn as unfaithful mutants, not as failed properties:** an earlier run recorded `19` for the last-wins selection and `10` for the file-name fallback. The first mutant substituted a stem expression that was not the `{name}-{version}` stem, so it also broke the root-stem agreement and failed for a second, unrelated reason; the second replaced the whole screen with a synthetic `OK` record and so never screened the artifact at all. Both were re-measured with the faithful recipes above, whose last-wins result (**10**) is also the figure the first independent review of this change reported for its own re-implementation of that property — a **different mutant**, which kept the depth filter and the ambiguity guard and only selected `roots[-1]`, so the agreement is corroboration of the withdrawn `19`, not the same experiment run twice. The harness itself is **not** committed and is not claimed replayable from this repository; the recipes are. Both prescreen classes were re-run with `socket.socket.connect`, `connect_ex`, `create_connection`, `getaddrinfo` and `send` replaced by a raising stand-in: **51 cases, 0 failures**, after separately showing the same poison fires on a real `urlopen` attempt. Also run at the code head this paragraph describes, all offline: `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund` (1 package, 0 vulnerabilities), `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0; `npm test` **855/855** pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (run once, and once more under `flock /tmp/hylja-agentic-campaign/test.lock` for the shared-run discipline — the TypeScript tree is byte-identical to the base, so that count is a regression guard and not a result of this change); `git diff --check` clean. **Deliberately not run and not re-asserted:** `npm run test:coverage`, `npm run audit:deps` and `npm run sbom` (no dependency, manifest or TypeScript byte changed), the attestation verifier's crypto, the sandbox smoke, and the 51-wheel and 38-artifact prescreen re-runs — the recorded `51/51` screen, its latencies and the manifest's per-artifact rows remain **author-attributed** measurements owned by the #40 author, and the recorded `zipp 3.23.0` name in `evaluations/presidio-worker/manifest.json` is **not** corrected by this commit, because that manifest and every published research document are outside its scope. **Stated limits, not covered:** the live `fetch_published_record`/`query_osv` behaviour and the advisory-identifier extraction are still exercised only through the inert stand-in, never a socket; PEP 440 is not implemented, so a published version whose recorded spelling differs from the artifact's would be refused rather than equated (fail-closed, and unmeasured against real index records); a `size` that is not an integer is refused at the fetch, but a **direct** call to `screen_local_artifact` with a non-numeric `expected_size` would still raise in `int()`, the only in-repo caller being the validated one; the suite is still not a coverage measurement; `MAX_MEMBER_BYTES` is still proven under a patched threshold rather than at the production value; the CRC control is still one corruption technique; the new fixtures remain Linux/POSIX-specific by design; and every sandbox limit above is still per process or per real UID with **no aggregate memory/CPU/process bound**. `scripts/research/README.md` describes the prescreen helper's behaviour; its table row and self-test-coverage sentence were silent on root-only identity selection and on the published-identity binding, so both now say that the identity is read from the one root `METADATA`, bound to the identity the published record publishes, and that a nested vendored `.dist-info` can neither replace nor query as the outer distribution — a prose-only correction of a usage/contract claim, with no status, no count and no new evidence duplicated there, and with its behavioural inventory still deferring the total to this section. **Documentation-only correction of this paragraph, same code head:** a first independent review of the code head returned **REQUEST_CHANGES** with one **MEDIUM** blocking evidence-integrity finding and no privacy, data-loss, security-boundary or runtime defect: three counts here were false of the committed suite (`PrescreenHelperTest` and `PrescreenMainOfflineTest` were written as 34 and 17 and are **36** and **15**; the new helper-level cases as nine and are **fifteen**; the new `main()` cases as eight and are **seven**), and the mutation figures carried no recorded recipe, so the one an independent re-implementation could match was not the one the harness had produced. Re-deriving those counts from the committed tree instead of from the prose also found three more integers of my own that sentence had wrong — "four refused outer identities" where the case declares five, "four member orders gave three different identities" where the base selection gives two, and "22 added, none removed or replaced" where one case was renamed — all corrected above. A second independent review of that correction found one further integer wrong **in the correction itself**: the rename rationale read "its sub-cases changed from two to five" where the red body declares three and the head declares five, and it is now three to five. That second round cleared every substantive item and reproduced four of the nine recorded recipes exactly, including the last-wins one above. **Every figure in this paragraph was then re-derived from the committed tree rather than from the prose**, and re-deriving it is what found the three extra integers of my own: the per-class counts by running each class; every case total by counting the test definitions per class and comparing that with the runner's own `Ran N tests`; the added, removed and renamed case names by comparing those definitions at `e2b5714`, `840a361` and the code head; every sub-case enumeration, **including the red checkpoint's own**, by counting the tuple each body declares; the four-order red behaviour by driving the base selection over the four orders directly (**two** distinct identities, the vendored one wherever the vendored member is written last); and the red attribution by listing the failing case names from a fresh `git archive 840a361` extraction — **19** distinct, none of them pre-existing, the 9 errors being two of them. **No review of this head is claimed or implied**, root owns publication, merge and issue closure. **This fix alone verifies nothing about #113's runtime, closes nothing in #46 or #40, and is not adoption approval, a comparative result, a #39 lock or freeze, blind custody, a benchmark score, a release gate or any protected-egress evidence.** Independent exact-head review of this change is required before it is relied on, and root owns publication, merge and issue closure; decision 010, the taxonomy and multidimensional drafts, the human #46 adoption gate, the #39 owner decisions, #48 selection and the v1 classifier/policy contracts are all exactly as they were.

Local #1 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 11.15.0): the red command `node --test test/*.test.mjs` failed with 0/6 passing before the runner/guard existed; its deliberately throwing runtime probe was ignored by the old typecheck-only `npm test` (which exited 0 with dependencies installed). After implementation, locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, and `npm run test:coverage` passed; disposable synthetic negative cases reject force-added ignored private fixture paths, tracked credential-like fixture blobs, generated artifacts, binary/symlinked fixtures, and guard execution from a subdirectory. The designated synthetic golden fixture is accepted. The Node test runner reports line/branch coverage for this small toolchain and compiled module, not coverage of future enforcement behavior. `npm run audit:deps` reported zero known vulnerabilities locally; `npm run --silent sbom` produced parseable CycloneDX JSON. CI is configured to run these gates from the lockfile without installing lifecycle scripts or persisting SBOM artifacts; both [#1 PR #51 check run 36134297661](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36134297661) and [run 36134323695](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36134323695) completed successfully.

Threat coverage for #1 is limited to supply-chain hygiene (locked integrity-pinned dependency, local audit/SBOM, pinned CI actions) and accidental tracked-fixture/CI-output disclosure (Git-index guard, ignored private paths, no planted value in guard output). New surfaces are npm installation, the Git-backed guard and test runner, and CI network/runner output. The guard uses bounded deterministic patterns, **not** exhaustive secret/PII detection; encoded values, contextual identifiers, and non-fixture files still need human review and future controls. No customer/production data or semantic/policy behavior is involved, so downstream utility, model latency/cost, held-out and shadow comparisons are not applicable to this toolchain slice.

Local #33 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, on `522430e`; no remote CI, push or PR): a bounded, **offline, NON-ENFORCING** release-evidence and promotion gate exists in [`src/release-integrity.ts`](../src/release-integrity.ts) with [`src/canonical-json.ts`](../src/canonical-json.ts), [`src/byte-encoding.ts`](../src/byte-encoding.ts), [`src/dependency-evidence.ts`](../src/dependency-evidence.ts), [`src/adapter-conformance.ts`](../src/adapter-conformance.ts) and the `node dist/release-gate-cli.js <evidence.json> <expectation.json> <subject-dir>` boundary; exact invocation, evidence formats, trust boundaries and limits are in [the release integrity contract](contracts/release-integrity-contract.md). The trusted signing keys, expected release version and source commit, artifact name, release-channel scope/profile, required conformance suite digests, trusted scanner identities, blocking severity, clock and maximum age come from the release owner's expectation and **never** from the evidence document, a manifest field or a caller asserting that something was tested. The untrusted side (canonical RFC 8785-style evidence, RFC 8032 Ed25519 signature, artifact/lockfile/SBOM/conformance/scanner bytes) is size-bounded, strictly allowlisted, digest-bound and signed before any decision; missing, malformed, non-canonical, mismatched or critical evidence denies with a sorted fixed code list that never contains a path, digest, package name, key id, byte or exception message. A recomputed digest binds the exact tested bytes to the signed claims and **is not publisher provenance**: signer honesty, build reproducibility, an attested build platform and keyless attestation remain owner gates, and no signing secret, registry, publish step, promote workflow or repository write permission was added.

The initial compileable red checkpoint `60ca710` (denying stubs) failed 47 of 49 groups, with the two that passed doing so vacuously on the stub. The first green head `270ddc4` then passed a clean local `npm test` (450/450) and was **independently reviewed and blocked** with four concrete defects, all reproduced against that exact head. The compileable red checkpoint `d185e47` re-established them: over the three #33 files, 53 groups pass and **6 fail** — B1, a recoverable base64/hex encoding of a planted original graded `plantedEgress: PASS`/`overall: PASS` and promoted, while the repository's own #19 sentinel blocks the same bytes; B2, bounded work at 64,625 ms for 8 records x 64 KiB x 8 needles, scaling 4x per dimension from a literal byte search; B3, an adapter that shows clean bytes to the pre-send control and releases different bytes passing completely; B4, a malformed `sha512-` pin and a well-formed `sha256-` pin each silently skipping the SBOM content cross-check. The corrections reuse in-tree standards rather than adding one: the harness's injected pre-send control is now a real `checkEgress` final-byte check over a harness-owned known-originals registry, its ALLOW hands back the private copy of exactly the bytes it cleared and the harness requires every captured record to be that copy, the harness re-checks every captured record and returned field independently, and each case reports the invariant it violated from a fixed vocabulary. The measured cost of the same adversarial volume is 1,916 ms in isolation and about 4,000 ms inside the suite, and inspection is additionally capped at 4 MiB per run, 1 MiB per record, 16 records per case and 64 cases. An integrity pin is now validated at parse time (`LOCKFILE_INTEGRITY_INVALID` for a malformed or unsupported value) and a `sha256`/`sha384` pin, which cannot be cross-checked against the SBOM's SHA-512, is reported as `DEPENDENCY_CONTENT_ALGORITHM_UNSUPPORTED` instead of skipped; the gate also reads what it already parsed, so a re-signed attestation whose cases are all `FAIL` with `overall: 'PASS'` is now `CONFORMANCE_CASE_NOT_PASSING`, a reported violation is `CONFORMANCE_VIOLATION_REPORTED`, and a foreign harness version is `CONFORMANCE_VERSION_MISMATCH`. An independent post-fix probe of all four findings plus five controls now reports every finding closed with the compliant double still verifying and the bypass double still denied, and the offline demonstration grew to 17 cases.

The synthetic adapter harness still proves the issue's adversarial acceptance case directly: a bypass adapter that keeps ordinary functional correctness (`functionalTests: PASS`) while skipping the pre-send check, leaking a planted synthetic original, releasing an opaque part it declared unsupported and following an unobserved redirect is reported `plantedEgress: FAIL`/`overall: FAIL` and denied with `PLANTED_EGRESS_UNPROVEN` and `CONFORMANCE_FAILED`, because every outcome is computed from harness-owned observations rather than adapter self-reports, and an adapter that "cloaks" by encoding rather than substituting is caught in the decoded views instead of passing. The 457 passing synthetic groups cover post-test artifact/lockfile/SBOM/conformance/scanner substitution, tailored and invented SBOMs, version/content/pin/resolution drift, an independently published dependency set and pinned lockfile digest, malformed and non-SHA-512 integrity pins, forged and untrusted keys plus mutated and malformed signatures, evidence for another tenant scope or destination profile, independently expected version and commit, stale/expired/future-dated/inverted windows, stale scanner and conformance evidence, an unknown/missing/duplicate/failing adapter, a weaker conformance suite, a conformance run bound to other subject bytes or another channel, a self-contradicting or foreign-version attestation, an absent/untrusted/incomplete/off-subject scanner, findings at and above the policy severity, malformed, duplicate-keyed, non-canonical, non-UTF-8, oversize and deep untrusted input, an invalid trusted expectation, a missing subject input, a bounded-work flood, determinism and a 64-case seeded byte-mutation sweep that never verifies. This addresses a supply-chain or adapter update that skips a planted protected-egress check or changes the signed artifact after testing; the new surfaces are the signing key set, the expectation file, the evidence and attestation documents, the subject directory, the #19 configuration the harness owns and the CLI's path handling, all of which fail closed. It does **not** discharge the threat model's supply-chain bypass: a signer that lies about a conformance run is not detectable here (evidence is digest-bound, not re-executed), the harness is in-process and synthetic so a bypass that leaves the process is still not expressible, lockfile/SBOM parsing is format-pinned to npm and CycloneDX 1.4/1.5 with `sha256`/`sha384`/`sha512` pins, `check:sbom` compares documents rather than installed bytes (`npm ci` remains the independent installed-bytes control), the strongest independent dependency pins are optional so the signature can be the only anchor for the locked tree, the CLI refuses a subject directory reached through a symlinked ancestor, and the live `npm audit --audit-level=high` remains a separate scanner whose normalized output the gate consumes rather than reproduces. After the corrections, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage`, `git diff --check`, `npm run audit:deps` (0 vulnerabilities), `npm run check:sbom` (this repository's real lockfile cross-checked against its own ephemeral CycloneDX 1.5 SBOM, exit 0) and `npm run check:release` (17 synthetic cases, exit 0) all exited 0 locally, with 457/457 tests passing and compiled coverage of 96.73% lines / 92.10% branches for the gate, 97.86%/87.88% for the conformance harness, 99.42%/88.17% for dependency evidence, 98.90%/97.26% for canonical JSON, 100%/92.50% for byte encodings, 92.68%/77.36% for the CLI and 97.85%/92.46% across all files. Those numbers are test-instrumentation counts for synthetic offline checks, **not** release safety, signer trust or enforcement coverage; no artifact was published, no signing service was configured, remote CI has not run for this worktree, the blocking review findings were fixed rather than re-reviewed, and the signing authority, GitHub/CI trust policy, conformance-suite ownership, severity sign-off and independent-pin posture are explicitly left open for the owner.

Integration note (local, no push, PR or remote CI): this branch merged a temporary integration commit that root assembled from `origin/main` `7361df4` and the exact published #101 shadow-judge and #103 proposed annotation-design source trees. That merge had one content conflict, `src/node-crypto.d.ts`, resolved by keeping a single narrow declaration per function — the shared `createHash`/`createHmac` shapes and the #33 Ed25519 surface — with no overload set, no `any` and no widened input beyond the reviewed side's own `string | Uint8Array`; every call site in the merged tree type-checks against it. No security rule, budget, threshold, detector, policy, classifier or runtime source changed in the merge; classification v1 and the **proposed** decision 010/taxonomy/multidimensional drafts keep their existing status. Local validation at the merged head: locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run check:release` (17 synthetic cases, exit 0), `npm run check:sbom` (this repository's real lockfile against its own ephemeral CycloneDX SBOM, exit 0), `git diff --check` (clean) and one `npm test` run under the shared lock, 590 pass / 0 fail. The earlier per-module coverage figures above remain attributable to the pre-merge head and were deliberately not re-measured for a declaration and comment change. The source review's one required non-blocking follow-up was applied as documentation only: the per-record inspection scope and the **absence** of cross-record concatenation detection are stated again in [`src/adapter-conformance.ts`](../src/adapter-conformance.ts) and in the release integrity contract's *Limits*, so two individually authorized records joining into a planted value reads as a declared pre-existing limit rather than a supported guarantee; the behaviour itself is unchanged and no test pins it as a guarantee. Nothing here is a conformance, trust, adoption or production claim: the merged tree is still offline, synthetic and **NON-ENFORCING**, remote CI has not run for this worktree, and independent exact-head review of the merged tree is still required.

#2 synthetic golden/regression cases exercise exact JSON round trips for all 26 initial operation families, unknown/unversioned/malformed rejection, four fresh ordered proof claims against separately supplied identity/request/source/actual-route context, forged/stale/missing provenance, mismatch across principal/workload/tenant/project/session/purpose/source/destination/profile/redirect, payload and metadata `CONTROL` claims, stream continuity/cancellation, malformed representation, immutable payloads, non-echoing errors, and UTF-8/traversal limits. A deterministic 64-case tenant/project/principal/route variation set checks each mismatch against an otherwise identical, positively parseable binding (stable proof timestamps); no protected release permission is produced. This addresses untrusted-content promotion, poisoned model/tool claims, tenant/confused-deputy mixups, route/profile substitution and a limited fragment-context aspect of streaming leaks. The new attack surface is the adapter-to-core trusted-context handoff and its proof/clock/redirect lifecycle: #2 checks congruence/freshness only, **not** authentication or cryptographic proof integrity. Upstream adapters/brokers must authenticate principals/workloads, verify project membership, observe effective routes including redirects, guard the context argument, and track stream history; a caller able to fabricate that argument can fabricate authority. A replayed first fragment within the freshness window or an unobserved redirect remains possible without later stateful enforcement; this module neither decodes representations nor inspects data or transmits bytes. Policy authorization, secret handling, brokered uncloak, holdback and independent exact-byte egress checks remain future work. No real/private corpus was used; synthetic in-memory examples are all reserved `.invalid`/`example.invalid` values. Utility evidence is exact payload/schema round trip, **not** downstream task success or fidelity under cloaking. Informational local 1,000-cycle synthetic create/serialize/parse measurements were 177.34 ms before review fixes and 248.23 ms after (~0.248 ms/cycle after); these are neither a traffic-latency SLA nor a benchmark versus #1, which had no envelope. No model/semantic calls or external egress occur, so model latency/cost and actual privacy-release metrics are not applicable.

Independent #2 review found and reproduced four further contract bugs on the first commit: an 80,000-key sub-1-MiB wire took ~8.27 s to reject due to quadratic key checks; a time-varying JavaScript adapter object could pass validation and bind a different operation/stream flag; a forged internal error instance could echo planted data; and an event within the five-minute freshness window could predate all four proof issue times. Public-behavior regressions were added first (`npm test` exit 1, 23 pass / 4 fail); a synthetic local-sink positive round trip and missing-local-profile create/parse negatives were also added. The corrected snapshot-based schema reads each permitted data descriptor once, caps object keys at 10,000 before per-key inspection, validates in linear time, returns generic non-echoing errors, and requires `occurredAt` within each proof interval. After these fixes, locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage`, and `git diff --check` all exited 0; 27/27 tests passed, and compiled envelope coverage was 98.85% lines / 85.79% branches / 100% functions. The near-limit-plus-80,000-key regression took ~0.30 s locally after the fix (timing threshold 4 s; not a service SLA). At that intermediate #2 checkpoint, remote CI and independent rereview were still outstanding. Neither the context object nor its timestamped proof references authenticate themselves: actual independent identity/session and project binding, effective route/profile and redirect observation, and deny-before-bytes conformance are pending [model gateway #21](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/21) and [MCP gateway #23](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/23). The standard parser currently collapses duplicate raw JSON keys last-wins; this is a differential-parser residual, not permission to release. Downstream integrations must use the normalized bound envelope/serializer rather than forwarding raw wire to another parser, and prove that at their own protected release boundary (or reject duplicates before use). No release, authentication or egress enforcement is claimed from #2.

A further independent rereview at `4546174` found that a time-varying JavaScript Proxy could pass the first small-key cap, then trigger a second uncapped `ownKeys` enumeration inside whole-object descriptor collection. A new synthetic public-behavior regression first failed (`npm test` exit 1, 27 pass / 1 fail) when a four-key draft expanded to 80,000 reported keys on the second enumeration; it also checks that an over-cap first enumeration causes zero descriptor visits. The fix inspects descriptors **only for the first captured capped key list** and rejects missing/malformed descriptors without re-enumerating the caller object. A clean locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage`, and `git diff --check` then all exited 0; 28/28 tests passed, with compiled envelope coverage at 98.84% lines / 85.79% branches / 100% functions. This is bounded behavior for the tested synthetic proxy, not immunity from arbitrary host-level resource exhaustion. At that intermediate #2 head, remote CI and independent rereview were still outstanding; no protected-release claim follows from the fix.

#4 synthetic cases exercise 180 class/source-trust/actual-sink combinations (12 classes × five trusts × three sinks), a five-sensitivity matrix, separate SEND/USE/DISPLAY/EXPORT rules, and 64 tenant-scoped pending-review variations. Positive controls precede negative decisions: missing/unknown/mismatched source, authenticated principal/workload/tenant/project/session/purpose, actual route/profile/redirect, bundle ID/version or independently pinned content digest, malformed configuration, unresolved/UNKNOWN or failed classification, semantic `KEEP` against explicit `BLOCK` or a cleartext ceiling, external credential/SECRET unsafe treatments, and missing/forged/stale review outcomes deny or remain held. Deterministic replay uses a versioned trusted policy snapshot and privacy-safe opaque references; `REQUIRE_REVIEW` holds without bytes/effects until a separate, upstream-authenticated review outcome selects a currently permitted treatment. This addresses tested policy-substitution/confused-deputy release, semantic downgrade and review-transition threats; new surfaces are trusted bundle/profile resolution, digest pinning and review handoff. The pure module **does not authenticate its caller, independently verify identity/project membership/route/policy distribution/reviewer approval, transform content, issue broker grants, send bytes or perform final-byte egress checks**: upstream integrations must supply and protect those facts; a caller able to fabricate both the trusted context and bundle digest can fabricate a policy decision. The digest and decision fingerprint are unkeyed replay commitments, not authorization tokens. No actual outbound payload, vault isolation, end-to-end leakage, downstream utility, held-out semantic cases, shadow comparison or production data were evaluated in #4. A selected treatment cannot itself release data; downstream transformation and independent exact-byte enforcement remain later work.

First independent #4 review reproduced two **blocking P1** counterexamples at `f33ab6f`: different PERSON/RESTRICTED candidate units A/B in one interaction produced identical held decision fingerprints, so an approval for A selected a treatment for B; a forged `RESOLVED PERSON/PUBLIC` record with empty evidence and the same provenance replaced a trusted RESTRICTED classification and selected external `KEEP`. A separate compiling-schema red checkpoint `ff584b8` added five direct synthetic assertions: `npm test` exited 1 (61 pass / 5 fail) with the existing suite still green; failures specifically demonstrated both substitutions, empty resolved evidence and a request-side candidate mismatch. After fix `875b1bd`, clean locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage`, and `git diff --check` exited 0: 67/67 tests pass, including 26 policy groups and 41 inherited groups. Compiled policy coverage is 96.70% lines / 84.39% branches / 100% functions. Positive same-candidate/same-composer controls and 64 distinct candidate variations show the actual behavior: a separately supplied per-unit opaque candidate ID and independently pinned SHA-256 of the full normalized classification must match before selection; review fingerprints and separately supplied outcomes bind both, so an A approval remains held for B. A `RESOLVED` record without nonempty, structurally consistent detector `FOUND` evidence is rejected. This is still **congruence, not authentication**: the trusted integration must assign unique privacy-safe IDs per classified unit, authenticate the composer handoff and pin its digest independently of request content, and independently authorize reviewers. Classification provenance and candidate IDs must not contain originals; the digest/fingerprint are not bearer credentials. No bytes are emitted by these tests or this seam. Independent #4 rereview of the new head remains required; no push, PR, remote CI or acceptance is claimed.

The focused 256-event local run changed from ~12.476 s to ~0.165 s wall time on this host; this is a bounded-work regression observation, **not** an operational SLA. Independent exact-head review **blocked `eef0bc7`** on two concrete P2 preparation-integrity gaps: Git `assume-unchanged`/`skip-worktree` could hide changed public fixture and draft bytes with matching self-updated SHA-256 while `git diff` reported clean, and inherited HOME/global `core.fsmonitor` could execute an untrusted helper during verification. A same-content external hardlink was a defensive path-scope gap, though not a different-content hash bypass. Test-only RED `3c77393` reproduced both hidden-index flag variants, a disposable fsmonitor marker and the hardlink before correction (fixture guard/build/typecheck passed). The corrected verifier checks each fixed public file **including the manifest** against its raw Git HEAD blob, independently compares staged index object IDs to HEAD, refuses multiply linked regular files, and invokes only Git metadata/raw-blob commands with global/system config and local fsmonitor/hooks disabled (no diff/filter drivers). Locked local install/build/typecheck/`npm test`/coverage/`git diff --check` pass after the correction: 145/145 tests including the new disposable negative controls. A subsequent independent review blocked that exact corrective head; neither this drift check nor its hashes approve policy, authorize egress, prove private custody or retroactively lock a v0. No push, PR or remote CI result is claimed for the correction.

The next independent exact-head review **blocked `309c25f`** on two further P2 Git-metadata substitutions: replacement refs could substitute the raw HEAD blobs for both an edited fixture and self-edited manifest without changing their tree/index object IDs; a repository-local `info/grafts` file could falsely make an unrelated source commit appear ancestral. Disposable test-only RED `83ab2a9` demonstrated both false-valid results (fixture guard/build/typecheck passed). The correction disables replacement objects for **every** sanitized Git subprocess and rejects the resolved local graft path without reading or printing its contents before source ancestry checks; raw HEAD comparison, explicit index OIDs and the earlier no-hook tests remain in place. After merging reviewed D05 main `a571bf1`, locked local `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, build, typecheck, `npm test`, coverage and `git diff --check` pass at the merged checkpoint (158/158 tests); the CLI reports preparation-valid-but-ineligible for intact public files. The corrected `1183f13` exact head passed independent full security rereview and a separate bounded adversarial Git review; docs-only final head `76f5a9e` received independent delta approval, [PR #63](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/63) merged, and postmerge [main CI run 36220010260](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36220010260) passed. This is evidence for the public preparation aid only, not a scored gate. Neither replacement/graft defense nor a public hash creates policy authority, pre-tuning chronology, blind custody, scored eligibility or an external release boundary.

The earlier D05 projection-pending wording above records its adapter checkpoint. A further focused public D05 source→canonical field-span projector is implemented under [`evaluations/d05-canonical-json-spans.mjs`](../evaluations/d05-canonical-json-spans.mjs) as evaluator-private, synthetic-only and NON-ENFORCING. Its compileable RED stub/tests at `8c4c789` passed the fixture guard/build but failed five of nine focused behavior groups (four negative stub rejections passed). Subsequent adversarial tests extended that fixture and URL grammar matrix without reading a planted oracle. A strict bounded parser now walks the **entire raw public fixture bytes**, checks unique decoded JSON keys including escaped aliases before any lossy parse, binds one `D05-DEV-001` at `/fixtures/2/input/value`, and reconstructs the pinned adapter's sorted canonical field bytes. Key/token-attached boundaries translate independently test-authored decoded leaf code-point spans to exact `field-0` UTF-8 byte slices for customer, project, endpoint **host only**, and outputPath. Tests cover repeated identical values, reordered raw keys, exact #5 test-only registration and rejection of malformed, missing, ambiguous or mutated input. It is deliberately ASCII/unescaped/flat/eight-field-only; unsupported escaped/Unicode/nested/array/integer-like, bad numeric/URL or absent raw provenance receives generic rejection, not a scored pass. The evaluator keeps decoded source truth private; the projector itself never loads official oracle data, scores, approves treatments, edits the candidate, creates blind cases or freezes v0. This locally reconstructed byte contract checks source-to-canonical attribution only, **not** fixture-file authenticity against a dishonest caller (which would require a separate trusted custodian/pinned file digest before scored use), candidate-output JSON duplicate fidelity, equivalent encoded leakage, actual sink enforcement, policy authority, scored #39 eligibility or the independent #19 sentinel. The existing pre-lock no-Jev reference remains exploratory and ineligible. The precise supported/unsupported interface is in [the evaluation adapter README](../evaluations/README.md#evaluator-private-d05-raw-source-span-interface-development-only). Local green evidence after the additional adversarial controls and merge of latest `origin/main` (`581caa9`) is the clean locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage` and `git diff --check` on the final head; this is contract test evidence, not protected-release coverage. Independent exact-head security review remains required; no push, PR or remote CI for this projector is claimed. The subsequent main CI run for the separate documentation-only merge did not start jobs due to account billing/spending limits, not a test failure; do not infer a projector CI result from it.

An intermediate independent review of D05 projector head `191502a` identified a bounded **P2 attribution risk**: a sourceSpan covering only part of the `customer`, `project` or `outputPath` decoded string was accepted when its substring and normalization were internally consistent, despite all three fixed public DEV occurrences being full leaves. The same narrow URL grammar accepted a network-path-like doubled slash inside the endpoint route. The test-only RED checkpoint `56088c3` passed fixture guard/build and failed both new focused behavior groups (9 pass / 2 fail). The correction requires whole-leaf boundaries for those three controls while retaining URL host-only boundaries, and rejects empty/dot/double-slash route segments at projector creation. These are fixture-occurrence attribution limits, not approval of endpoint safety, a candidate rule or scored eligibility. The corrected exact D05 head `2e03d99` passed independent final-head rereview, [PR #62](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/pull/62) merged, and postmerge [main CI run 36218775220](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/actions/runs/36218775220) passed. None of that reviews this separate #39 preparation head or creates a freeze/score.

An AI-authored [#65 taxonomy research draft](research/issue-65-taxonomy-draft-p0.1.md) and its **unwired** [`draft-2` table](../src/taxonomy-draft.ts) propose semantic type / domain / subtype paths with identifier-content-credential form, evidence kinds and personal-data priors. The neutral `IDENTIFIER` family does not itself assert personal-data status, and the old v1 mapping helper/field has been removed under the agreed no-compatibility direction for this unreleased project. The research comparison with current classification remains documentary, not a migration interface. The draft does **not** satisfy the human #65 lead/review gate, change classification v1 or define #39 oracle labels.

A separate AI-authored [#65 source-gap supplement p0.1](research/issue-65-source-gap-supplement-p0.1.md) researches the explicit source gaps named in the [#65 public source index](research/issue-65-public-source-index-p0.1.md), linked from it: CAE finite-element mesh/load-case/boundary-condition/result artifacts, CAM toolpath and feed/tool-parameter content, engineering transmittal/revision/approval/retention vocabulary, IT configuration-baseline and integration-contract structure, and the engineering-confidentiality question, each with an explicit statement of what the retrieved source does and does not establish, plus candidate subtype proposals, `FORMAT`/`STRUCTURE`/`TENANT_DICTIONARY`/`CONTEXT` ambiguity rules, tenant-ontology-isolation reasoning, privacy-context qualification and future ADR/internal-cutover implications. It is **research only**: it adds no draft-table entry, changes no proposed finding, wires no code, creates no fixture or oracle label, and is AI-reviewed only. Its retrieval log records which pages were actually read and that licensed ISO/IEC and TOGAF/ArchiMate texts were **not** inspected; residual gaps stay with the human #65/#66 reviewers and the qualified legal/policy reviewers they designate.

A docs-only, **non-executing** desk pass for the [#38](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/38) campaign children [#40](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/40)-[#48](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/48) was added as four research documents: a [versioned role/surface conformance and evidence-limited decision dossier](research/issue-38-48-role-surface-conformance-dossier-v0.1.md), [#48 subsystem decision cards filling the nine #48 decision-quality fields for all sixteen subsystems at source scope](research/issue-48-subsystem-decision-cards-v0.1.md), an [affected-issue reconciliation proposal for #6-#35, #37 and #65-#68](research/issue-38-48-affected-issue-reconciliation-v0.1.md), and [synthetic development-only test plans](research/issue-43-68-synthetic-dev-test-plans-v0.1.md) for fidelity/restoration, credential presence, contextual re-identification and USE-without-reveal authorization. Nothing was installed, imported, executed, sent, scored, frozen or adopted; no vendor was contacted, no trust store changed, no protocol locked and no accepted architecture rewritten; decision 010 and the taxonomy/dimensional drafts remain **PROPOSED**. Its value is `source-inspected` code facts read at immutable pinned commits on 2026-10-01 and recorded with citation, never as an observed outcome: Presidio 2.2.364 (`779dbd2`) ships 74 default recognizer entries with **no person-name recognizer** and **with syntactic `IP_ADDRESS`, `MAC_ADDRESS` and `URL` recognizers whose emitted score is a pattern confidence under caller-owned acceptance, whose results carry recognizer identity but no tenant/purpose/source/sink provenance, and whose IP path has no documentation-range or infrastructure-role logic**, delegates `PERSON` to a separately selected spaCy model, defaults `score_threshold` to 0, and its anonymizer is stateless with caller-supplied keys and no mapping store; OpenAI Privacy Filter (`f7f00ca`) has three label taxonomies whose `v2` includes generic `private_address`/`private_url` while **no version has a host/customer/project/role class and the label policy is fixed by training**, downloads its default checkpoint from HuggingFace with **no revision pin**, and reports a tokenizer round-trip mismatch as a warning rather than blocking; Dataiku Kiji v1.6.4 (`a0606601`) stores `original_pii` in **plaintext SQLite** with no tenant/scope/key-version column and no crypto dependency in its module graph, documents that Codex MCP/model-list/telemetry paths **pass through verbatim**, relays redirects without following them, and keeps opt-in Sentry with unknown content scrubbing; CloakPipe v0.10.0 (`7de6269b`) encrypts its vault at rest but calls the AEAD with **no associated data**, keys tokens globally per vault file, and ships a model-reachable MCP `rehydrate` tool whose only parameter is `text`; LiteLLM 1.103.0 (`c991f4b`) needs both Presidio Analyzer and Anonymizer services, makes fail-closed blocking **configuration-conditional**, and ships a first-party hosted-Jev guardrail that POSTs raw conversation/system/tool text to `api.typesafe.ai` and blanks content by Noul score, **defaulting to fail-open** when the judge is unreachable. Dated freshness checks recorded `presidio-analyzer` still at 2.2.364 and **litellm moved to 1.103.2**, so all LiteLLM findings are pinned to 1.103.0 only. Consequently **no** conformance cell is `enforced`, every model/MCP/tool/file/shell/env/skills/memory/web/handoff/telemetry/alternate-endpoint/direct-network surface is `untested` with **no observed outcome**, and the dossier states the hosted-tool/streaming/redirect bypass requirements plus **zero sink bytes on denial** as normative prerequisites for any later trial. All sixteen [#48](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/48) subsystem choices, including distinct trusted context (#2), classification (#3), deterministic effect-owning policy (#4), conditional broker (#17), final serialized-byte sentinel (#19) and separate administrative (#28) and deployed network (#32) roles, are recorded as **proposals** — every one currently `build`/`wrap`/`defer` on unexecuted evidence and subject to the required human/architecture review and new ADRs. The nine #48 decision-quality fields for each subsystem (evidence source against tested version `UNEXECUTED`; requirements met/not met at source-document scope against unproven runtime; critical counterexample and test gate; security residual; hypothesised fidelity test with utility impact `unknown`; qualitative operational complexity with monetary/runtime cost `UNMEASURED`; licensing/maintenance; portability/replaceability; simpler alternative) are filled at **source scope only** in the decision cards; executed-evidence, licensed-rights, human #65/#66, executed-conformance and access-limited gates stay open, so decision quality is **not** complete. Detector overlap is recorded as **partial syntactic overlap with unmeasured efficacy** — neither as absent capability nor as proven un-substitutable — and reuse of individual generic recognizers behind the narrow candidate contract remains open for #40 while the deterministic effect-owning authority stays Hylja's. No affected issue is cancelled, narrowed or adopted; #65/#66 stay ahead of the #39 freeze, #43 evidence stays ahead of #68 and #13, and #67 stays out of the first enforcement proof.

A second independent native Pi review of the corrected head `7f748fd` returned **REQUEST_CHANGES**
again, with **one blocking finding still open**: B-2 was only half fixed. That report is an external
artifact and is not committed or edited here; it approves nothing, and a further independent review of
the corrected head is still required. B-1, N-1, N-2, N-3 and N-4 were independently re-verified as
fixed, and the `e667186` red checkpoint was independently replayed (30 groups, 24 pass, 6 fail, names
matching the plan exactly).

**B-2, the cross-scope audit write was refused only afterwards.** `auditGateAllows` compared
`appended.receipt.scope` with the registry scope **after** `appendAuditEvent` had already committed,
so a hook that self-declared tenant A while carrying tenant B's ledger and tenant B's trusted context
denied the activation but had already written tenant A's `ADMIN_APPLIED` into tenant B's chain and
advanced B's head, and the committed regression asserted `foreign.entries.length === 1`, i.e. it locked
in the defect. The root clarification for this slice is explicit that this does not satisfy "nothing
recorded/nothing changes on scope mismatch", and that the reviewer's earlier "human design decision on
B-1 and B-2" wording was not a new approval prerequisite: these are autonomous bug fixes in an
explicitly authorized offline foundation, so no design gate was created for them.

The fix reads the **actual carried scope before any append**: `auditHookView` now reads the ledger's
own exported `scope` — and, for defence in depth, the trusted context's scope — through this module's
existing bounded own-data-descriptor reader, and refuses `AUDIT_SCOPE_MISMATCH` (or
`INVALID_AUDIT_HOOK` for something that is not an #20 ledger) **before** `recordAudit` runs. That
reuses #20's exported `AuditLedger` shape and nothing else: no stream reader, no entry validation and
**no ledger core change**. The receipt re-validation before the registry mutation is **kept** as the
second, independent line, because it binds the *result* rather than a declaration. The prior claim in
the contract and the plan that detecting this earlier "would mean re-reading the ledger's own scope
here, which is #20's reader and not this module's to fork" was **inaccurate for this codebase** and is
withdrawn: `AuditLedger` is an exported interface with a `scope` field and the governance module
already holds the reader needed to read it.

The rewritten regression asserts exactly what is now required: for a hook that falsely self-declares
scope A while carrying ledger/trusted B, the **registry is unchanged AND the foreign ledger's
`entries` length and chain head are unchanged**, for activation, for exception registration, and for
revocation of a genuinely live exception (whose `revokedAt` stays `null` — that case was previously
untested because registration through the mis-scoped hook never created the exception). A further case
pins the pre-append refusal for an unreadable ledger, for a stream scoped to the right tenant but no
project, and for a trusted context scoped to another tenant. Re-run independently against the built
`dist/` from a temp script outside the worktree, since deleted: `outcome: DENIED AUDIT_SCOPE_MISMATCH |
audit {"status":"NOT_ATTEMPTED","finding":"NOT_ATTEMPTED"} | registry.config: null | foreign BEFORE
{"entries":0,"head":null} AFTER {"entries":0,"head":null} | foreign read: 0 entries`, and the same
foreign-head-unchanged result for exception registration. The happy path still records into the
correctly scoped ledger.

Two precision observations from that review were also closed, in the safe direction, with cases
written first: a **removed profile** and a **rebind to a strictly narrower destination** (an external
trust zone moved into a local one) are now labelled `NARROWED` with reason
`NARROWED_DESTINATION_OR_TREATMENT` and are **never counted** as newly exposed, while every
destination change stays visible per finding through `baselineSink`/`candidateSink`; an unrecognised
trust zone never produces a narrowing, so a host-altered bundle stays on the reporting side. The
report's earlier blanket "closed fields, counts and digests only" phrasing is now scoped precisely:
findings carry closed field names, closed-vocabulary values, counts and digests **plus the exact sink
reference each side declares**, a bounded administrative destination token re-read from the re-pinned
bundle and never a payload, purpose or original. The residual that genuinely remains is stated: the
configuration snapshot holds the caller's bundle object, so the content pin is re-derived and the
profiles are read as two separate steps, and a host that rewrites the bundle between them is the same
host-writable boundary the registry already has.

`npm run audit:deps` 0 vulnerabilities, the ephemeral CycloneDX SBOM cross-checking
