An independent native Pi review of the exact source head `8264470` returned **REQUEST_CHANGES** with **two reproducible HIGH findings**, one defeating an explicit #28 acceptance criterion and one a cross-tenant audit-evidence defect, plus three actionable non-blocking findings (N-1 foreign-scope exception registration, N-2 an unasserted capacity bound, N-3 floor vectors that never touched governance) and one documentation inaccuracy (N-4). That report is an external artifact and is not committed or edited here; it approves nothing, and a further independent review of the corrected head is still required. **Both HIGH findings are fixed, and both are new behaviour of this slice rather than pre-existing foundation limits.**

**B-1, the simulation did not report a newly allowed or newly rebound sink.** The exposure test skipped any probe whose profile was absent from one bundle, so a candidate that **added** a destination profile (baseline `PROFILE_MISMATCH` -> candidate `SELECTED KEEP`) and a candidate that **rebound** an existing profile's sink both reported `newlyExposed.count: 0`, and the report never named a sink at all: `newlyExposed.sinks` only ever held `probe.profileId`. A profile id is not a destination, and #28 asks for "newly exposed classes/**sinks**". Each side is now evaluated by the **real #4 seam against the exact sink its own configuration declares**, with a one-sided profile evaluated against the other side's sink so #4 answers `PROFILE_MISMATCH`/`DENIED` itself rather than being skipped; exposure is then the union of a release-level relaxation and "the candidate points this profile at an exact sink the baseline did not", so an added sink is `NEW_EXPOSURE`, a rebound sink is `SINK_REBOUND` when the level did not also rise, and a candidate that **removes** a profile declares no sink and therefore narrows without being reported as an exposure. Every finding now carries `baselineSink`/`candidateSink`, `newlyExposed.sinks` holds exact sink references and the new `newlyExposed.profiles` holds the profile ids for attribution. Independently re-derived with the reviewer's own scenario: adding `third.invalid` -> `https://third-party.example.invalid/collect` now reports `newlyExposed {classes:[PERSON], sinks:[https://third-party.example.invalid/collect], profiles:[third.invalid], operations:[SEND], count:1}` with baseline reason `PROFILE_MISMATCH` and candidate `SELECTED KEEP`; rebinding `allowed.invalid` to `https://exfil-moved.example.invalid/v1` reports both probes `SINK_REBOUND` with `https://allowed.example.invalid/v1 -> https://exfil-moved.example.invalid/v1` and `count: 2`; and an independent #4 call on the candidate alone confirms the new sink really is `SELECTED KEEP / RULE_SELECTED`.

**This paragraph records the state at `394c212`; the second independent review recorded above found B-2 only half fixed, and the residual it describes here was closed at `d623380`.**

**B-2, the audit hook's declared scope was checked but not the ledger the evidence reached.** `sameScope(hook.scope, registry.scope)` compared a **self-declared** field, while #20 derives an entry's scope from the **ledger** and stamps it on the receipt, so a hook declaring tenant A while carrying tenant B's ledger and tenant B's trusted context passed the only check and committed tenant A's `ADMIN_APPLIED` into tenant B's chain with the activation `APPLIED`: a cross-tenant evidence defect reachable by passing the wrong ledger. The committed receipt scope is now compared with the registry scope before any registry write, in all three entry points, with exact congruence (a stream carrying no project is not congruent with a registry scoped to one, and an absent or malformed receipt is not congruent with anything). `src/audit-ledger.ts` is unchanged. **Stated rather than implied, and corrected at `d623380`:** as it stood at `394c212`, by the time the committed scope was known the foreign-stream record already existed, so a mis-scoped hook cost an orphan `ADMIN_APPLIED` record in the foreign stream. The clause that also claimed "never ... a cross-tenant write" is **withdrawn as false** - the cross-tenant audit write is precisely what happened, as the contract itself admitted two paragraphs earlier. The claim that detecting it earlier "would mean forking #20's stream reader" is **withdrawn as inaccurate for this codebase**: `AuditLedger` is an exported interface with a `scope` field and this module already holds the bounded reader needed to read it. Both were corrected at `d623380`, which refuses before any append; see the paragraph above. The same check now also refuses exception registration and revocation, where the change never happens.

**N-1** is closed: a registry refuses an exception minted for another tenant or project with `EXCEPTION_SCOPE_MISMATCH`, so an exception cannot be filed in one tenant's registry and only turn out to be unusable later (reproduced independently: tenant-B exception into tenant A now `DENIED / EXCEPTION_SCOPE_MISMATCH` with the registry unchanged). **N-2** is closed by a committed capacity case that also pins the deliberate asymmetry: the cap gates **registration only**, never revocation, because refusing a revocation at capacity would strand a live exception, which is the fail-open direction. **N-3** is closed by rewriting the generated floor vectors so they run against a registry **holding a live exception**: 144 class x operation x sink vectors now each also call `evaluatePolicyException`, which is `APPLICABLE` for exactly one of them and `DENIED / EXCEPTION_BINDING_MISMATCH` for the other 143 while the #4 floors are asserted on the same registry. **N-4** is closed: the contract now says a refusal decided before any append reports `NOT_ATTEMPTED` rather than one of #20's codes. **N-5** (the unattributed earlier coverage run) is left exactly as recorded.

**Red checkpoint, committed and replayable:** `e667186` is tests-only with the implementation at `8264470`, and `npm test` at that commit exits 1 at **736 tests, 730 pass, 6 fail** — exactly the six new groups (an added destination, a rebound sink, a removed destination, a mixed-scope audit hook, a foreign-scope exception registration and the capacity bound), with every inherited group green. **What was actually run at this head, all offline and under `flock /tmp/hylja-agentic-campaign/test.lock`:** `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0, `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0, `npm test` exit 0 at **736/736** (30 governance groups, 706 inherited), `npm run test:coverage` exit 0 at 736/736 with `policy-governance.js` at **97.14 % line / 83.84 % branch / 100.00 % function** coverage and 98.67 % / 93.37 % / 96.63 % across all files (contract exercise of a synthetic offline substrate, not enforcement), `npm run audit:deps` 0 vulnerabilities, the ephemeral CycloneDX SBOM cross-checking `DEPENDENCY_EVIDENCE_CONSISTENT`, `node evaluations/preparation-integrity.mjs` exit 0 as `PREPARATION_VALID_BUT_NOT_ELIGIBLE / PUBLIC_DRAFT_ONLY`, and `git diff --check` clean; the reviewer's six synthetic scenarios were re-run against the built `dist/` from a temp script outside the worktree, which was deleted afterwards. No threshold, budget or fixture was changed to accommodate any run, and no cause is invented for the earlier unattributed failure.

Local #28 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 11.15.0; branch `agentic/policy-control-plane`, base `6e4bc65`, no push, PR, merge to main or issue write): a bounded, **offline, NON-ENFORCING** policy governance substrate exists in [`src/policy-governance.ts`](../src/policy-governance.ts) over the merged #4 policy seam, the #20 audit ledger, the shared canonical-JSON helper and the #3 composer; its exact inputs, refusal order and honest limits are in [the policy governance contract](contracts/policy-governance-contract.md). It keeps **versioned configuration snapshots** (a governance `revision`, a creation instant, an administrative purpose, and an independent pin over the exact #4 bundle, re-derived on every read), **narrow exception objects** bound to exactly one tenant, project, subject, purpose, semantic class, sensitivity, destination profile plus exact sink reference, operation and configuration revision, with a mandatory expiry and an explicit revocation, a **bounded policy-diff simulation** reporting the classes, sinks and operations a candidate would newly expose, and **separate trusted approval and #20 audit hooks**. The accepted foundation is preserved exactly: `decidePolicy` remains the only decision identity and `hylja.foundation` version `1` the only supported policy schema — an unknown id/version is refused with a typed `CONFIGURATION_POLICY_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` rather than extending `decidePolicy`'s wire allowlist, and nothing in `src/policy.ts` changed — while the governance `configVersion` is a separate, separately refused schema version and a `revision` is an activation counter that is never comparable to a policy version. **No global or wildcard bypass is expressible**: tenant and project are both required, `*`/URL/email/free text cannot be planted in any verbatim-read field, an exception must expire within a hard 24-hour ceiling (`EXCEPTION_TTL_EXCEEDED`) with an inverted window refused, a `SECRET` or `CREDENTIAL_OR_SECRET` exception may only select `MASK`/`REMOVE`, activating a newer revision supersedes older exceptions automatically (`EXCEPTION_CONFIG_SUPERSEDED`), and a revoked identifier cannot be resurrected by re-registering. An exception is **not** an authority: `APPLICABLE` carries `authority: 'NONE'`, `grantsTreatment: false` and `requiresPolicyDecision: true`, and the #4 floors still stand — a committed case registers and applies an exception and then shows the same `SEND` tuple still `HELD` with `REQUIRE_REVIEW`, the exfil sink still denying `CONFIDENTIAL` and `CREDENTIAL_OR_SECRET`, `EXPORT` still denied, and an `UNRESOLVED` classification still `DENIED`/`UNRESOLVED_CLASSIFICATION`. The simulation is computed by **running the real #4 seam twice per probe** (active bundle, candidate bundle) over a fixed module-owned synthetic binding and the exact sink each bundle declares, so a "newly exposed" finding is an observed transition the committed cases re-derive independently; it takes no approval, returns `activation: UNAVAILABLE`, and is structurally unusable as an activation or registration input because both entry points accept only objects this module issued. Approval is a **trusted integration obligation**, not authentication here: it binds scope, approver, purpose, operation, revision, the exact target digest, the currently active configuration digest (`GOVERNANCE_ZERO_DIGEST` when none), the exception tuple and a window capped at one hour; missing, unsupported, forged, substituted, stale, expired, not-yet-valid, foreign-scope, wrong-purpose, wrong-operation and oversized-window approvals all deny and leave the registry unchanged. Audit is #20's hook with #20's `gateHighRiskEffect` reused unchanged, and a sealed ledger, an unusable key, an out-of-scope hook or any other non-committed append leaves a high-risk activation **unchanged** with `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`/`AUDIT_EVIDENCE_INVALID`/`AUDIT_SCOPE_MISMATCH` rather than a partial promotion, while a recorded `ADMIN_APPLIED` is always followed by the write it describes. Every crossing value is read from one bounded own-key snapshot of own data descriptors with the declared array length capped before any sort, hash, parse or comparison; a hostile `ownKeys` is asserted to fire exactly once, timestamps must round-trip exactly so an imaginary date such as `2026-02-31T00:00:00.000Z` is a fixed `INVALID_CLOCK` rather than a rolled-forward instant, `Date.now()` is never read (the clock is an argument, so expiry is deterministic), and every refusal is a fixed code with no planted original, request content, free-text purpose or caller string in any outcome, report, refusal or audit record. **Red checkpoint, committed and replayable:** `0c250d3` is the deliberately denying compileable stub plus the behaviour tests, with `npm test` exit 1 at **727 tests, 706 pass, 21 fail** — the 21 new groups and every inherited group green. The green head `4c1e0c3` adds the implementation and two further cases (configuration supersession; a profile neither bundle declares) for **23 groups** (a following commit renames the not-attempted audit status so no refusal reports an audit code it did not observe, and asserts that behaviour; no decision, code or floor changes): a positive scoped expiry case and denials at the exact expiry instant, for a changed tenant, project, subject, purpose, class, sensitivity, destination profile, destination ref and operation; replay after expiry and after revocation; forged approval authority, version, digest and revision; a planted free-text approval purpose; policy content altered after approval (`CONFIGURATION_CONTENT_CHANGED` with the active revision unmoved); a simulation report fed back into both entry points; audit outage, unusable key and scope mismatch leaving the registry unchanged; an over-cap and a duplicated probe list; hostile getters, a throwing `ownKeys` trap, a key-growing time-varying proxy, unknown properties and five malformed clocks; 24 generated tenant vectors × 23 foreign scopes asserting `EXCEPTION_SCOPE_MISMATCH` and `EXCEPTION_UNKNOWN` with no cross-tenant reach; 144 generated class × operation × sink floor vectors; planted protected originals scanned across every outcome, evaluation, denial, report, refusal and the whole #20 read result; and a 256-probe bounded-work run. **What was actually run at the head it measures (`4c1e0c3`/`8264470`, before the independent review recorded above, which carries the current scope and evidence), all offline and under `flock /tmp/hylja-agentic-campaign/test.lock`:** locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund` (0 vulnerabilities), `node scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` exit 0, `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0, `npm test` exit 0 at **729/729** (706 inherited plus 23 new groups), `npm run test:coverage` exit 0 at 729/729 with `policy-governance.js` at **96.38% line / 80.30% branch / 100% function** coverage and 98.63% / 93.28% / 96.61% across all files — numbers that measure contract exercise of a synthetic offline substrate, **not** enforcement, protected-egress safety, tenant isolation in production, approval authenticity or release readiness. `git diff --check` clean, and `npm run audit:deps` reports 0 vulnerabilities while the ephemeral CycloneDX SBOM cross-checks clean against this repository's own lockfile (`npm run --silent sbom` then `node scripts/check-sbom.mjs` -> `DEPENDENCY_EVIDENCE_CONSISTENT`). **One unattributed run is recorded rather than hidden:** the first combined sequence above reported `npm run test:coverage` at 729 tests, 728 pass, **1 fail**, and that run filtered its own output so the failing group's name was **not captured**. It did not reproduce in three subsequent identical `npm run test:coverage` runs and one further combined run, all 729/729, and the new suite alone runs in about 1.3 s against a 15 s assertion, so **no cause is attributed and none is excluded**; several inherited suites assert 2-30 s wall-clock budgets (for example `audit-ledger`, `adapter-conformance`, `configured-candidates`, `configured-units`, `contact-candidates` and `egress-sentinel`), and this plan already records earlier unattributed full-run failures under host load. No threshold, fixture or test was changed to accommodate any run. **Limits, stated rather than implied:** this seam is **not an enforcement boundary** — a pure governance module that authenticates nothing and sends no bytes is evidence for a seam, not proof of protected-egress safety. No provider API, HTTP or admin server, persistence, authentication service, key management, production registry or distribution mechanism, real signing authority, vault/mapping lookup, transport, #13 treatment semantics, #19 send path, deployment or managed-network enforcement is implemented or claimed; [#48](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/48)'s administrative-plane reuse decision stays open, so this is a separately reviewable bounded foundation and **not** broad product adoption, and decision 010 and the taxonomy/dimensional drafts stay **PROPOSED**. The in-memory registry is a plain host-writable object whose only protection is re-verified content pins, the audit hook inherits #20's honest in-array storage boundary (not external durability), the synthetic host in the tests is a test double and **no congruence digest is a credential**, and approver authentication, project membership, separation of duties, deployment/distribution, detector and classifier recall, actual route observation, downstream transformation fidelity and held-out evaluation all stay upstream obligations. Independent exact-head review of this head is still required, and it is not relied on here.


Implementation work is tracked from [roadmap issue #36](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/36), with scoped implementation issues #1-#35 and #37 carrying their own dependencies and acceptance criteria. A separate [comparative reuse/buy/build campaign #38](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/38) may propose later scope changes; it does not itself prove implementation or alter accepted security invariants.

Local #20 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8; branch `agentic/audit-ledger`): a compileable RED checkpoint `eacb824` from an interrupted harness migration was inherited, not trusted; the work was redone from the actual diff. Four defects found in the inherited partial implementation were reproduced before fixing and are covered by new regressions. Clean locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage` and `git diff --check` all exit 0 at the head: **436/436** tests pass, and the compiled `audit-ledger.js` module is **100.00% lines / 93.62% branches / 100.00% functions**. The new regressions were confirmed genuinely red against the inherited implementation (31 pass / 4 fail: uncommittable stream threw instead of returning `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`; unreadable ledgers were mislabeled `INVALID_CONTEXT`/`INVALID_AUTHORITY`; read/export results aliased the ledger's mutable event objects; and 2,000 appends took **31,982 ms**). **Evidence provenance:** that RED checkpoint, the figures above and the timing numbers are local author-branch evidence on `agentic/audit-ledger`; they are **not** ancestors of the connector's published snapshot of this work, so they are not replayable from the published history and are recorded as historical local measurements rather than as a published gate. The defects and corrections: (1) **quadratic append** — the append path re-validated the entire stream per event, so filling a default 4,096-entry ledger cost ~85 s, turning evidence accumulation itself into an audit-outage risk; the append path now takes one bounded `length` read and validates only the entry it extends, while verification/checkpoint/read rebuild every entry once per call — 2,000 appends fell to ~0.1 s and 3,200 appends from **52,371 ms to 152 ms**, linear; (2) an unescaped `TypeError` escaped `appendAuditEvent` when a stream could not commit (sealed or non-extensible entries array), now the typed `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` outage code, so a caller cannot catch the exception and route around it; (3) an unreadable ledger was reported as a caller/context or authority failure, now a distinct `INVALID_LEDGER` on append, verification and read/export; (4) `readAuditEvents`/`exportAuditEvents` returned the ledger's own mutable event objects, so two readers shared one object and a read result was a mutation channel into stored evidence — read/export now deliver independent, deeply frozen rebuilds that still serialize byte-identically to the source entry. The inherited test named "append rejects an unverifiable chain key change" asserted the opposite of its own name and was corrected: the ledger deliberately retains no key, so it cannot know a key changed, the append is recorded, and the independently retained checkpoint is what exposes the fork as `CHAIN_BROKEN`.

The implemented [`#20` audit ledger contract](contracts/audit-ledger-contract.md) is a provider-independent, **non-enforcing** event contract and append/tamper-evidence core over synthetic data. Event fields are strictly allowlisted per kind with no raw payload, free-text metadata, purpose/provenance text, query, exception message or caller-authored error; failures are fixed codes or fixed-message `TypeError`s, and the module contains no logging call at all (asserted by test over source, test and compiled output). Actor, tenant/project scope and both HMAC keys come only from supplied trusted integration context — those names are absent from every draft allowlist, so self-asserted authority is `INVALID_DRAFT`, not a recorded claim. Caller-supplied opaque entity/correlation/interaction/candidate references are **never stored**: each becomes a scope-bound, role-separated keyed pseudonym (`HMAC-SHA256(pseudonymKey, tenant ␀ project ␀ role ␀ reference)`, truncated to 128 bits), with 64 generated tenant/project variations proving non-correlation across scopes. Only identity/version tokens are read verbatim, structurally excluding `@`, `/`, `:` and whitespace so an email, URL, host, path or purpose cannot be stored there; key versions are additionally numeric. The residual is explicit and not fixable here: a protected original that is itself a legal token (e.g. `jane.quinte` as a principal) cannot be distinguished from an identity, so the supplying integration must supply authenticated identity identifiers. Administrative key and policy operations are attributable to the trusted actor because that attribution is why those events exist. Events pin the exact intelligence bundle (policy id/version/64-hex content digest plus an ordered, duplicate-free component version set), the #3 `SEMANTIC_CLASSES` × `SENSITIVITIES` pair and the #4 decision state/treatment/digest. `AUTHORIZATION_ATTEMPT` and `MAPPING_LIFECYCLE` are **reserved schemas** for #17 and #18 that record what a future broker or lifecycle operation would record, including denials; no broker or vault is implemented, neither is a dependency, no event carries a grant/capability/token/reveal field, and no bundled key ever authorizes anything.

Tamper evidence is anchored, never claimed from an internal chain. Each entry carries `sequence`, `prevDigest` and `entryDigest = HMAC-SHA256(chainKey, canonicalJson)`; the ledger retains no key and recomputation uses the supplied one. `verifyAuditStream` states plainly that internal chain integrity is **not** tamper evidence — a key holder can rewrite a whole stream — so a consistent chain with no retained checkpoint is `UNANCHORED`/`NO_ANCHOR`, never `VERIFIED`, and `VERIFIED` is always scoped to `anchoredThrough` with `unanchoredEntries` reported. Modification, reordering, insertion, gaps, deletion (`ENTRY_MISSING`), tail truncation (`TAIL_TRUNCATED`), key-holder rewrites and cross-tenant substitution are `TAMPERED` only because an independently retained checkpoint disagrees; changed, wrong-scope, duplicate, unsorted and over-count checkpoints are rejected. The storage boundary is stated honestly: an **in-memory synthetic substrate with no durability, replication, separate administrative domain or anchor of its own** (nothing here is file-backed) — it is not a production durable audit service and not an external checkpoint/WORM/transparency authority. Read and export are scope-isolated **per entry**: the authority scope must equal the stream scope *and* every delivered entry's own scope must equal it before anything is selected, otherwise `SCOPE_MISMATCH` with no entries and no foreign pseudonym, digest, actor, bundle or tenant metadata; both are bounded (sequence range, kind filter, limit ≤ 1000, default 100) and separate: export requires an explicitly asserted `exportAuthorized` and a read authority carrying that flag is rejected, so reading never implies exporting. There is no cross-tenant listing, search or bulk API, and no plaintext mapping API of any kind. Required append/commit unavailability — a cleared `accepting`, a capacity bound, or a stream that cannot commit or whose commit cannot be confirmed — returns a typed restrictive failure and `gateHighRiskEffect` never yields `permitted` for it, so no dependent high-risk effect proceeds unaudited.

Threats addressed within the tested surface: audit as a second plaintext store (planted originals are excluded from the exact serialized bytes of every entry, receipt and checkpoint), forged provenance and self-asserted actor/scope/authority, cross-tenant entity/correlation collision and stream substitution, dropped/reordered/deleted/truncated/modified events, recomputed unauthenticated chains presented as verified, audit outage and unaudited high-risk effects, unbounded and hostile caller objects (throwing getters, Proxies throwing on `get`/`ownKeys`/`getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, inherited prototypes, over-cap key sets), and resource exhaustion from quadratic append. Not covered and therefore not claimed: buffering tamper-evident evidence across a sink outage (only the security model's blocking branch is implemented); production durability, replication, backup or restore; an external checkpoint/WORM/transparency/notarization authority; key management, rotation, versioning, destruction or KMS integration for the two HMAC keys (only the all-zero case is refused); authenticated identity and project membership for actor attribution; separation of duties for administrators; retention, expiry and deletion of audit evidence; multi-writer concurrency, sequence arbitration and distributed ordering; integrity re-checking on read (`readAuditEvents` validates, rebuilds and enforces per-entry scope congruence but does not recompute digests, so an anchored integrity judgement on a read result is conditional on the caller separately running `verifyAuditStream` against a retained checkpoint); whole-stream validation on the append path (corruption away from the append head is caught at verification, not at append — pinned by test); fabrication or replay of an append **result** passed to `gateHighRiskEffect`, which is a pure structural check that cannot bind a receipt to a committed entry and is a documented call-site obligation; auditing of audit reads and exports themselves, which are unaudited and pageable in `ceil(n/1000)` calls, and abuse/rate signals; caller-supplied, unverified `occurredAt` (no injected clock — only shape and `Date.parse` acceptance); external durability of a confirmed commit; and integration with the #17 broker, #18 lifecycle, #19 Egress Sentinel or any adapter. No real, private, blind or held-out material, production credential, or future #17/#18 producer was used; every fixture value is obviously synthetic and non-routable. Independent exact-head review, push, PR, remote CI and acceptance all remain outstanding; delivery belongs to the orchestrator.

The first independent review of the published head `138effb` (tree-identical to local `c93568f`) returned **REQUEST_CHANGES** with one blocking P1 and non-blocking P2/P3 findings, all reproduced here against that published pre-fix tree — the final test file run against the published `138effb` module is **45 tests, 38 pass / 7 fail**: (a) **silent append loss** — `appendAuditEvent` performed an unverified dynamic write, so a sink that accepts an own no-op `push`, or a `Proxy` whose `set`/`defineProperty` traps report success without writing, produced `RECORDED` with a full receipt while storing nothing, and `gateHighRiskEffect` then returned `permitted: true` over zero evidence — contradicting the contract's "a stream that cannot commit returns `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`" and the plan's "no dependent high-risk effect proceeds unaudited"; (b) **cross-tenant read/export** — relabelling the outer ledger to tenant B while the entries still belonged to A returned A's events, pseudonyms, digests, actor and bundle metadata under B's valid authority, while `verifyAuditStream` already called the same structure `TAMPERED`/`SCOPE_MISMATCH`; (c) an **all-zero HMAC key** was accepted, so a publicly derivable key produced a fully "verified" chain; (d) `items()` enumerated the whole key set before applying the length bound, making rejection of a hostile `entries` array cost time proportional to it; and (e) misleading bound/limit wording. The correction: the append now **confirms the commit** by reading the record back through its own bounded descriptors — the exact record at the slot it occupies, with the array's own `length` the single value that grew by one — and any sealed, read-only, non-extensible, length-lying, substituting or read-back-failing container is the typed `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` outage, which `gateHighRiskEffect` never permits; **per-entry scope congruence** against the trusted access scope now gates read and export before selection, so one foreign entry denies the whole read and discloses no foreign metadata; an **all-zero key** is refused while every other property of key quality stays a trusted KMS/key-management obligation; array **length bounds are decided from one O(1) descriptor read before enumeration** (a 3,000,000-own-key `entries` array is now refused in 0.1 ms instead of 2,380–2,784 ms across two runs), the append path still never enumerates own keys so append stays linear (16,000 appends measured 43.9–47.1 µs/event after the fix against 44.3–50.8 µs/event before it, i.e. no per-append rescan), and the bound comments now state what bounded work does and does not promise for hostile JavaScript traps. New behavioural regressions include a generated nine-shape hostile-sink matrix asserting that every `RECORDED` result corresponds to a record readable in that array, 32 generated tenant substitutions, a relabelled-plus-substituted read/export refusal, a descriptor-trap over-long array refused without any `ownKeys` call, and a caller-supplied `occurredAt` recorded verbatim and digest-bound. Honest limits kept explicit and pinned by test rather than papered over: a container that lies about **both** the write and the read-back cannot be distinguished in language, so a confirmed commit is presence in this in-memory array, not durability; `occurredAt` is caller-supplied and unverified; audit reads and exports are themselves unaudited and pageable; read/export results carry no digest recomputation, so anchored integrity on a read is conditional on a separate `verifyAuditStream`; and `CHAIN_BROKEN` is not the only corruption verdict away from the append head — a tenant-substituted entry is `SCOPE_MISMATCH`. Decision 010 and the taxonomy/information-model drafts remain **proposed** and unwired; no accepted v1 classifier/policy contract, policy threshold or budget changed, and no production auth/KMS/durability/promotion effect is claimed. Independent exact-head re-review of this corrected head is still required; this approval does not transfer to any other SHA, and no push, PR, issue edit, message, remote CI or acceptance is claimed.

## Dependency-ordered milestones

### 0. Foundation and executable contracts

Goal: establish TypeScript toolchain, normalized interaction envelope, classification model, policy vocabulary, deterministic security invariants, and synthetic eval harness.

Exit evidence:

- strict TypeScript build and tests;
- synthetic fixtures only;
- runnable synthetic contract/property tests for tenant-scoped policy/authorization, blocked-secret behavior, forged policy context, and fail-closed decisions (no passing placeholders); storage, cache, and broker isolation are proved again when those components exist;
- threat cases mapped to specific negative tests, including classification conflict and missing destination/profile context.

Primary issues: #1-#5.

### 1. Text cloaking proof

Goal: local synthetic text request -> deterministic and configured candidate generation -> optional local or independently guarded semantic shadow judgment -> policy -> transformation -> independent check of the exact serialized egress.

A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #6 normalization stage exists in [`src/normalization.ts`](../src/normalization.ts). It never rewrites the input. It returns the original text as the root view plus decoded Base64/base64url/percent/hex views with provenance. Binary decodes contribute their printable strings, including ASCII runs from UTF-16LE/BE at both byte parities. Decoding also covers Base64 at all four alignments (for prefixes glued to a run), odd-length hex, and concatenated and line-wrapped Base64. Identical disjoint copies are all recorded as occurrences of one view. Explicit depth, total-expansion, view-count, decode-work and run-length budgets turn anything uninspected into a recorded span and a `PARTIAL` result, never clean. Invalid UTF-8 *root* bytes are a `FAILURE` with no replacement text. `COMPLETE` only means no budget was exceeded; it is not "clean". Out of scope: JSON/JS/HTML escapes, colon-separated hex, form `+` encoding, quoted-printable, compression and archives. Also not decoded: Base64 runs shorter than 12 characters, hex runs shorter than 16 digits, printable runs under 8 characters inside binary, wrapped Base64 with lines under 16 characters, and UTF-32. These forms stay in their parent text for detectors. The stage also gives a content-type *hint* and a bounded detection fold (NFKC, invisible characters removed, dashes unified) with an offset map. Folded output is capped at the input-unit limit, a combining-mark cluster is capped at 4096 units, and over-limit expansion fails before offset maps are allocated; the composition seam records that fold failure as `PARTIAL` with an opaque view. Work is linear. The default 1 MiB budget stays around 1 s on adversarial inputs; the maximum opt-in budget (16 MiB input, 128 MiB decode work) can take roughly 12 s, so callers that raise it must run it off the request path. The direct detectors and parsers remain independently callable; the bounded composition seam described next wires #8/#9/#37 to #6 views, while #7 fields are still separate.

A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #6 composition seam in [`src/normalized-detection.ts`](../src/normalized-detection.ts) now scans the raw and compatibility-folded root and decoded views with #8, #9 and #37. It returns metadata only: string-input root raw matches have exact original UTF-16 spans, folded root matches have covering spans, and decoded matches carry the outer encoded-run span or spans rather than an invented exact plaintext offset. Byte input is explicitly labelled as offsets into decoded UTF-8 text, never byte-source offsets. Repeated encoded copies retain every recorded outer occurrence; nested and binary-strings views retain only the encoded envelope. Normalization limits, detector failures, configured-name scope failures, fold/scan limits and candidate caps all produce `PARTIAL` or `FAILURE` with privacy-safe reasons and opaque locations; no `COMPLETE` result means a policy allow or that unsupported encodings are clean. The seam has synthetic repeated/nested/Unicode, tenant-scope, failure and generated encoding tests.

A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #7 follow-on composes parsed fields into that same seam. Every view is parsed by the single #7 format its own content-type hint names, and the root additionally runs the trusted `formats` list the integration supplies (which is how YAML, TOML and connection strings are reached at all; decoded views are new text and are parsed only by their own hint). That list is a union with the root's hinted format, never a replacement for it, so a wrong or hostile format assertion can add a parse and its own opaque ranges but can never suppress the parse the hint named. Each field's decoded value is scanned with #8, #9 and #37, and #8's `FIELD_KEY` rule is fed a trusted key hint taken from the parse: the deepest credential-like key-path segment, or the value of a `name`/`key` sibling that #7 matched as naming this value. A value written verbatim in the view keeps exact original spans; an escaped, percent- or entity-decoded value, and any value of a decoded view, is reported only as a covering span (`ORIGINAL_COVER`/`UTF8_TEXT COVER`) or the encoded envelope, never as an invented exact offset. A verbatim value is otherwise left to the view scan, which already covered that text, so nothing is duplicated. Overlapping and repeated evidence is retained: a structural credential candidate covering a format match inside the same field, and the same value in several fields, each keep their own span, evidence id and input reference. The de-duplication key is the **occurrence** — view, representation, in-view span, #7 format, field span, path digest and, for a #7 field, the in-value occurrence offsets — never the encoded envelope, because every occurrence inside one decoded view shares that envelope; two parsers over one view keep their own attributed candidate each. (The in-value offsets were added after an independent review showed that a value #7 decoded reports one whole-field span for every match inside it, which had collapsed repeated occurrences in decoded field values; see the #37/#10 correction paragraph below.) Malformed, unsupported, opaque and over-budget parser states stay `PARTIAL` with their own opaque locations and reason codes, a `#7` reason is never dropped even on a `COMPLETE` parse (a duplicate key therefore keeps the document ambiguous rather than reading as fully inspected; `COMMENTS` is the one exempt reason, because comment text is inside the view text this seam already scans in full), a per-request field budget makes the remainder of the exhausting view **and every later view it skips** opaque rather than silently unscanned, and a requested format that does not hold is reported as opaque too. Key names are payload content and are never emitted: a candidate carries only the #7 format, a request-bound digest of the key path, the hint source and depth, #7's `highRisk` flag, the field span, the match offsets inside the decoded value, and whether that value is verbatim. That path digest is unlinkable across requests only to the extent the trusted integration honours its side of the contract: it is bound to the caller's `inputRef`, so distinct `inputRef`s produce uncorrelatable digests while a caller that reuses one `inputRef` across messages would let key paths be correlated within its own traffic. Configured NAME evidence at both view and field level is still bound to the request's tenant and project. Residuals: a value flagged only by a non-ASCII key or a sibling value #8 does not recognize gains no candidate (that needs an agreed #8 vocabulary); encoded YAML/TOML/connection strings stay unparsed; a percent-escaped URL produces a decoded view that no longer parses as a URL and is therefore reported as an inspection gap; a caller-asserted format that disagrees with the content hint is run *in addition to* the hinted parse, which can add redundant evidence and reach the per-source candidate cap sooner; and one value may carry textual, structural and format evidence at once. The seam itself still does not merge candidates into one v1 classification unit, transform values, authorize release or prove exact-byte egress; both of those now exist beside it as described below (#10 wired in, accepted-v1 units composed). End-to-end evaluation remains pending.

Local #7 parsed-field composition evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): after locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check`, the focused `node --test test/parsed-field-composition.test.mjs` passes 37/37 and the full `npm test` passes 428/428; `npm run test:coverage` reports the compiled `normalized-detection.js` at 94.01% line, 89.16% branch and 100% function coverage, up from 86.24% line / 78.21% branch at the pre-integration head and from 93.33% line / 88.02% branch at the first composition head. The first behavioral suite was written before its implementation; the compileable seam that ignores #7 failed 20 of the 25 groups that existed at that checkpoint (`npm run build` passing, so those were behavioral failures, not missing exports). That chronology is author-observed development evidence: the published branch is a single squashed commit, so it cannot be re-derived from the published tree alone. Synthetic cases cover verbatim exact spans, escaped/percent/entity-decoded cover spans, decoded-view envelopes, byte-input `EXACT` versus `COVER`, request-bound path digests that reveal no key name and, for distinct `inputRef`s, cannot be correlated across requests (the digest is bound to the caller-supplied `inputRef`, so this is a trusted-caller obligation the seam does not and cannot enforce), `name`/`key`-sibling key hints in JSON, YAML and XML, keys that merely describe a credential, inherited credential ancestors, malformed JSON and an over-depth JSON parse as `PARTIAL` with whole-input opaque locations, unsupported XML kept `PARTIAL` while text scanning still found the credential, an unrecognized dotenv line reported at its own range, repeated identical fields, a structural credential candidate overlapping a format match inside one field, a 60-case generated variation over escaped/re-escaped/non-ASCII field spellings, a 40-case tenant/project scope variation, invalid and descriptor-lying `formats` inputs, the per-request field budget, the view unit cap, an empty field, and non-echoing failure paths including a throwing request getter. A bounded-work regression composes 4,000 parsed log fields under a 256 MiB heap with a 30 s timeout. All examples are synthetic `.invalid`/documentation-range values; no public #39 oracle, held-out case or private corpus was read or used. Threats addressed: encoded/obfuscated values (a credential reachable only after a parse decode), key-name disclosure through result metadata, cross-tenant configured-name resolution over the new field path, parser-state downgrade to "nothing found", and resource exhaustion from unbounded field composition. New attack surfaces are the trusted `formats` list (descriptor-snapshotted, bounded and validated once per index), the sibling-value key hint (payload text can only choose among #8's fixed credential subtypes, so it can over-cloak but never under-cloak) and the per-request field budget. This is congruence and bounded work only: the composition does **not** authenticate its caller, `inputRef`, formats, scope or configuration handle, does not bind a candidate to an interaction, does not compose v1 classification units, transform, authorize, verify final bytes or persist anything, and a `COMPLETE` result still authorizes no release and claims no clean content.

Local #7 correction evidence (same toolchain), after an independent review **blocked the first composition head** (local `0dace7c`, whose tree `9d3d0991` is byte-identical to the reviewed published `79eb68f`) with two defects that the review reproduced against that exact tree: a de-duplication key built on the occurrence-independent encoded envelope, so every repeated protected value inside one decoded view after the first was dropped with no marker while the result still read `COMPLETE` (a regression of #6 behaviour, which has no such key), and a caller `formats` list that *replaced* the root's content-hint parse, so `formats: ['LOG']` over a JSON document reported `COMPLETE` with an empty reason set and no candidate for a credential reachable only after the #7 JSON decode. Both were reproduced here through the public `dist` API, and the first was confirmed as a regression by replaying the same cases against base `522430e` (2/3/2/3/5 reported spans there, 1/1/1/1/1 at `0dace7c`). Six behavioural groups were added first and the new suite was replayed against `0dace7c`: `node --test test/parsed-field-composition.test.mjs` then exited 1 with 31 pass / 6 fail, and passes 37/37 after the correction. The fix keys de-duplication on the occurrence (view, representation, in-view span, #7 format, field span, path digest) and unions the caller's root formats with the hinted format instead of substituting them. The same pass also took up the review's non-blocking notes: a view the field budget has exhausted, and every later view it skips, are now recorded as uninspected (previously they were silently unscanned while only the exhausting view was marked); a #7 reason is no longer dropped on a `COMPLETE` parse, so a duplicate-key document stays `PARSER_JSON_DUPLICATE_KEY` and `PARTIAL` instead of reading as an unambiguous complete parse (`COMMENTS` is the single exempt reason, because comment text is inside the view text this seam scans in full); the path-digest claim above now states the trusted unique-`inputRef` obligation instead of absolute cross-request unlinkability; and the missing end-of-file newline in `src/normalized-detection.ts` is restored. Locked `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (428/428) and `npm run test:coverage` all exit 0 locally on the corrected head, and `git diff --check` is clean. Replaying the same probe sweep against the blocked head and this head shows the emitted reason/opaque vocabulary is a fixed identifier set that only grew (`PARSER_JSON_DUPLICATE_KEY`, `PARSER_DOTENV_OPAQUE_RANGES`, `PARSER_INI_OPAQUE_RANGES` added; none removed), a 9-format x 5-document sweep shows a requested format can no longer remove any finding the hint-driven parse would have made, and a 120-case generated sweep over Base64/hex/percent-encoded repeated values reports every planted occurrence. Bounded work is unchanged in order: a 900-section INI document with a two-format union takes ~124 ms here versus ~143 ms at the blocked head, and a 16 MiB opt-in LOG input ~13.3 s versus ~14.4 s; these are local wall-clock observations, **not** an SLA. This is a correction, not an approval: independent rereview of this exact head, remote CI, held-out recall, v1 classification merging, #13 transformation, #19 exact-byte egress checks and end-to-end utility remain outstanding, so #6, #7, #8 and #37 stay **open**. **Qualified in place 2026-10-02:** this records the issue states at that checkpoint. [#7](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/7) was subsequently closed at its bounded structured-parser scope only; [#13](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/13) transformation, [#19](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/19) at a real send point, and every held-out, scored and enforcement claim named here remain unimplemented and unclaimed.

A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** accepted-v1 classification-unit composition exists in [`src/classification-units.ts`](../src/classification-units.ts), and #10 configured sources are wired into the same #6/#7 seam; the interface, grouping rules and limits are in [the classification-unit contract](contracts/classification-unit-contract.md). With a trusted configuration handle supplied to the seam, #10's dictionaries, engineering templates and #7 key-path field hints now run over its raw and folded views and its parsed fields under the same tenant and project, with exactly the provenance every other source gets there (exact for text written verbatim in a string root, covering for folded or decoded text, the encoded envelope for a decoded view). Without a handle nothing from #10 runs and no reason is added; a forged or foreign handle is `PARTIAL` with an opaque location while the tenant-independent engineering-key rule still runs, exactly as it does inside #10 itself. The composition groups those per-occurrence candidates into units and runs the accepted `composeClassification` on each one. Grouping is deterministic and conservative: candidates from different scan lines (view representation, or #7 field path digest plus field span) never merge; within a line a candidate joins only the most recent group it *relates to* — the same occurrence span or a strictly crossing one — so repeated and disjoint occurrences stay distinct units with their own references even inside one encoded envelope, a configured name inside an email local part keeps its own unit, and one over-covering detector match can no longer swallow the entities inside it; a trusted whole-value field hint never merges in either direction, so unrelated entities across one long decoded field cannot silently become one whole-field identity. Candidates are placed by their occurrence span (the match offset inside the decoded value for a #7 field), not by the view span, because a decoded value reports its whole field span in the view. Every candidate lands in exactly one unit and every contributing detector record is retained on it verbatim, so no source is discarded in favour of a neighbour and a conflict between merged claims stays `UNRESOLVED` at the highest claimed sensitivity. Each unit carries its own opaque reference derived from the caller's `inputRef` and the unit index (the analogue of the reviewed #4 A/B defect: two same-value occurrences can never share a reference), its view/representation/field location, and either one contributor's original location or a covering/envelope union of them — never an invented exact offset; a unit that cannot state its location homogeneously reports `UNIT_LOCATION_UNRESOLVED` and is recorded as opaque. Units, evidence, candidate, reason and opaque-location counts are capped conservatively and a truncated unit is reported as opaque rather than dropped, and everything #6/#7 left uninspected is carried through. One minimal tenant/project-bound opaque trusted contact-sensitivity configuration (`createContactSensitivity`) supplies a configured sensitivity per PERSON subtype: because the accepted composer refuses to resolve a unit while any claim carries none and #37 emits none by design, the composed channel receives a record from producer `hylja.contact-sensitivity` carrying the candidate's own type and subtype plus that configured sensitivity, while the detector's own record is kept on the unit. Absent sensitivity keeps PERSON units `UNRESOLVED` with `MISSING_SENSITIVITY`; a foreign, forged or malformed handle supplies no sensitivity at all and makes the result `PARTIAL` with a fixed reason; tenant A's configuration cannot resolve anything for tenant B; a weaker configured contact sensitivity never overwrites a stronger configured detector claim (`CONFLICTING_SENSITIVITY`, unit unresolved at the higher value); and no payload `trusted` flag, guessed sensitivity, model authority or invented authentication is involved — the constructor consumes trusted integration inputs and authenticates nothing. No raw text, configured term, key name or source path appears in any serialized result, and `classification.ts`, `policy.ts`, `secret-detectors.ts`, `egress-sentinel.ts`, every adapter and #13 are unchanged.

Local #37/#10 classification-unit evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, on merged main `e6f1a46`; no remote CI, push or PR): after locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check`, the focused `node --test test/configured-units.test.mjs` passes **29/29** and the full `npm test` passes **633/633** (604 inherited plus 29 new), with compiled `classification-units.js` at 99.66 % line / 85.29 % branch / 100 % function coverage under the focused `--experimental-test-coverage` run and `normalized-detection.js` at 93.96 % / 91.03 % / 100 % across the focused seam suites. The only uncovered lines in the new module are two defensive `catch` blocks whose inner readers already catch; no threshold, budget, detector rule, claim, reason or registry entry of the accepted contracts was changed, added or retuned. RED chronology, author-observed: the focused suite was written first against a compileable stub that returned `FAILURE`, where 19 of the 20 groups then existing failed behaviourally and only the determinism group passed vacuously; the composed seams passed 37/37 and the seam's own 11 groups unchanged throughout. The behavioural tests are public-interface outcomes over synthetic `.invalid` values: two occurrences of one value become two units with distinct references, two occurrences inside one encoded envelope share the `ENCODED_RUNS` envelope and still stay two units, an equal-span configured term and configured name merge into one `CONFLICTING_SEMANTIC_TYPE` unit that retains both producers' records, a credential and the contact inside the same span stay one unresolved unit at `SECRET`, disjoint entities in one long field stay separate, a trusted #7 field hint becomes its own whole-value unit with exact or covering provenance and a request-bound path digest, generated documents and formats (plain text, dotenv, INI, YAML, JSON, XML, Base64) report each planted occurrence with the provenance its encoding deserves, absent/foreign/forged/malformed sensitivity leaves units `UNRESOLVED`, tenant A configuration yields nothing for tenant B, a weaker contact sensitivity cannot weaken a configured one, the unit and evidence caps stay opaque instead of truncating quietly, and getters, `Proxy` traps, symbol keys, `toJSON`, forged evidence claims and forged registries are refused with fixed codes that echo nothing. Bounded work is asserted without raising any threshold: an adversarial composed payload stays inside a generous 20 s ceiling and a generated repetition payload composes under a 256 MiB heap. Threats addressed: entity collapse across detectors (one over-covering match, one whole-value hint or one long field silently becoming one identity), cross-occurrence reference reuse of the reviewed #4 A/B kind, evidence substitution between detectors, cross-tenant resolution through the new configuration surfaces, invented provenance, and truncation reported as coverage. (Candidates the seam itself dropped inside a decoded #7 field value never reached this composition at all; the independent review that blocked this head reproduced that, and the correction paragraph below fixes the seam's identity for every source it runs.) New attack surfaces are the seam's `configured` handle, the trusted contact-sensitivity handle, the unit reference space and the per-unit evidence and unit caps; all four fail closed. Limits, stated rather than hidden: this is congruence and deterministic composition only — it does not authenticate its caller, `inputRef`, scope, context, handles or registry, does not bind a unit to an interaction or pin a classification digest for a reviewer, does not transform, cloak, authorize, persist, contact a sink or prove anything about outbound bytes, and a `COMPLETE` result still means nothing about clean content. #37 stays open for held-out recall, for the downstream #13/#19 text slice and for independent review; proposed decision 010 and the #65/#66/#68 drafts are untouched.

**Correction after an independent review (`REQUEST_CHANGES` at `45940a6`, report only; it approves nothing).** The review blocked this head on one P1 and reproduced it through the compiled public API: the seam's de-duplication identity keyed a #7 field candidate on the **view span** alone, so in a value #7 decoded (escaped, entity-decoded) every match in that value reported the same whole-field span and the second occurrence of one protected text was dropped with **no reason, no opaque location and a `COMPLETE` result**. That contradicts this slice's own per-occurrence claims and, because the newly wired #10 source flows through the same identity, it inherited the drop: `{"asset":{"note":"Northwind\u0020Synthetic AB and Northwind\u0020Synthetic AB"}}` yielded **one** configured candidate where the verbatim spelling of the same field yields two. Reproduced here at base `e6f1a46`, at `45940a6` and after the fix, with the same handle, tenant/project and budgets (in-value offsets per source, obviously synthetic values): configured `0..22` only at base and at `45940a6`, `0..22`+`27..49` after; contact `0..20` only at base and at `45940a6`, `0..20`+`25..49` after; host `0..22` only at base and at `45940a6`, `0..22`+`27..49` after; credential `9..33` only at base and at `45940a6`, `9..33`+`43..67` after. So the collapse is **pre-existing at base** for #8/#9/#37 and was newly inherited by #10; it is corrected here for every source the seam runs rather than absorbed into the #37 slice. The fix appends the **in-value occurrence offsets** to the field segment of that identity and updates the module's own statement of the invariant; a repeat of the very same occurrence is still suppressed, because identical view span *and* identical in-value span is the same occurrence read twice (asserted directly: one occurrence in one decoded field value is still exactly one candidate, and a trusted whole-value field hint keeps its own unit without duplicating the match). A #6 **decoded view** keeps its own accepted representation instead: identical disjoint copies of one run stay one view whose `ENCODED_RUNS` envelope names every outer occurrence, which is now asserted as a control rather than changed. RED was behavioural first: against the reviewed head's own build the three new groups (escaped-JSON configured repeat, entity-decoded repeat, and the same repeat for CONTACT/SECRET/INFRASTRUCTURE with a verbatim control) failed — 30 pass / 3 fail of the 33 groups then in the file, while the control group for a single occurrence passed — and all 35 pass after the correction. Also addressed from the review's non-blocking notes, none of which changed a threshold, cap, claim, reason or registry entry: the detection reader now **refuses unknown keys** inside any record (an extra `trusted: true` in a claim is a whole `INVALID_DETECTION` rather than a silently carried field), detection and opaque-location reasons are copied only when they are uppercase code tokens and otherwise become `DETECTION_UNREADABLE_REASON`/`UNREADABLE_OPAQUE_REASON`, a `COMPLETE` detection carrying opaque coverage is downgraded with `UNINSPECTED_COVERAGE`, `src/classification-units.ts` gained the trailing newline every other source has, and the contract now states the #10 handle gate explicitly (the tenant-independent engineering-key rule still runs only when a handle is supplied — a declared follow-on, not a silent behaviour change to requests that configure nothing) and adds a consumer checklist covering the fresh-`inputRef` obligation, not cross-pairing a detection with another scope, reading `unit.classification` rather than `detectorEvidence[].claim`, and deciding every unit. Local evidence at this corrected head (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8; no remote CI, push or PR): locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0, the focused `node --test test/configured-units.test.mjs` passes **35/35**, the full `npm test` passes **639/639** (633 at `45940a6` plus the six new/updated groups), and the focused coverage run over the three seam suites (84/84) reports compiled `classification-units.js` at 99.67 % line / 86.99 % branch / 100 % function and `normalized-detection.js` at 94.04 / 91.07 / 100; the only uncovered lines in the new module are the two defensive `catch` blocks whose inner readers already catch. This is a correction, not an approval: independent re-review of this exact head, remote CI, dependency audit/SBOM, held-out recall, the #13 transformation and the #19 exact-byte egress slice all remain outstanding, so #37, #6, #7 and #10 stay **open**, and proposed decision 010 with #65/#66/#68 remain proposed. **Qualified in place 2026-10-02:** this records the issue states at that checkpoint. [#7](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/7) was subsequently closed at its bounded structured-parser scope only; [#13](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/13) transformation, [#19](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/19) at a real send point, and every held-out, scored and enforcement claim named here remain unimplemented and unclaimed.

A bounded, **PROPOSED and NON-ENFORCING** #114 overlap composition draft exists in [`src/overlap-composition-draft.ts`](../src/overlap-composition-draft.ts), with its proposed contract in [the #114 overlap composition and rewrite draft](contracts/overlap-composition-draft.md). It is **not** wired into classification v1, policy or any adapter, it selects no treatment and it creates no mapping: it composes *already emitted* detector findings into overlap regions over the accepted #3/#6/#7 seams, and it is review evidence for #114 and its owners #3/#7/#8/#37/#40/#68, not an accepted contract. Its relationship to the accepted per-occurrence seam merged on main ([`src/classification-units.ts`](../src/classification-units.ts), [its contract](contracts/classification-unit-contract.md)) is explicit: a unit is one occurrence or crossing overlap and stays the input to policy, a region is the closure of *overlapping* spans and is the input to a rewrite, so #13 must decide every unit **and** satisfy every region. Fusing them would either hide a nested credential inside a wider unit or a wider obligation inside a nested unit. Its three rules are deterministic and deliberately narrow. Overlapping findings in one coordinate space become one region whose obligation set is the **union** of its members' claims, with no score, confidence, arrival order or detector priority anywhere in the input or the result, so a wider PERSON, URL or engineering span can never displace a nested native SECRET finding (accepted #6 candidates carry no score at all, and a producer-supplied confidence is neither read nor emitted). Every accepted finding keeps its own member reference, evidence reference, producer, input reference, view location, #7 field location and original envelope, whether or not its span was consolidated, and `retention` counts received, retained, rejected, uncomposed (`received - represented`, so an unreadable or unrecognised finding is counted there) and duplicate evidence *before* consolidation. Identical, containing, contained, partially intersecting and duplicate spans are one region with explicit relations; spans that touch without overlapping are two regions plus an adjacency relation and are never merged. Equal contradictory spans (accepted v1 already reports a credential that is also a host at one span) stay one region holding both claims, and each region carries the ordered **accepted #3 composition** of exactly its members, so a conflicting region is `UNRESOLVED` with the SECRET floor and non-reversibility retained; past the accepted composer's per-channel bound of 256 the region composes `UNRESOLVED`/`UNKNOWN` and the floor is then carried only by `obligations`, so the rewrite refuses such a region. Repeated identical field values keep one region per occurrence with its own field span and source-bound path digest, and two occurrences of one value inside one decoded field value are not duplicates because the identity carries the accepted #6 in-value occurrence offsets. Escaped, percent- and entity-decoded field values and compatibility-folded, invisible-character and non-BMP matches report a covering region, never an invented exact span. Overlaps a single coordinate space cannot express are typed refusals that keep both sides rather than merges: `CROSS_VIEW_OVERLAP` when two normalized views' original envelopes cover the same bytes, `COORDINATE_SPACE_FOREIGN` when two original-location families in one view also overlap, and `OPAQUE_LOCATION_OVERLAP` when a region falls inside a #6 opaque location, so a parser or budget gap never reads as inspected-and-clean. The accepted #6 candidate sources are enumerated from the #6 type, so #10's merged `CONFIGURED` findings compose and are rewritten like any other, and a future #6 source fails this module's build instead of being dropped at runtime; a source it still does not know is reported as `UNKNOWN_FINDING_SOURCE`, counted unrepresented and refused by the plan as `INCOMPLETE_CANDIDATE_SET`. The rewrite is the single path that already exists: `planOverlapRewrite` replaces the trusted #7 field value covering each region with one opaque caller-supplied constant and refuses the whole plan when any region is uncoverable (`REGION_NOT_FIELD_BOUNDED` for protected text in a JSON key or a span crossing fields, `ENCODED_VIEW_NOT_REWRITABLE` for decoded views, `REGION_EVIDENCE_INCOMPLETE` for evidence the composer could not read, `SOURCE_AMBIGUOUS`/`SOURCE_HAS_COMMENTS`/`SOURCE_NOT_COMPLETE` for sources #7 itself refuses, `REPLACEMENT_ECHOES_ORIGINAL` for a constant carrying a window of a covered original). `applyOverlapRewrite` then takes the composition as well as the plan and treats the plan as a reviewable artifact rather than an authority: it re-derives the plan from the composition, the exact source bytes, the format and the plan's own constant, refuses any mismatch (`PLAN_BINDING_MISMATCH`), rebinds the source digest (`STALE_SOURCE_BINDING`), re-parses, re-verifies the round trip independently of #7 and applies a proportional surviving-run post-condition (`ORIGINAL_SPAN_STILL_PRESENT`, or `POSTCONDITION_BUDGET` when the check cannot complete). Provenance carries digests, spans, evidence references and a rewritten-field count only, so no reversible alias can be created here.

**Integration with merged main, and correction after an independent review of `3d0ce02`.** This branch merges `origin/main` `6e4bc65` (PRs #105–#112/#115 plus the parser deadline work) with `--no-ff`; the only conflict was `docs/plan.md` at the milestone anchor, resolved by keeping both sides verbatim, so every merged main paragraph, status and correction note is preserved unchanged and `src/normalized-detection.ts`, `src/structured-parsers.ts` and `src/classification-units.ts` are main's. `git merge-tree` before the merge reported `docs/plan.md` as the single conflicted path, and the combined tree typechecks and builds. An independent review of `3d0ce02` returned `REQUEST_CHANGES` (report only; it approves nothing) and its findings were reproduced against the unfixed draft on that combined tree in committed RED checkpoint `47557d3`: `node --test test/overlap-composition-draft.test.mjs` was then 32 tests with **23 pass / 9 fail**. The reproduced blockers were that merged main's #10 `CONFIGURED` source was rejected as `INVALID_FINDING` while the plan still reported `PLANNED`/`REWRITTEN` and the configured customer term survived; that `applyOverlapRewrite` re-established neither the echo rule nor the region-to-field binding, so a tampered plan could put a truncated protected original into the bytes or rewrite an unrelated field and report it protected; and that the no-surviving-original check compared whole field values rather than the documented eight-unit window, so a surviving window of a covered original read as `REWRITTEN`. All three are fixed and pinned by behavioral regressions. The non-blocking findings were taken up too: the duplicate identity now carries view id, representation, original envelope and the #6 in-value occurrence offsets, so distinct occurrences are no longer labelled duplicates; relation reporting is bounded by `MAX_RELATION_PAIRS` member pairs (a real bound, replacing an unreachable distinct-kind count); `COORDINATE_SPACE_FOREIGN` now requires an actual overlap; the above-256-member case states and tests the composer ceiling; one invisible-character group used a literal `\u200b` and asserted nothing, and both files now use a real U+200B so the fold path is exercised; and the contract now states that `REWRITTEN` carries no completeness signal when #6 hit its own candidate cap, and that the source bound is in UTF-16 code units. Main's cooperative parse deadline is respected rather than inherited by accident: a forwarded host is validated with #7's `isParseHost` and passed through, and a source that exhausts the deadline fails closed as `SOURCE_NOT_COMPLETE`.

Local #114 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8) on the combined tree at `7c735cc`: after locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck`, the two focused files pass **45/45** (`node --test test/overlap-composition-draft.test.mjs test/overlap-characterization.test.mjs`), `npm run check:fixtures` and `git diff --check` exit 0, and one full `npm test` passes **751/751** with 0 fail and 0 skipped. Threats addressed: winner-takes-all overlap merge displacing a nested credential, detector or arrival order dropping protected evidence, duplicate consolidation losing a producer's evidence, a decoded or stale location being treated as an exact one, a partially rewritten message reading as protected, and a candidate-only merge standing in for a final-byte check. Limits: this is a draft over accepted seams, not an enforcement boundary, and it does not choose treatments, resolve obligations that cannot be combined, rewrite decoded views or non-field text, bind to an interaction, authenticate a caller, hold a stream back or record an effect; its surviving-run post-condition is literal, so case/separator/encoding variants remain #19's job and two values sharing half of one of them refuse conservatively; `REWRITTEN` is not a completeness signal for findings the caller never received; whole-field replacement is deliberately coarse; the four outcome kinds are asserted separately and a #19 catch is never credit for a merge or a rewrite. **Escaped JSON and quoted dotenv source-to-rewrite coverage for the #114 draft, 2026-10-02 (test-only; base `d3c3772`, the published #124 commit).** The escaped-value side of the same path is now committed evidence too: six new groups in [`test/overlap-composition-draft.test.mjs`](../test/overlap-composition-draft.test.mjs) take a value whose source spelling decodes to a planted original through the whole chain, where the earlier groups stopped at a classification record. Every case is built from the real `detectNormalizedCandidates`, `parseStructured`, the draft's own public functions and #19, over this repository's obviously synthetic `.invalid` originals. An escaped JSON value (`{"password":"syn\u002dpass\u002d4f2b9c7e1d.invalid","region":"eu"}`) is the main case: the source holds no verbatim planted original, #7 resolves the field's decoded value to it while the source slice is the longer escape spelling, #6 emits two view-level and two field-bound candidates whose `field.verbatim` is `false` and whose original is `ORIGINAL_COVER`, the composition is one region whose span is that field's own parsed value span (13-50) with `CONTAINING`/`IDENTICAL`, the `SECRET` floor and `reversible: false`, the plan is `PLANNED` on exactly that span with a 16-hex source-bound `fieldRef` and every member's evidence reference, the apply is `REWRITTEN` to the exact bytes `{"password":"****","region":"eu"}`, an independent reparse returns the same key paths with the constant, re-detection reports no `SECRET` candidate, the provenance digests equal the SHA-256 of the input and the constant, #19 `BLOCK`s the source on `HIGH_RISK_PATTERN` **and** `KNOWN_ORIGINAL_DETECTED` naming only the registered reference `planted.secret.1`, and #19 `ALLOW`s the rewritten bytes with `release` byte-equal to them. The quoted dotenv analogue (`DB_PASSWORD="syn\-pass\-4f2b9c7e1d.invalid"`) takes the same path end to end: covering `ORIGINAL_COVER` over the field's value span (13-42), `PLANNED`, `REWRITTEN` to `DB_PASSWORD="****"`, key path and quoting preserved on reparse, `ALLOW` with byte-equal `release`, and a record set carrying no planted value, no escape spelling and no key name while the detector rule id does. The same supported quoted path is also exercised with the Unicode and normalization cases that move offsets: a non-BMP prefix and suffix keep UTF-16 offsets (span 13-42 for a 29-unit value), and decomposed (`U+0308`) and zero-width (`U+200B`) spellings — which the source does not hold verbatim — report `FOLDED` plus `RAW` with a covering original, rewrite anyway and still release. The edges are asserted rather than assumed: dotenv backslash escapes decode only inside double quotes, so a bare value keeps them, stays verbatim and `ORIGINAL_EXACT`, and cannot take the constant at all (`UNENCODABLE_REPLACEMENT`); a quoted escaped credential beside an ordinary bare line is `PLANNED` and still refused by the apply, so a plan is a reviewable artifact and not a promise; the same decoded original behind a percent-escaped run is a #6 decoded view and the plan refuses `ENCODED_VIEW_NOT_REWRITABLE` although its raw field region is coverable, with the apply then refusing the refused plan; and the dotenv bindings refuse `STALE_SOURCE_BINDING` for a one-character edit, an appended line and a removed escape and `FORMAT_MISMATCH` for `JSON`, returning `{status, reasons}` only — no bytes, no planted value, no key name — while the honest plan still rewrites the same source afterwards. **These groups are coverage of existing behaviour, not a RED checkpoint for a contract change**, and no implementation defect was found: the first run of the edited file was 45 pass / 3 fail, and all three failures were wrong expectations in the new assertions themselves, corrected against measured behaviour — a `"PASSWORD"` text search that matched #8's own uppercase `PASSWORD` **subtype** rather than a key name (fixed by using the non-subtype key `DB_PASSWORD`, which makes the key-name claim discriminating), a guard character class that also matched the dotenv source's own newline, and an over-strong claim that the percent-escaped case's decoded-view region carried the credential floor when its own claim is the host-shaped one. This correction pass changes **no** source, test, constant, contract constant or accepted module: the delta is prose in `docs/plan.md` plus one bounds sentence in the draft contract, so the behavioral evidence stands as measured rather than re-argued. The changed focused file passed **33/33** at `6ac1ee6` in this worktree, and the independent review re-ran the focused pair at that same head and recorded **45/45**. Prose is not evidence for prose and no test greps documentation text, so nothing was added or changed here; after `npm run build` the focused file was run once more at this head and passes **33/33** (0 fail, 0 skipped), and `npm run typecheck`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `git diff --check`, the relative-link and anchor pass over the two touched documents and the authority check (the draft still imported by no `src/`, `scripts/` or `evaluations/` file, decision 010 still **proposed**, the #65 taxonomy, #66 information-model and #68 transformation-semantics drafts still unaccepted and unwired) are all clean. The full suite and any coverage run were deliberately not repeated. A fresh independent exact-head review of the corrected head is outstanding; no approval, adoption, custody, push, PR or issue action is claimed.

Bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #9 infrastructure detectors exist in [`src/infrastructure-identifiers.ts`](../src/infrastructure-identifiers.ts). They find:
- IPv4/IPv6 addresses, subnets and ports (zero-padded, bracketed and embedded-IPv4 forms) and MACs;
- DNS names anywhere, including URL paths, nested URLs and `user@host`, with IDN labels;
- URLs with their host and port;
- POSIX, Windows (either separator), UNC and `file://` paths, including colon-separated lists and quoted paths with spaces;
- AWS ARNs and accounts, Azure subscriptions and resource groups, GCP projects (resource names and keyed) and service accounts, storage buckets (URI, host and path forms) and keyed cloud IDs;
- keyed single-label hosts and environments.

Each candidate carries derived fidelity facts: IP version and scope (documentation ranges only at /24 or /32 and narrower, reserved and IPv4-mapped handled), well-known port service, URL scheme (custom schemes reported as `OTHER`), path style and name class. Candidates carry v1 evidence without sensitivity or values. Precision limits:
- a dotted name counts as a host with host context (`@`, `//`, `:port`), with three or more labels including a digit or hyphen, or with a known gTLD, private/cluster suffix (`svc`, `consul`, `lab`, ...), IDN or two-letter TLD when it is not a code chain. A code chain is a name where every label before the TLD is a common receiver (`self`, `window`, `config`, ...), a single letter, or camelCase/underscore-shaped; `process.env.HOST` and version strings are excluded;
- remaining misclassifications: unusual gTLDs without context are missed, and code chains whose receivers are not in the list (`server.address.host`, `readme.de`) are treated as hosts;
- dotted names ending in common file extensions are not hosts;
- unquoted paths with spaces, relative paths, nested single-label URL hosts and device/session/correlation IDs are out of scope.

More than 256 candidates in one field fails closed (`FAILURE`). The golden check is a development measurement, not held-out evidence. The direct detector API remains field-local; #6 composition scans its normalized views and composes #7 parsed fields.

Initial categories: PERSON (including EMAIL/PHONE), CREDENTIAL_OR_SECRET, HOST, IP/URL, CUSTOMER/PROJECT. PERSON/EMAIL/PHONE must have an explicit candidate source and synthetic acceptance cases; shadow judgment is not that source.

Bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #10 configured candidate sources exist in [`src/configured-candidates.ts`](../src/configured-candidates.ts). A trusted configuration is bound to one tenant and project behind an opaque handle. It holds folded term dictionaries (customer/partner, project/contract, business-confidential), engineering ID templates in a restricted, backtracking-free template language (asset, drawing, part, item, document, functional-location, PLC and SCADA subtypes) and key-path field hints for #7 fields. A configuration from another tenant or project, or a forged object, never matches; the result is `PARTIAL`. Results and configuration errors never contain configured text. An independent generic engineering-key rule (`part_number=`, `plc_tag=`, ...) is labelled separately from tenant evidence. Templates are deterministic per start. A variable-length placeholder must be followed by a literal outside its class or by the end, and there can be at most three of them. Matches start only at identifier starts, and spans extend forward over the rest of the identifier (`.`/`:` continuations, never `/` or `://`). Dictionary matching folds NFKC and case (including `İ` and `ß`), and runs with invisible characters both removed and treated as spaces. It is longest-first and non-overlapping, so a term overlapping an earlier longer match is not reported separately. The generic rule also reads Swedish keys, and requires a digit for part, drawing, item, document and asset values. Only configured sensitivity lets v1 composition resolve. The fixtures are development measurements; the sources now run inside the #6 composition seam over its views and #7 parsed fields under a trusted handle, and the composition seam composes them into v1 units as described below.

Bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #7 parsers exist in [`src/structured-parsers.ts`](../src/structured-parsers.ts). They cover JSON (with spans), dotenv/shell assignments, INI, URLs (including compound `jdbc:` schemes, `;key=value` parameters after the authority, bare and `;`-separated query parameters and key=value fragments), connection strings (key=value and URI forms) and log header/key=value lines.
