A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #11 semantic-judge shadow seam exists in [`src/semantic-judge-shadow.ts`](../src/semantic-judge-shadow.ts) with a narrow TypeSafe/System One protocol adapter in [`src/typesafe-system-one-adapter.ts`](../src/typesafe-system-one-adapter.ts); its interface, bounds and threat map are in [the shadow contract](contracts/semantic-judge-shadow.md). It supports only what the local, synthetic-only version can honestly support:
- versioned question sets of the three documented System One kinds (`noul`, `choice` with 2..255 options, `score` with 2..10 levels), question ids constrained so a value cannot ride in as metadata, and a `propose` block that may name a semantic class only;
- local request minimization: the raw candidate value and every neighbouring fragment are validated and then replaced by request-local ordinal placeholders (`cand-01`, `nbr-01`) plus closed non-secret context codes, so raw values cannot reach the request, its bytes, the record or any diagnostic; the request is branded, so an adapter cannot translate a forgery; `request.digest` covers the minimized view and is therefore content independent;
- strict validation of the canonical answer shape and of the documented provider body, with every unknown field (a `treatment`, `sensitivity`, `trust`, `instructions` or `authorization` field), surplus key, non-`local-synthetic/*` served model, oversized response and out-of-range or mismatched probability refused rather than passed through;
- two independent controls for time: caller cancellation aborts the judge's own work, the deadline only stops waiting, so a genuinely late answer is observable (`settlement.late`) and is discarded instead of being merged into the final record. Missing, invalid, abstained, refused, timed-out and cancelled judgments are each an explicit outcome with closed reason codes; failures add reasons and never produce an empty-looking success;
- versioned shadow metadata: question-set and judge versions, requested and served model, request digest and bytes, per-question answer with the provider distribution and confidence, latency, response bytes, deadline and token usage. A Score level is derived locally by argmax and marked `levelIsLocallyDerived`, because the provider documents that score levels are not numerically calibrated;
- evidence only: every record is `authority: 'NONE'` with `advisory: { suggestedTreatment: 'NONE', effect: 'IGNORED_NO_AUTHORITY' }`, and a claim carries `semanticType` plus `confidence` and nothing else. Shadow evidence alone leaves #3 composition `UNRESOLVED` (no detector evidence, no sensitivity); a deterministic `CREDENTIAL_OR_SECRET`/`SECRET` floor survives a conflicting shadow claim, and #4 policy still denies the external sink with `semanticRecommendation: 'KEEP'`.

There is **no external execution path**. `EXTERNAL` is refused before a request exists with `UNSUPPORTED_EXECUTION`, `MISSING_DESTINATION_POLICY`, `MISSING_ROUTE_BINDING` and `MISSING_BYTE_VERIFICATION`, because core destination policy for the judge sink, an authenticated binding to the real routed judge destination and an independent #19 check of the exact serialized outbound bytes do not exist yet. The only executable judge is a module-issued data script that cannot open a socket, read a credential or reach a network, and it must declare a `local-synthetic/*` served model. Caller flags (`safe`, `synthetic`, `trusted`, `destinationPolicy`, `routeBinding`, `byteVerified`) are refused as unknown option keys: a flag is not an authorization primitive, a fake local transport is not authentication or interception, and no model-generated token text acquires control trust. The candidate-kind vocabulary contains no credential kind, so a #8 finding is not representable judge input.

Local #11 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8; red checkpoint `c59b677` passed fixture guard/build and failed 21 of 23 focused behavioral groups against compileable permissive stubs that echoed raw values, answered every request and applied `KEEP`): after the correction, locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (416/416, including the 25 new shadow and adapter groups), `npm run test:coverage` and `git diff --check` all exit 0, with compiled `semantic-judge-shadow.js` at 99.25% line / 85.71% branch / 98.70% function coverage and `typesafe-system-one-adapter.js` at 96.98% / 82.14% / 100% (the adapter line figure in this checkpoint record is the measured one; an earlier 96.95% did not reproduce on this head). Synthetic cases cover exact minimized-request and wire-body shapes, content-independent digests, nearby protected context with tokenized candidates, raw-candidate omission, refusal of caller flags and forged configuration, hosted/moving model labels, timeout, cancellation before and during a run, a discarded late result, oversized and malformed responses and metadata, per-answer invalid answers, an abstention, an adversarial `keep_original_value` suggestion, credential-floor preservation through #3 and #4, cross-tenant replay refusal, 64 seeded tenant/ref/bounds variations, closed-code-only diagnostics under a throwing `Proxy`, and a source check that neither compiled module can import a network, process or dynamic-evaluation API. Remaining uncovered lines were forward-compatible paths (a duplicate answer id, a transport rejection and defense-in-depth re-checks) that the data-only local double cannot reach. This is local contract evidence only.

Independent review of that head requested changes on two P2 findings, both reproduced here against the compiled artifact of the exact pre-fix tree and fixed by behavioral regressions that were red before the fix. **B1:** `runShadowJudgment`'s `record` promise could **reject**, and the local script's answer-key bound (64) was wider than the parse bound (`expected.size + SHADOW_MAX_QUESTIONS`), so a module-issued judge with surplus keys (or surplus keys plus `responsePadBytes`) threw `MALFORMED_RESPONSE` inside the record builder; awaiting only `settlement`, the documented way to observe late work, left that rejection unhandled and Node 22 exited 1. A script answer value that threw while being measured propagated the caller's own exception message out of the same call. **B2:** after the candidate had been read through the validating snapshot, `runShadowJudgment` read `candidate.ref`, `neighbors` and `context` again outside any guard, so a time-varying `Proxy` either threw `INVALID_REQUEST` synchronously out of the entry point (no `{ record, settlement }` at all) or let an unvalidated second value reach the record's attribution. The correction: `record` and `settlement` now resolve for every input and nothing throws out of the entry point; the caller's input is read exactly once, with the request, its bytes and `candidateRef` all taken from that one validated snapshot; malformed identity uses the fixed safe `unbound` placeholder and the deterministic floor is echoed once it was read; a local script is answered within `SHADOW_MAX_SCRIPT_ANSWER_KEYS`, so a module-issued judge can no longer outrun the parser. The data-only contract was hardened rather than merely caught: script answers are copied through own enumerable data descriptors into frozen JSON values under depth/key/string/byte budgets, so a getter, `toJSON`, function, symbol, BigInt, cycle, sparse array, non-finite number, custom prototype or over-budget value is refused at creation **before any caller code runs** (asserted with invocation counters), size measurement uses a bounded walk of Hylja's own instead of `JSON.stringify`, and a caller's `Proxy` trap that throws becomes the context's own closed code (`INVALID_REQUEST` for configuration and requests, `MALFORMED_RESPONSE` for responses) in the core and in the adapter, so no caller exception message leaves either module. Two silent configuration no-ops were removed rather than kept: a bare `propose.semanticType` on a labelled question is refused instead of abstaining forever, and a malformed served model is `MALFORMED_RESPONSE` instead of `INVALID_REQUEST`.

Local evidence for that correction: the corrected test file is **red** against the pre-fix tree, extracted with `git archive 660c4b2` into a disposable directory, built there and never merged back. Reproducing that measurement needs one disclosure: the corrected test file imports five bound constants that do not exist in the pre-fix module, so against the unmodified pre-fix tree it fails at link time and **0 of 23** shadow groups run; to measure behavior, only those five constants were **appended** (425 bytes, append-only, verified) to the pre-fix compiled artifact in that disposable tree, with no other change to it. Measured there: **5 of 23** shadow groups fail — `the record promise always resolves...` (with its settlement-only child process), `a local script is copied bounded JSON data...`, `a time-varying or hostile caller object is read once...`, `a provider payload is judged as a response...` and `an oversized, malformed or non-local response...` (whose non-finite-score case now belongs to the factory) — and **18 pass** unchanged. The adapter half needs no shim: against the unmodified pre-fix tree it is **7 pass / 1 fail** of 8, the failure being `a hostile body or request is a closed code...`. All of these are green after the correction. After the correction, locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (422/422, including 31 shadow and adapter groups; the same tree without the two new test files is 391/391) and `npm run test:coverage` all exit 0, with compiled `semantic-judge-shadow.js` at 98.50% line / 88.56% branch / 97.87% function coverage and `typesafe-system-one-adapter.js` at 97.13% / 83.22% / 100%. New synthetic cases cover surplus and oversized scripts, a settlement-only child process that must exit 0, accessor/`toJSON`/symbol/BigInt/cycle/hole/non-finite/custom-prototype/depth/key/byte refusals with zero caller invocations, a snapshot copy that later caller mutation cannot change, single-read candidates and neighbours/context under time-varying `Proxy` objects, throwing traps at every input and option position, an abort API that must be present, multi-byte and lone-surrogate byte counts equal to the exact UTF-8 JSON length, and `null`/boolean/string answer values refused per answer rather than as configuration. Remaining uncovered lines are forward-compatible and defense-in-depth paths the data-only local double cannot reach: a duplicate answer id, the run-entry catch, a `judgePayload` guard, a transport rejection (`ADAPTER_THREW`), the cancelled-after-judged ordering rule, and the record-path and size/parse guards. This is local contract evidence only.

The inherited intermittent full-run failure reported for the earlier head (415/416 in one run, with no further failure in that reviewer's 17 later runs) remains **UNLOCATED and UNREPRODUCED**, and it is **not closed** by anything below. No failing group name, assertion or captured output for that run exists in this repository or in either review artifact, so its cause is unknown and is not claimed here; the older #7 note above is left as recorded. What was measured on this corrected head, and only this: ten consecutive full `npm test` runs on an otherwise idle 12-core host all exit 0 at 422/422, so the failure did not occur there; and wall-clock `bounded-work budget` assertions **do** exist in these suites and are load-sensitive, because deliberately inducing CPU load (24 busy loops on those 12 cores) made them fail — focused `node --test test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` four times gave one or two failing groups per run (group 13 `jo x jo x jo 6152 ms` and group 26, both against 5,000 ms thresholds), and one full `npm test` under the same load failed five groups, every one a wall-clock assertion, across configured-candidates, contact-candidates, normalization and egress-sentinel. Those runs were **induced**, they demonstrate that contention is a **possible** explanation available for such a failure, and they identify nothing about the original uncaptured run. Scope limits, stated rather than hidden: no threshold, budget or detector rule was raised, retuned or otherwise touched; this is a development-workstation observation and **not** a service SLA; a CI runner can be slower or busier than any laptop; and a diagnosis still needs a host and run that actually reproduces the original failure. This seam's own new tests add no wall-clock assertion, so they add no new timing exposure.

Remaining gates, all still open and none attempted here: a real hosted transport requires the three missing controls above plus destination-profile policy for the judge sink and a separately reviewed exception for any protected context in a judge request; #19 must inspect the exact serialized judge bytes before any such send. No Jev account, API key or endpoint was contacted, no third-party binary was installed or executed, and the protocol facts come from source inspection of the primary TypeSafe documentation and the published `@typesafe-ai/sdk@0.6.0` tarball, re-verified at any future integration. The #39 frozen/held-out comparison of a judge against the deterministic-only baseline is **not** done and is **not** claimed: #39 has no human approval, no approved rubric, no blind custody and no v0 freeze, so no benchmark score, no held-out comparison and no semantic-accuracy, over-cloaking, latency/cost or downstream-utility result exists from this seam. The local judge is a deterministic script, so nothing here measures model behavior, and the seam is not wired into #6 composition, #7 fields or a real adapter send point.

A bounded, **NON-ENFORCING** #12 contextual re-identification shadow measurement seam exists in [`src/reidentification-shadow.ts`](../src/reidentification-shadow.ts), with its interface, bounds, counting semantics and limits in [the #12 contract](contracts/reidentification-shadow.md). It sits on the reviewed #11 seam and changes no #11 vocabulary, no classifier v1 contract and no proposed draft; its only import is `./semantic-judge-shadow.js`, and it drives that seam's existing `runShadowJudgment`, so judge timeout, cancellation, refusal, malformed, oversized and partial responses are reused as #11 defines them rather than re-implemented, and there is no external execution path (only `IN_PROCESS`; `EXTERNAL` and any `synthetic`/`trusted`/`safe`/`destinationPolicy`/`authorization` option is refused before a request exists). The shipped **development** corpus is 39 structural records (33 evaluated cases, 6 unlabeled population background) across 16 synthetic sites, covering all eight quasi-identifier dimensions, and it contains **no text of any kind**: a record is an opaque site reference plus abstract `.invalid` handles, every record states `directNameReplaced: true`, and in **this corpus** every handle of every record occurs in at least one other record, so identification comes from the combination rather than from a value or a name — a property asserted over the whole corpus, and a property of the shipped corpus only, since a caller-supplied corpus is not required to satisfy it. Labels (15 `REIDENTIFIABLE`, 18 `NOT_REIDENTIFIABLE`) are planted independently of the local model and compared against it at runtime. The deterministic local model groups records by their whole combination over **(dimension, handle) pairs** and counts **distinct sites**, not records, so two records of one site are still re-identifying while a combination shared by two or three sites is not; keying on the dimension as well as the handle prevents two different facts from merging into one cell and under-claiming. The bounded versioned question set (`hylja.reid.shadow@1`, `choice`/purpose `REIDENTIFICATION_RISK` plus a utility `score`) is issued through #11 and is **branded**: only the set this module issues may be scored, because a caller-minted set carrying the same `id` and `version` with different question text would be reported under metadata that does not identify the questions asked; the per-row request digest binds the question text independently. Because #11 minimizes every value the request is content independent, asserted rather than asserted-about: a corpus whose handles are all permuted produces byte-identical request digests. The report counts `trueFlag`, `falseFlag`, `miss`, `correctSafe` and `abstained` separately for the local model, the judge and their fail-safe combination; a false-safe judgment is a **miss**, never a pass and never a safe verdict; an abstention **escalates** to `RISK` rather than relaxing, and the over-flag cost of that escalation is reported as `escalatedByAbstention` instead of being absorbed. Every row and the report itself are `authority: 'NONE'` with `advisory: { applied: false, effect: 'IGNORED_NO_AUTHORITY', requiresDeterministicPolicy: true }`; generalization recommendations carry `implemented: false` plus derived equivalence-cell facts, are ordered by population cardinality with rationale names that say so, and exclude any dimension whose single value every record already shares, because generalizing a universal value cannot widen a cell. There is no generalization lattice and no predicted post-generalization cell size, nothing is generalized or transformed, and no recommendation reaches classification, policy, a sink or an adapter.

An independent review of the first #12 head `c286061` returned REQUEST_CHANGES with two blocking evidence-accuracy findings and four non-blocking ones; no protection defect, fail-open path or held-out/hosted call was reported, and the reviewer's independent re-derivation of every count matched the report. All six are fixed at this head with behavioral regressions rather than documentation edits alone: **(F1)** the corpus precondition is now true rather than asserted — three background records carry the `CUSTOMER_TYPE`, `ROLE` and `DEVICE_CLASS` values that had been singletons, and a test asserts the sharing property over the whole shipped corpus (39 records, 6 background) instead of over one case; **(F2)** the `toJSON` cases the contract and this note claimed are now real cases in the hostile-spec table — on a record, a quasi-identifier and the corpus object, plus an inherited `toJSON` — each refused with invocation counters proving it never ran; **(F3)** a cell is keyed on `(dimension, handle)`, so a dimension-swapped handle pair stays two `RISK` cells instead of merging into one `COMMON` cell (the dimension-blind key under-claimed, and the seeded generator's property oracle inherited the same conflation); **(F4)** only the issued question set may be scored, with `isIssuedReidentificationQuestionSet` exposed and a substituted set carrying identical `id`/`version` refused; **(F5)** recommendation rationales are renamed to what is actually computed (`LOWEST_POPULATION_CARDINALITY_DIMENSION`, `OTHER_DIMENSION_IN_UNIQUE_COMBINATION`) and universal dimensions are excluded; **(F6)** the dead `GROUPS` and `SITE_COUNT` constants are deleted and `tenantRef` is now a bounded opaque token (128 characters, the lowercase token shape #11 uses for configuration), so an email, path, uppercase or scheme-shaped tenant reference and an over-long token are each refused. Red evidence for the behavioural findings was captured against the exact `c286061` head first — 12 of 13 probe checks failed there, including three singleton handles, `reid-dim-a` reported `COMMON`, a substituted question set accepted, `reid-case-10`/`reid-case-33` recommended a universal value, five value-shaped tenant references accepted and both dead constants present.

Local #12 evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, this worktree, no push/PR/remote CI): locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund` (0 vulnerabilities), `npm run typecheck`, `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm test` (618/618, including 28 groups for this seam), `npm run test:coverage` (618/618; compiled `reidentification-shadow.js` at 100.00% line / 93.03% branch / 100.00% function coverage, and 98.40% / 92.88% / 96.35% across all files), `git diff --check` (clean), `npm run check:sbom` and `npm run check:release` (17 synthetic cases) all exited 0. The groups cover the issue's negative case (`reid-case-01`: a unique rare geography/date/process/project combination, flagged `RISK` by the local model and in the combined column), the false-safe miss, refusal, timeout-with-late-settlement, missing, malformed and oversized answers, a forged judge, a judge identity that cannot be read, hostile and widened corpus specs (accessor, three `toJSON` placements, an inherited `toJSON`, throwing `Proxy`, function value, raw-looking/email-shaped/routable handles, invented or absent labels, unknown and repeated dimensions, duplicate ids, over-bound lists, the record cap at and above the limit) with invocation counters proving no caller code runs, the whole-corpus sharing property, the dimension-aware cell key in both directions, question-set substitution, the universal-dimension exclusion, the tenant-reference shape, 16 seeded generated corpora with both unique and shared cells, count identities, corpus isolation under unrelated population growth, tenant-bound digests, the fixed report shape with no treatment/authorization/sensitivity/trust/scope field anywhere, and a source check that the compiled module cannot import a network, process or dynamic-evaluation API. This is a **development** measurement on a synthetic structural corpus: it is not held out, it is not a frozen protocol, no #39 held-out case or blind oracle was read, generated or scored, no hosted judge was contacted, and the scripted always-risk and always-safe judges are doubles, so agreement or disagreement here is **not** evidence of a judge's semantic re-identification ability. The fix pass re-measured only the changed source; no threshold, budget or detector rule was raised or retuned, and the inherited intermittent full-run failure at line 212 above remains unlocated and unreproduced. Remaining gates, all still open and none attempted here: an independent review of this corrected head (the `c286061` review is a REQUEST_CHANGES verdict, not an approval, and this note does not substitute for the human or independent acceptance the process requires); independent #39 blind/held-out custody and a frozen protocol before any score; a separate, explicitly reviewed promotion decision before any re-identification or generalization signal may affect a release, treatment or destination (issue #12 "no direct policy authority"); destination-profile policy, an authenticated route binding and a #19 exact-byte check before any external judge send, plus a policy-authorized representation of a combination if a hosted judge is ever to see one; real population, linkage/adversary and utility evidence for any uniqueness model; and remote CI. No real customer, employee, infrastructure or credential data was used anywhere, and this seam forms no enforcement boundary.

A bounded, **OFFLINE, NON-ENFORCING** #27 champion/challenger replay and shadow comparison substrate exists in [`src/champion-replay.ts`](../src/champion-replay.ts), with its interface, trust boundaries and declared limits in [the replay contract](contracts/champion-replay-contract.md). It compares two independently provided deterministic evaluators over locally generated, obviously synthetic development cases (`.invalid`/`example.invalid` names and the 192.0.2.0/24 documentation range; only `DEVELOPMENT_FAMILY_IDS` families are accepted, so a blind family is refused) and reports only harness-owned observations: a privacy-marker outcome computed from the emission against a marker the substrate derives from the case key, utility-expectation outcomes, harness-measured latency, error counts, cost fixed at `UNMEASURED`/`OFFLINE_NO_COST_SIGNAL`, a keyed-scope human-review recommendation with `authority: 'NONE'`, and per-case disagreements. Immutable versioned bundle references must equal the independently pinned `{id,version,digest}`, otherwise every case on that side is `REJECTED` and the evaluator is never called. A deliberately permissive challenger that releases the planted marker is recorded `MARKER_EMITTED` whatever it claims, and it cannot replace the champion: `promotion` and `productionEligibility` are always `UNAVAILABLE` with the five external gates enumerated (authenticated production registry, human approval, held-out scoring, independent bundle integrity, production egress evidence), no candidate package/model execution or hosted transport exists (`shadow` is offline, in-process, `authority: 'NONE'`), unknown keys such as `promote`/`authorization` are refusals rather than flags, and fixture expectations, evaluator self-reports and optional #67 annotation evidence stay advisory labels — unknown, conflicting or invalid labels are explicit and cannot alter any outcome. Malformed/unknown/missing results, tool errors, foreign-tenant or out-of-scope-family cases, duplicate keys, stale or substituted bundle digests, deadlines and cancellation all stay explicit `UNKNOWN`/`REJECTED` results, never a pass and never a fallback to the other side; `runChampionReplay` never throws or rejects. Records bind minted ordinal case refs, run/scope/case pseudonyms keyed by the trusted per-run HMAC key, the bundle reference and closed reason codes only, so no case text, emission, tenant label, case key or evaluator exception message is exposed and no global digest can correlate two tenants or two runs; every report reference is shape-valid on both paths (the invalid report carries all-zero digests), latency is reported as measured rather than clamped because a deadline bounds waiting for an async answer rather than synchronous work, and the marker-verification refusal is `CASE_MARKER_EXPECTATION_MISMATCH`. Four independent reviews have blocked this work, each time with blocking defects reproduced here against the exact reviewed tree before any change. The first, of `44a71aa` (REQUEST_CHANGES), found **(B1)** that the containment decision ran through the mutable `String.prototype.includes`, so one assignment inside `evaluate` made the harness record six genuinely released markers as six withholdings with empty per-record reasons and zero disagreements, and rejected legitimately planted cases as unplanted, and **(B2)** that listener detaching happened before `settle`, so a structurally valid signal whose `removeEventListener` throws left the run permanently unsettled with an unhandled rejection. The second, of the corrected `d37c142` (REQUEST_CHANGES), confirmed both closed but found **(N1)** that the report deep-freeze stopped silently part-way through a maximum-size report although the contract and this paragraph stated the report was deep-frozen, and **(N2)** that `Array.prototype.includes` and `RegExp.prototype.test` were called live but neither captured nor observed, so poisoning either one admitted a flagged result and spec instead of refusing them and fabricated a run reason. The third, of `f899551` (REQUEST_CHANGES), confirmed those closed but found **(N4)** that `Promise.resolve` was still live, so replacing it left an armed deadline timer whose callback threw an uncaught exception about a second **after** the report had returned, ending the caller's process. The fourth, of `8a846e5` (REQUEST_CHANGES), confirmed those closed but found **(N6)** that the sentence I had written to bound a pre-load mutation bounded it wrongly, in the permissive direction: an always-true `Array.prototype.includes` before import emptied every reason list, rejected every case as a duplicate with its code dropped and left both sides recommending `NO_FINDING_IN_THIS_SUBSET` with no run reason and no tamper code, an always-true `RegExp.prototype.test` before import let a malformed bundle reference through into the report, and an always-0 `String.prototype.charCodeAt` made every redacted emission read `MARKER_EMITTED` — none of it visible to identity comparison, because the captured and live references are then the same object. All four corrections keep scope and human gates unchanged and only make the failure direction conservative: the observation runs through intrinsics captured at module load and applied with the captured `Reflect.apply`; any replacement of an observed intrinsic is reported as `HARNESS_INTRINSIC_TAMPERED` on the run and on every record; a bounded wait drops its armed timer and settles explicitly if anything in its executor throws; the opaque-token and digest shapes are scanned by code unit so the module never calls a pattern implementation; the reason vocabulary, the closed vocabularies and the case-key reservation use `===` scans; the reason list is de-duplicated with no array method at all; and, for a realm mutated **before** the module was imported, every captured primitive is asked one known answer once at load and both entry points refuse explicitly with `HARNESS_REALM_UNTRUSTED`, so no report is produced from a realm this module cannot vouch for. Local evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8; no push, PR or remote CI): the committed compileable red checkpoints `b4af03d`, `ef39fcb`, `b84ee14` and `c23a0f8` failed 6 of 22, 5 of 24, 1 of 25 and 1 of 26 groups against `44a71aa`, `d37c142`, `f899551` and `8a846e5` respectively, and after the corrections the focused `node --test test/champion-replay.test.mjs` passes 26/26 with compiled `champion-replay.js` at 99.42% line and 96.69% branch coverage, the two uncovered paths being the self-test's catch and the bounded wait's executor guard, neither reachable in a clean realm; the groups cover the permissive leaking challenger, a stale and a content-substituted pin, twelve malformed/hostile/oversized/missing/throwing result shapes, a `Proxy` whose descriptor trap throws, deadline and pre-/mid-flight cancellation with a settlement-only child process that must exit 0, hostile and un-interrogatable cancellation signals under a bounded race, poisoned and observed intrinsics in both directions including cross-side, a poisoned lookup intrinsic retaining every refusal with no fabricated reason, a replaced promise constructor leaving no armed timer and no uncaught exception behind it, a replaced array map leaving the corpus reference unchanged, four pre-load realm mutations refused explicitly by both entry points with the reason list intact, a 128-case report in which all 256 records and every aggregate are frozen and uneditable, foreign tenant/project scope, safe keyed references with same-input determinism, bounded corpora, a 32-seed generated sweep, astral-plane and lone-surrogate handling, capped disagreement output, prototype and result-mutation attempts, and the compiled module's import graph with poisoned `fetch`/`WebSocket` globals. The whole-run budget is not exercised by the suite and was verified once by a bounded local experiment (128 cases x 2 sides at ~130 ms: ~30.1 s wall, `PARTIAL`, 26 `RUN_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` records, all 256 records frozen) — a development observation, not an SLA. The marker check remains **exact UTF-8 substring containment**: an encoded, folded, split or recombined release reads as `MARKER_WITHHELD`, a declared coverage gap and not a privacy guarantee — #19's canonical views remain the independent control at a real send point — and observation integrity is not sandboxing: a realm that answers the load-time known answers but is broken in some other way, the iterator protocol over module-local tables, the shared canonical-JSON helper's internals (so a record digest is not tamper-proof), the `node:crypto` bindings, memory and the caller itself all remain outside what this module can see, which the contract states as limits. This is development behavior of an offline comparison aid: it authenticates no caller and no pin, holds no production registry, performs no held-out scoring, exposes no blind data, tunes nothing, and establishes **no** scored comparison, promotion, rollback, release or production eligibility claim; every counted number is a development observation of locally generated synthetic cases, not a held-out or production metric, and independent review of this head, remote CI, held-out scoring and an authenticated promotion path all remain open.

The local #88 hardening tests use only planted synthetic originals and canaries. `npm test` passed 364/364 tests, including compressed byte escapes, Latin-1/byte strings, short final default-`xxd` rows, a signed container beneath tentative raw deflate, opaque escapes, advisory IDs with Base32-like path prefixes or attached suffixes, and advisory or generic IDs after repeated raw/escaped slashes, plus ordinary mixed-case and underscore-separated advisory paths, exact-length digest and SSH ed25519 wire-key exemptions, short known originals under Base64/hex/raw deflate and DER-shaped PEM, quoted conditional credentials and deterministic ordinary-text controls. This is development evidence; independent final-head approval, remote CI, held-out evaluation and a real pre-send adapter boundary are not claimed.

The follow-on **closed** the #88/#19 residual **declared-form** gap for representations whose own layout rebuilds the payload with nothing else in the group: labelled chunks, declared `+`/`/`/`>` joins, JSON arrays of labelled objects and decimal/Node `Buffer` byte arrays are now rebuilt, decompressed, container-checked and matched. That sentence was true only for label names outside the hex alphabet until the hex-alphabet correction below; the measured limits it leaves are listed with the known limits rather than folded into this claim. Independent review of the published head `2f48013` (`REQUEST_CHANGES`, report only; it approves nothing and is not amended here) found two blocking gaps in that claim, both recorded below: an encoded-looking field interleaved between labelled chunks defeated the series reading and released a planted original and canary, and two fail-closed assertions in the tests-only RED checkpoint `0bb3c66` were re-scoped rather than satisfied. Its tests-only RED checkpoint `0bb3c66` was replayed independently at that exact head: fixture guard and build passed and `node --test test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` reported 47/52 passing with five behavioural failures (labelled chunks, label order and mixed syntax, container-shaped byte arrays, bounded work and the privacy-safe result). Local #88 follow-on evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): after `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, focused `node --test test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` (56/56 groups), `npm test` (401/401 tests) and `git diff --check` all pass, and `npm run test:coverage` passes 401/401 with compiled `egress-sentinel.js` at 99.32% lines / 94.89% branches / 100% functions (the uncovered lines are the same pre-existing ones as before this change). The new cases are public-interface decision and reason-code assertions over planted synthetic values only: a seeded generator dresses one compressed planted note as labelled fields, JSON bodies, JSON arrays of labelled objects, separator joins and decimal arrays across chunk widths 3–32 and several label, quote, gap and separator spellings; adversarial spellings add list markers, prose between parts, dashed and zero-padded labels, single quotes, query strings, escaped and percent-escaped separators, quoted and trailing-comma byte arrays; controls keep ordinary labelled configuration, numeric arrays, timestamps, RGB/matrix lists, Buffer digests, paths and arithmetic allowed, plus a generated 400-section report that must stay allowed within a work bound; three overload payloads with distinct values exceed the reconstruction budget and block with `SENTINEL_BUDGET` or `UNINSPECTED_CONTENT`, while a moderate 2,000-group message stays inside it. New attack surfaces are the extra per-message reconstruction budget and its fail-closed code, the label/gap/width bounds above, and the extra per-view scans; each reader is skipped in one scan when its necessary character is absent, so messages without a separator, label or `[` pay almost nothing (measured within noise of the pre-change baseline on the 1 MiB adversarial timing payloads). Residuals are the decode-only limit and the bounds listed under known limits: unknown uncompressed binary inside a declared form, escaped bytes split across labels, `=>` separators, over-long labels, and non-numeric label order are not reconstructed, and a 94 KB message of 5,000 identical labelled groups blocks opaquely after roughly 0.9 s on this host, within noise of the pre-change baseline for that shape; the pre-existing many-small-streams shapes remain the slow ones.

**Declared-reconstruction budget contract (disclosed: one assertion was changed, not satisfied).** `0bb3c66` asserted, in the same test, that a 5,000-field labelled flood, 5,000 eight-byte decimal arrays and 5,000 distinct ` + ` joins all `BLOCK` ("Beyond the declared reconstruction budget the message is uninspectable, never presumed clean"). The implemented budget is **16,384 distinct reconstructions per message**, with 8 MiB of joined value and 8 MiB of joined characters, chosen *above* the RED probe's 5,000. Replaying the unmodified `0bb3c66` test file against the implementation head reports 50/52 with exactly two failures, and each of the three fail-closed payloads was re-measured here at the base `522430e`, at `5edaffc` and at this head: the 5,000-field flood **passes** (identical values are read once and the message blocks `OPAQUE_EMBEDDED`) and the 5,000 distinct-join assertion **also passes**, blocking `SENTINEL_BUDGET` with no `release` — at the base as well, so it was satisfied before any of this work and never needed re-scoping. Only the 5,000-eight-byte-decimal-array assertion reads `ALLOW` (152,455 bytes released) at all three of those heads, and it is the one assertion that was **replaced** in the head test file with a 17,000-payload overload sized above the new budget. It was **not** satisfied, and the contract was changed rather than the code. The two failures of that replay are therefore not an array and a join assertion: they are the disclosed unsatisfiable canary assertion in the label-order test and this decimal-array assertion. The reasoning behind the change is a correctness property, not a budget increase: every reconstruction inside the budget is inspected in full and never sampled, so a message whose 5,000 reconstructions are all clean legitimately inspects clean, and refusing it would report a limit as a verdict. Both directions are still asserted in the suite — a 1,024-reading declared-series message is reconstructed rather than refused, and above-budget payloads block with `SENTINEL_BUDGET`, no findings and `release === undefined`. No timing or size threshold was raised in this correction, and the inherited `configured-candidates` timing budget is untouched.

**Unsatisfiable truncated-canary assertion (a separate correction).** The same RED file asserted the canary on the 9-chunk prefix of a 13-to-18-chunk gzip payload. That prefix is 54 gzip bytes and inflates to at most 18–23 bytes of a 93-byte note, while the canary token begins at offset 27 of the note, so `CANARY_DETECTED` is unsatisfiable there by construction (measured with the module's own inflate settings). The head test asserts the planted original for that one truncated payload, and the canary wherever the payload is complete, with the reason recorded at the assertion. That assertion, not a reconstruction budget one, is the first of the two failures the unmodified RED file reports against the implementation.

**Correction of the interleaved-field gap (`f601349` tests, `b8f109b` and `af901a7` implementations).** A labelled group is now also read as one declared label series at a time — the fields sharing a label name, numbered (`part1 … partN`, `p2=…`, dashed, zero-padded or case-varied) or not (`data`, `value`, `note`, the keys of an array of labelled objects) — in text order and in numeric-label order, plus first-wins and last-wins where a series repeats a label number, and the metadata beside the dominant series as its own reading. Selection is by label name only: a group of n labelled fields yields at most n/2 series readings, each costing only its own size, so this is linear in the group and never a search over subsets. Every earlier reading of the group is kept, so no view or known-original match that existed before is suppressed, and unrelated metadata keeps its own independent inspection. A field whose label comes from the hex alphabet (`face: 1a2b3c4d`, a dump offset column) is a value written before a colon rather than a key: it belongs to no reading, and it no longer ends the group either, because one such field anywhere in a declared sequence used to defeat every reading of it.

Every join is now charged even when the budget drops it as a duplicate, too short or too long, because uncharged work is work nobody can bound; the new work ceiling fails closed like the two existing bounds. Local evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): the tests-only checkpoint `f601349` reports 57/59 against the unchanged #19/#88 source with two behavioural failures (interleaved ordinary field; duplicate, ambiguous and unrelated labels), replayed again at the implementation head as 59/59. Measured against the published head: 1,200 seeded dresses of one compressed planted note go from 1,122 blocked to 1,200 blocked with 0 regressions and 0 new blocks on 1,500 generated ordinary payloads; 300 dresses per decoy class (no decoy, word, decimal, hex, Base64-shaped) go from 252–300/300 to 300/300 each; an interleaved decoy whose own label is hex (`d0`, `cafe`, `deadbeef`, `bad`, `f00d`, `abc123`, `eff`) goes from 1–2 of 4 chunk widths blocked to 4 of 4; a payload under a second declared name, that shape plus a decoy, an index label beside a `data` label plus a decoy, and a repeated label number carrying a different value go from 134–151/191–196 to every case blocked; `hexdump -C`, `xxd -g1` and plain `xxd` dumps, C arrays, digest lists, offsets and hex-labelled configuration stay allowed. 1 MiB prose, `key=value`, labelled, hex-dump, numeric-array, declared-join, entity, escape and report shapes stay within noise of the previous head (worst 1.6 s in that set at both heads), and the reconstruction-count overload the new readings create is refused with `SENTINEL_BUDGET` instead of being released. Measured remaining limits, unchanged by this correction and stated rather than closed: a sequence whose own parts are missing, replaced or truncated is not reconstructed (116/186, 136/186 and 82/196 blocked over 200 seeded widths each, mostly from opaque counting of the surviving prefix), a two- or three-chunk prefix of a longer payload usually releases that partial payload (27/190 and 42/190 blocked), and a payload whose parts alternate between two declared names *and* has an unrelated field interleaved is reconstructed only when some other reading happens to line up (4 of 6 chunk widths blocked), because recovering it in general would mean choosing a subset of names. The join-work ceiling is a guard on reader fan-out, not a tuned threshold: the largest joined volume measured was 2.2 MiB of characters (on a shape that stops earlier anyway with `UNINSPECTED_CONTENT`) and an ordinary 560 KB Kubernetes-shaped manifest joins 658 KiB, so no message within the 1 MiB input limit was found to reach 8 MiB and the ceiling's trip is not covered by a test. Coverage of compiled `egress-sentinel.js` at that checkpoint was 99.34% lines / 95.01% branches / 100% functions (404/404 tests), up from 99.32% / 94.89% / 100%, with the same pre-existing uncovered lines and none added; the current figures are in the hex-alphabet paragraph below. No live transport, benchmark score, production enforcement, approval or release claim follows, and no private, held-out or #39 oracle material was read or used.

**Correction of the hex-alphabet asymmetry (`5edaffc` review; this head).** Dropping *every* hex-alphabet label also dropped any declared series the sender happened to name with hex letters, so the same bytes blocked under `part1 … partN` and released the planted original and canary with `release` under `face1 … faceN`, `dead1 … deadN`, `bad1 …`, `add1 …`, `faded1 …`, `cafe1 …`, `beef1 …`, `cab1 …`, `deed1 …`, `decade1 …` and `facade1 …`. The exclusion is now scoped by the field's own shape instead of by its alphabet: a hex-labelled field joins a reading when its numbers declare one complete series under one label name — every member numbered, the numbers distinct and covering a complete run from 0 or 1 with no gap — and it is then read like any other declared series, in text order and in numeric-label order, charged by the same per-message budget as every other reconstruction. A dump offset column (`face: 1f8b0800`, `d10: 00000000`) never has that shape, so it still joins no reading and does not split the group. The readings are additional rather than substitutional: every reading the group had before keeps exactly the fields it had, so no view or known-original match is suppressed and the metadata beside the dominant series keeps its own independent inspection. Measured (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8, one gzip of one planted note, `known` registered): across eleven label names × two chunk widths (6 and 8) × three value forms (Base64 counted from 1, Base64 counted from 0, hex), 24 of the 66 payloads released the planted note at `5edaffc` — all eleven names at width 6, nine of eleven at width 8 (`decade` and `facade` were caught there only as opaque by the plain chunk reader), plus the 0-based and hex-chunk spellings at both widths — while the identical bytes under `part`, `data` and `zzz` blocked at all of them. At this head all 66 block with `CANARY_DETECTED` and `KNOWN_ORIGINAL_DETECTED` and no `release`, together with the interleaved-field, syntax, order and two-series cases of the new public-interface regression (70 blocking assertions at two widths), which fails at `5edaffc` (`'ALLOW' !== 'BLOCK'`) and passes here. Ordinary engineering text is unchanged: hex-labelled hex values (`face0 … face39`), hex-labelled 16-byte values, hex-labelled word values, hex-labelled decimals, `hexdump -C`, `xxd` and `xxd -g1` rows, a labelled offset column beside labelled parts and an offset column written as `face:`/`d10:`/`d20:`/`d30:` rows are `ALLOW` at `5edaffc` and at this head, as they are at the base; a 105 KB hex-labelled manifest stays `ALLOW` and two near-cap labelled messages return the same `UNINSPECTED_CONTENT` as at `5edaffc` (1.42 s against 1.43 s, and 1.56 s against 1.58 s on this host, single measurements). The unscoped alternative — treating every hex-labphabet label as a key — was not taken: it would drop the offset-column exclusion that ordinary dumps and offset-bearing logs rely on, and it would add the group's whole field list to every general reading, which on a 1 MiB hex-labelled configuration is the difference between roughly one and roughly nine megabytes of charged join work against the 8 MiB ceiling. Gates at this head (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): the fixture guard, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, focused `node --test test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` (60/60 groups), `npm test` (405/405 tests) and `git diff --check` pass, and `npm run test:coverage` passes 405/405 with compiled `egress-sentinel.js` at 99.57 % lines / 95.24 % branches / 100 % functions, up from 99.34 % / 95.01 % / 100 % at `5edaffc`; the uncovered lines are the same pre-existing ones (one digest-length table entry and the stream gate's defensive branches in the compiled module) and none was added. Replaying the unmodified `0bb3c66` test file against this head still reports 50/52 with the same two failures, so this correction satisfies no replayed assertion and breaks none.

**Correction of the independent review's blocking finding and its three notes (this head).** An independent review of `e2ad93b` (`REQUEST_CHANGES`; it approves nothing and is not amended here) confirmed the mandatory dump-whitespace blocker closed and found one reproducible silent release created by the interior read: it spent its ceiling in text order and then skipped every later exempt run, so eight ordinary digest lines placed *before* a compressed planted original and canary inside a digest-shaped run turned `BLOCK` into a clean `ALLOW` with `release` bytes, non-monotonically, at any larger count. Three things changed.

*Exhaustion is now a refusal, not a silent pass.* Runs the opaque count excuses only by a coincidence of length are *obliged*: `MAX_INTERIOR_BYTES` (32 KiB of them per message) is read in full, distinct values only, or the message is uninspectable (`SENTINEL_BUDGET`). Every other exempt run — a short run, a declared reconstruction, a digest the message named under an algorithm context, a public certificate body — keeps the exemption this module has always published and is read within `MAX_OPTIONAL_WINDOWS`, because refusing those would refuse ordinary `go.sum` and certificate traffic, which is what the exemption is for. Obliged runs are read first, so a declared context cannot starve them. The sweep takes `INTERIOR_SWEEP + 1` starting offsets per window against both signature-less formats, uses RFC 1951's reserved block-type bit to rule out three of every four deflate offsets, keeps only a decode that is text (a speculative read adds text and nothing else, so a binary wrong-offset decode must not displace the real stream behind it), and no longer recurses or follows a stream chain, because the sweep already covers every offset of the run. The interior read also has its own `MAX_INTERIOR_INFLATED` decompression ceiling: a long list of compressible digests decodes by chance at many offsets, and sharing the message's own 4 MiB refused messages this module has always read and released for finding nothing.

*The multi-gigabyte resident memory is a per-attempt allocation this module can size.* A synchronous zlib call allocates one output buffer per call at Node's default `chunkSize` of 16 KiB, and a call that *decodes nothing* keeps that buffer until the runtime can run again: measured, a synchronous batch of 24,000 failed calls raises `arrayBuffers` by 16,148 bytes per call at that default, by 4,037 at 4,096 and by 1,049 at 1,024, independent of `windowBits`. **That is the high-water mark of one synchronous batch, not a per-process retention** — after two event-loop turns and a forced GC, `arrayBuffers` and `external` return to within 0.1 MiB of their baseline while RSS does not come back, so RSS is not evidence of retained reachable objects — and **no permanent-runtime-leak claim is made here**. Every decode attempt names its own 1 KiB `DECODE_CHUNK`, which makes a decoy-signature scan's high-water mark sixteen times smaller and costs nothing measurable on successful decodes.

*Two published claims were wrong and are corrected above.* The ASCII column is dropped only where the row marks it (`  |…|`); an unmarked column whose cells are hex of the row's own width is read as cells by the complete-row reading, which is what surviving a sender-controlled gap costs. This head's earlier attempt at that paragraph claimed the column's bytes "do not add to the opaque count" and that the extra reading is "never a suppressing one". **Both were false**, and the independent review of the two previous heads reproduced it: an ordinary dump of ordinary prose carrying an unmarked `dead beef cafe f00d …` column was refused `OPAQUE_EMBEDDED` where the base allowed it, a planted dump with the same column reported `OPAQUE_EMBEDDED` and lost the precise known-original and canary reasons, and at a larger row count the same planted payload was **released with `release` bytes** where the base blocked precisely. The column is sender-chosen and the outcome was measured in all three directions.

**The release is now fixed in the reader rather than described.** A dump row declares its own byte offset and consecutive rows advance it by exactly one row, so the row width is knowable without trusting the column separator. The complete reading is now bounded per row by that agreed width, falling back to the cells before the column when a row spells more cells than the width and to its own cell count when it spells fewer (a short final row). A doubled space or tab *inside* the field adds no cell and leaves the row's offsets and cell count untouched, so the complete reading still spans the field: the three B1 triggers (a doubled space, a tab, an absent `|` marker) are unchanged and pinned.

*Reconciling the per-row cell bound, which moved from 32 to `MAX_DUMP_CELLS = 64`.* It is **not** a work, view, depth or output budget and it guards no reconstruction: the reconstruction is bounded by the offsets-declared row width and the line by the caller's unchanged 400-character ceiling. What it decides is only whether a line is read as a dump row at all, and that is not neutral — the old 32 counted the **ASCII column's cells** toward the decision, which is below a 32-byte `xxd -c 32` row plus its column and below any `xxd -g1` row whose column spells more than 16 cells. A row the dump reader declines is not inert: it falls back to the byte-pair reader, which reads the column's pairs as data and interleaves them into the reconstruction. Measured with the row width in place: at 32, three of the five measured column spellings — two-character hex cells, four-character words and two-character words — still released a planted original and canary with `release` bytes; at 64 all five block precisely, marked or unmarked. 64 covers every standard dump row up to `xxd -c 64` plus its column. Declining a still wider row is not a release path either: rows of 64, 80 and 128 cells, which exceed the parsing bound or the line ceiling, are reconstructed by the byte-pair reader and block precisely, and that is pinned by test rather than assumed.

Every column spelling measured — `deadbeef cafe …` (four-character cells), `dead beef cafe …` (two-character cells), the same two written as word-like groups, and dots — now blocks a planted original and canary precisely with `|` present and `|` removed, and ordinary dumps carrying any of them are `ALLOW`, which is the base's behaviour. The interior-read residual is restated from what is measured rather than from a single sample.

Evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8): `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` exit 0. **Two** of the four behavioural groups this change added — `core audit B2: …` and `core audit: every decode attempt sizes its own codec window …` — are red at the source of `e2ad93b` and pass at this head, and they fail for the finding they name (`'ALLOW' !== 'BLOCK'`, and a 20 MiB synchronous high-water mark against the head's 1 MiB). The other two — `core audit: a speculative interior window …` and `core audit: the measured ASCII-column reading …` — are **characterisation, not regression**: both pass at `e2ad93b` as well, because the behaviour they pin already existed there. This change's earlier entry claimed all four were red; that was wrong and is corrected here. The `N4` accounting defect those groups describe (`speculative` not forwarded through `expand`'s chain and recursion paths) is a source change, and it is covered by the interior-window group as a no-coverage-loss check rather than by a red group. Measured on the 60 pre-existing groups whose test text is byte-identical to the base, one locked run each: base `e6f1a46` 60/60 in 42.3 s at 1.92 GiB peak RSS; `e2ad93b` 64.7 s at 4.16 GiB as the reviewer measured; the final head 60/60 in 62.7 s at 2.82 GiB, which the reviewer's own single measurement of this same content (72.4 s, 2.78 GiB) agrees with within run-to-run variation, so the chunk change removes a third of the memory the review measured and the interior read's cost is wall-clock rather than resident growth. That wall-clock cost is the price of reading every offset of every obliged run and is stated, not hidden: the module's own 1,000-line bare-hex digest-list control is allowed and takes about 2.8 s on this host, inside its existing 5,000 ms bound, and that message's high-water mark does not decay across repeated checks (~238, 219, 177 and 207 MB on four consecutive checks, against a decaying ~74, 47, 25 and 17 MB at the base). The multiplier is this change's own attempts, bounded by `MAX_INTERIOR_BYTES`, not the runtime's. The `MAX_INTERIOR_BYTES` refusal itself is defence in depth and, for realistic content, does not fire before #6's own decode budget: 500, 900 and 1,000 bare sha256-hex runs are allowed and 1,023 and above are `UNINSPECTED_CONTENT` at this head and at the base, on the same boundaries. The optional-window ceiling is likewise not a release path: a compressed note written in either of the other exempt syntaxes — as an `h1:`-named digest or inside a public PEM body, the two classes this module publishes an exemption for — is still refused with a reason and no `release` bytes at 0, 16 and 200 `go.sum`-shaped decoys, so nothing in the optional class releases either; what the ceiling bounds is the *precision* of that refusal, not whether it happens. `test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` is 72 groups and all 72 pass at this head: one locked `node --test` of the whole file reports 72/72 in 96.3 s at 4.15 GiB peak RSS (the review measured the same 72 groups at 92.9 s and 4.26 GiB on its own run; single measurements, consistent within run-to-run variation, not verified figures), and the same file in eight sequential partitions of nine groups reports 72/72 in 0.3–31 s per partition at 0.02–1.66 GiB, with every group also passing on its own. The whole-file figure is the whole file's *aggregate* over many adversarial payloads inside one process, and a synchronous batch's decode buffers are only released once the runtime can run again (see the memory limit above), which is why the partitioned and per-group runs are the ones quoted as the bound on one payload. The other 30 test files plus the three evaluation suites are 544/544 in one locked run (14.4 s, 1.54 GiB peak RSS; the review measured the same 544 in 13.2 s at 383 MiB, again consistent within run-to-run variation). The independent review of this feature's previous head recorded `REQUEST_CHANGES`; its blocking finding and its three notes are addressed above, and nothing here claims that review's approval or any successor's. This is local contract evidence over synthetic in-process checks: not independent review approval, remote CI, held-out recall, enforcement, tenant isolation or a real pre-send adapter boundary. The sentinel remains `NON-ENFORCING`, no adapter calls it at a send point, `#13` transformation does not exist, and the gates below are unchanged.

**#88/#19 residual correction: block-scalar credentials, an interior read of exempt runs, and the hex-dump whitespace blocker.** Three changes, each with a behavioural regression that is red at the head it corrects and green at this one. (1) *YAML block scalars in credential assignments.* `password: |` followed by an indented literal or folded body is a credential-shaped assignment whose value is on the following lines, so the single-line pattern could not see it: with no known original registered, `password: |\n  synthetic-block-scalar-value\n` was `ALLOW` while the quoted, plain and same-line forms all blocked. #7 already returned `UNSUPPORTED_YAML_SCALAR` for the same construct, so this was the independent #19 rule, not a parser failure. The indicator itself (`|`, `>`, with chomping and explicit-indentation digits, an optional trailing comment, real or JSON-escaped line breaks, nested and quoted keys) is never a value and never exempts the body; the same exemptions as the single-line forms apply (whole references and masks, a key-echo shorthand, a `PWD` path), and the per-block line and byte bounds are the module's other per-field bounds, so a longer block is read up to the bound and reading resumes at the next line. The controls that must stay allowed are pinned by test, including `password: |0` (not a header), `password: |\tvalue` (value on the header line), a pipe or arrow that is prose, and `user_password_hint`/`myapikey` as different keys. (2) *An interior read of runs the opaque count does not count.* A compressed stream behind other bytes inside a short run that is not a recognized digest, id or public key, or inside a run of a DER-shaped public PEM body, is found by a bounded read of starting offsets rather than by counting: brotli and raw deflate carry no signature to scan for. Its exact ceiling, its measured residual and why raising it is not free are in the known limits above; the substantive correction is that the read no longer switches itself off for the whole message when there are many such runs, which it did before, and that a decode which is not text is not evidence and can no longer displace the real stream behind it. (3) *Hex-dump row whitespace (the independent core audit's blocking finding B1).* `dumpLineHex` split an unmarked row's hex field at the first run of two or more spaces, so a single sender-controlled extra space or tab inside that field truncated the reconstruction to the first cell; the truncated reading still spanned the whole dump block, so it displaced the complete one, the gzip stream carrying the planted original and canary never inflated, and the message was `ALLOW`ed with `release` bytes. Reproduced at `770fb0a` and at its ancestors: `xxd -g1` byte pairs and default `xxd` two-byte groups, with the extra space at row 0, 16 or 32, as a tab, and `hexdump -C` with its `|` markers removed, all released; the same dump verbatim blocked. Each row is now read in every reading of its own hex field — the complete row first, then the row cut at the first run of two or more spaces — and `dumpRuns` keeps one block per reading, so neither displaces the other. The offset column is still dropped, ordinary text with offsets is not a dump, and a row whose own cells change width is read only up to that change. **This paragraph's claim, recorded here at `e2ad93b`, that the ASCII column is still not bytes including a hex-looking one, was wrong; it is corrected in the known limits above and pinned by test.**

Evidence at head `e2ad93b` (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8), kept as recorded and superseded by the paragraph above: `npm run check:fixtures`, `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0, and `git diff --check` is clean. The two behavioural groups it added (`core audit B1: …` and `core audit: the interior read …`) fail at the source of `770fb0a` — `'ALLOW' !== 'BLOCK'` on both — and pass at `e2ad93b`. `test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs` was 68 groups and all 68 passed there, verified in eight sequential partitions under the shared test lock (9, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9 and 5 groups; each partition 0–20.7 s and 0.02–1.96 GiB peak RSS) and then each group individually, because one process holding the whole file exceeded 2.5 GiB of retained native decode memory. The other 30 test files plus the three evaluation suites were 544/544 in one locked run (14.4 s, 1.6 GiB peak RSS). The review of that head found the interior-read exhaustion silent release and an inaccurate ASCII-column claim in it; both are corrected above. As there, this is local contract evidence over synthetic in-process checks, not independent review approval, remote CI, held-out recall, enforcement, tenant isolation or a real pre-send adapter boundary.

### 2. Reversible identity and vault

Goal: encrypted scoped mappings, entity consistency, brokered USE/DISPLAY operations, lifecycle/retention, and exact authorized round-trip restoration. No production original is persisted before encryption; privacy-safe audit and unconditional tenant isolation are prerequisites for brokered reveal.

Primary issues: #14-#18, with the audit prerequisite in #20.

### 3. Model gateway proof

Goal: OpenAI-compatible local gateway using the same core contracts, authenticated caller/destination binding, streaming policy, inbound/outbound enforcement, destination profiles, and audit evidence. #21 first proves conservative inbound inspection without uncloak; #22 adds authorized response restoration. Unsupported protected formats and hosted-tool bypasses are denied or explicitly outside supported coverage, never silently forwarded.

Primary issues: #20-#22; full bidirectional restoration is the collective exit, not #21 alone.

### 4. Agent surfaces

Goal: MCP request/result protection, file and shell boundaries, CLI harness launcher, then additional provider/harness adapters against shared conformance tests.

Primary issues: #23-#26.

### 5. Enterprise hardening

Goal: workload identity, KMS/HSM operations, full multi-tenant hardening, tamper-evident audit, behavior signals, backup/recovery, network bypass resistance, policy exceptions, and incident response. Baseline tenant isolation, authentication and audit required by earlier slices are not deferred to this milestone; complete managed-egress claims wait for #32.

Primary issues: #27-#33.

### 6. Semantic long tail

Goal: extend earlier candidate sources and shadow judgment into evaluated contextual re-identification risk, deeper business/engineering classification, multimodal/nested content, and broader enterprise DLP/industrial information protection. Earlier #10-#12 work does not by itself authorize production re-identification claims.

Primary issues: #12 and #34, building on #10-#11.

### 7. Deployment profiles

Goal: package and verify local, customer VPC, on-premises and air-gapped capabilities against the boundaries proved above.

Primary issue: #35.

## Sequencing rule

A later integration does not bypass an unproved earlier boundary. For example, do not add five harness adapters before one gateway has proved normalization, classification, policy, egress sentinel, and end-to-end utility against the shared eval suite.

Foundation issues #1–#5 and preparatory comparative-campaign #38 research may proceed in parallel. Resolve #65's taxonomy and #66's multidimensional classification/task-fidelity design sufficiently to define #39's independent planted-oracle schema and fidelity vocabulary **before** freezing v0. A prospectively locked, independently approved scope/oracle/policy/task/scoring protocol under restricted blind custody and a minimal executable #5 evaluation seam must precede any fresh eligible reference candidate and scored #40–#45/#47 comparison; the already exposed DEV reference cannot retroactively meet the pre-tuning lock. A disposable reference baseline is not an enforcement runtime or a reason to complete bespoke #6–#26 first.

Obtain the reviewed per-subsystem #48 reuse/wrap/buy/build/defer decision before committing to broad bespoke detector, proxy, vault, or adapter implementation. Unavailable critical evidence yields an explicitly unverified/deferred role, not an inferred security pass.

[#67](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/67) (human annotation/active-learning Gym) stays a **non-blocking** upstream data source for continuous improvement ([#27](https://github.com/Marcus-Levin/hylja/issues/27)), not a prerequisite for the first enforcement proof, the #38 campaign or a #39 v0 freeze. A PROPOSED design and versioned export format are in [specs/gym-annotation-design.md](specs/gym-annotation-design.md) with a synthetic [example artifact](examples/gym-annotation-export-draft-0.1.json) and an explicitly evaluation-only, unwired schema checker in [`evaluations/gym-annotation-schema.mjs`](../evaluations/gym-annotation-schema.mjs). It changes no v1 classifier or policy, accepts nothing in decision 010, appoints no reviewer, and carries no protected value: artifacts hold no value, digest or note, admit only development-partition scope, and feed evaluation through the existing review path. Local evidence (Node 22.22.3, npm 10.9.8) after locked `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-fund`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:coverage` and `git diff --check`: 411/411 tests pass under the runner, including 20 behavior groups for the checker (malformed, hostile, oversized, scope-conflicting, blind-partition and authority-claiming artifacts, span/byte-mapping rules, a leaf sweep proving no free-form string carrier, imaginary or rolled-over timestamps, order-independent disagreement, and deterministic queue ordering), and the checker reports 98.65% line / 88.07% branch coverage under the Node coverage report. `check:fixtures` validates the example's tracked path; its value-free property is enforced structurally by the format (no value, note, digest or free-text field, bounded pointer segments, fixed enums, real-instant timestamps), not by that guard, which inspects only the repository's own synthetic corpus paths. This is contract behavior of a development aid, not a trained system, tenant isolation, an importer, a reviewed annotation, an approved promotion or an independent oracle; a Gym artifact is authored after observing candidate output and therefore can never supply #39's independent planted oracle. Two earlier independent reviews of this design found concrete defects - an unvalidated value-carrying enum leaf, an unadjudicated conflict derived as agreement, an eligibility gate that ignored deletion and expiry, and then a retention gate that answered "not expired" for an unreadable or imaginary date - all fixed here and covered by the groups above. Those reviews apply to the earlier heads, and independent re-review of this head is still required. One full run on this head, taken while other work was loading the host, took 110 s and reported five failures; the failing group names were not captured, so **no cause is attributed and none is excluded** - that run is recorded as unattributed, and three later full runs plus the coverage run at the same head were green at 26-28 s. The cause of an unattributed failure is unverified, not resolved. What is verifiable about load sensitivity in this repository: several inherited suites assert wall-clock budgets, including three 5-second ones in [`test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs`](../test/egress-sentinel.test.mjs) and a 30-second nested-run budget in [`test/toolchain.test.mjs`](../test/toolchain.test.mjs), and this plan's #7 evidence paragraph in Current state already records an earlier intermittent full-run failure in the egress-sentinel suite. None of those thresholds, and no fixture, was changed by this work or to accommodate any run.

This evidence gate does not weaken the accepted contracts, Policy Engine, broker, or final egress boundary. Changes to implementation scope require explicit review before adoption; changes to accepted architectural direction also require a new decision record.

Each implementation issue states dependencies and acceptance criteria. The shared security/evaluation requirements in roadmap issue #36 apply to all implementation work.
